# Ta’aleem Books: review sheet

Made by `node scripts/books/lint.mjs` from `src/lib/books/`. Every book, chapter by chapter, with each hadith, ayah and du'a,
for an Islamic Studies teacher to check before class. Items marked **check** need a teacher's eye in particular.

## My First Arabic Letters 1 (Level 1, ages 4–6)

Meet all 28 Arabic letters, one picture word for each, and trace them with crayons.

### 1. Alif and Ba

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/001.webp`: This is your Arabic letter book. Let us meet the letters!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/002.webp`: This is Alif. Alif is tall and straight. Arnab is a rabbit. Colour the rabbit and the big Alif.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/003.webp`: Trace the dotted Alif from the top down. Now write your own Alif on the lines.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/004.webp`: This is Ba. Ba says b. Bayt is a house. Colour the house.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/005.webp`: Ba has one dot under it. Trace the Ba. Then put in the dot!

### 2. Ta and Tha

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/006.webp`: This is Ta. Ta has two dots on top. Tuffaha is an apple. Colour it red or green.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/007.webp`: Trace the Ta. Do not forget the two dots.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/008.webp`: This is Tha. Tha has three dots. Thawb is a dress. Colour the pretty dress.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/009.webp`: Trace the Tha. Count the dots: one, two, three.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/010.webp`: Every picture is on this page two times, so look carefully. Draw a line to join the two that match.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/011.webp`: Find two letters that are the same. Join them with a line.
7. Listen and find: ا ب ت ث
8. Match: أَرْنَب = ا · بَيْت = ب · تُفَّاحَة = ت · ثَوْب = ث

### 3. Jeem, Haa and Kha

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/012.webp`: This is Jeem. Jeem has a dot inside. Jamal is a camel. Colour the camel brown.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/013.webp`: Trace the Jeem. Put the dot in the middle.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/014.webp`: This is Haa. Say it from deep in your throat. Himar is a donkey.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/015.webp`: Haa looks like Jeem with no dot. Trace it carefully.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/016.webp`: This is Kha. Kha has a dot on top. Khubz is bread. Yum!
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/017.webp`: Trace the Kha. The dot goes on top this time.
7. Listen and find: ج ح خ ب ت
8. Quiz: Which letter has a dot inside? **ج** · Which letter has a dot on top? **خ**

### 4. Dal and Dhal

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/018.webp`: This is Dal. Dal says d. Deek is a rooster. Cock-a-doodle-doo!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/019.webp`: Trace the Dal. It is small and bent like a knee.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/020.webp`: Find the letters that are the same. Join each pair with a line.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/021.webp`: Each duck has a letter. Each egg has a letter too. Join every duck to the egg with its letter.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/022.webp`: This is Dhal. It is Dal with a dot. Dhayl is a tail.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/023.webp`: Trace the Dhal and give it a dot.
7. Listen and find: د ذ ج ح خ

### 5. Ra and Zay

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/024.webp`: This is Ra. Ra says r. Reesh is a feather. Colour the soft feather.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/025.webp`: Trace the Ra. It slides down below the line.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/026.webp`: This is Zay. Zay is Ra with a dot. Zahra is a flower.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/027.webp`: Trace the Zay. Then add its dot.
5. Listen and find: ر ز د ذ
6. Match: دِيك = rooster · ذَيْل = tail · رِيش = feather · زَهْرَة = flower

### 6. Seen and Sheen

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/028.webp`: This is Seen. Seen has three little teeth. Sayyara is a car. Beep beep!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/029.webp`: Trace the Seen. Go up and down for each tooth.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/030.webp`: These are things in the classroom. Tap each one to hear it. Then colour them.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/031.webp`: Look at the first letter in the row. Circle it every time you see it again.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/032.webp`: This is Sheen. Sheen is Seen with three dots. Shajara is a tree.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/033.webp`: Trace the Sheen. Put three dots on top.
7. Listen and find: س ش ر ز ب
8. Quiz: Which one is a book? **كِتَاب** · Which one is a pen? **قَلَم**

### 7. Saad and Daad

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/034.webp`: This is Saad. It is a strong, heavy s. Sundooq is a box.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/035.webp`: Trace the Saad. Make a round loop, then a big bowl.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/036.webp`: This is Daad. It is Saad with a dot. Difda is a frog. Ribbit!
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/037.webp`: Trace the Daad. Do not forget its dot.
5. Listen and find: ص ض س ش

### 8. Taa and Dhaa

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/038.webp`: This is Taa. It is a strong, heavy t. Tayr is a bird.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/039.webp`: Trace the Taa. Draw the tall line last.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/040.webp`: Look at each picture. Join it to the letter its name starts with.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/041.webp`: Each boy is fishing for his letter. Join each boy to the fish with the same letter.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/042.webp`: This is Dhaa. It is Taa with a dot. Dharf is an envelope.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/043.webp`: Trace the Dhaa. Put the dot by the tall line.
7. Listen and find: ط ظ ص ض ت
8. Match: سَيَّارَة = س · رِيش = ر · صُنْدُوق = ص · طَيْر = ط

### 9. Ain and Ghain

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/044.webp`: This is Ain. Say it from the middle of your throat. Ayn is an eye.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/045.webp`: Trace the Ain. Start with the little curve at the top.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/046.webp`: This is Ghain. It is Ain with a dot. Ghazal is a gazelle. It runs fast!
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/047.webp`: Trace the Ghain. Add the dot on top.
5. Listen and find: ع غ ط ظ

### 10. Fa and Qaf

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/048.webp`: This is Fa. Fa has one dot. Fam is a mouth. Smile!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/049.webp`: Trace the Fa. One dot goes on its head.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/050.webp`: Look at each picture. Circle the letter its name starts with.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/051.webp`: These are fruits. Tap each fruit to hear its name. Have you tasted every one of these fruits at home?
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/052.webp`: This is Qaf. Qaf has two dots. Qalam is a pen.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/053.webp`: Trace the Qaf. Its tail goes down low.
7. Listen and find: ف ق ع غ
8. Match: مَوْزَة = banana · عِنَب = grapes · بُرْتُقَالَة = orange · أَنَانَاس = pineapple

### 11. Kaf, Lam and Meem

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/054.webp`: This is Kaf. Kaf says k. Kursi is a chair. A chair has four legs and a back to lean on.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/055.webp`: Trace the Kaf. Draw the little hook inside it.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/056.webp`: This is Lam. Lam says l. Lisan is a tongue. Can you show your tongue?
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/057.webp`: Trace the Lam. It stands tall and then curves round at the bottom.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/058.webp`: This is Meem. Meem says m. Mawz is a banana. Colour it yellow.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/059.webp`: Trace the Meem. Make a small round head first.
7. Listen and find: ك ل م ف ق

### 12. Noon and Waw

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/060.webp`: This is Noon. Noon says n. Najm is a star. Twinkle twinkle!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/061.webp`: Trace the Noon. Its dot sits in the middle.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/062.webp`: These are toys. Tap each toy to hear its name. Which toy do you like best?
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/063.webp`: Join each picture to the letter its name starts with.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/064.webp`: This is Waw. Waw says w. Wajh is a face. Draw a big smile!
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/065.webp`: Trace the Waw. Round head, then a long tail.
7. Listen and find: ن و ك ل م
8. Match: كُرَة = ball · قِطَار = train · دَرَّاجَة = bicycle · طَائِرَة = aeroplane

### 13. Ha, Lam alif, Hamza and Ya

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/066.webp`: This is Ha. It is a soft h, like a breath. Hirra is a cat. Meow!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/067.webp`: Trace the Ha. It has two little windows.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/068.webp`: Lam and Alif join together like this. We say la. Trace it.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/069.webp`: This little letter is Hamza. Trace the Hamza.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/070.webp`: This is Ya. Ya has two dots under it. Yad is a hand. Wave hello!
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/071.webp`: Trace the Ya. Two dots go underneath.
7. Listen and find: ه ي ن و
8. Quiz: Which word is a cat? **هِرَّة** · Which letter has two dots under it? **ي**

### 14. Pictures and letters

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/072.webp`: Join each picture to the letter its name starts with.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/073.webp`: Look at each picture. Circle the first letter of its name.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/074.webp`: This is your body. Tap each part to hear its name. Then point to it on you!
4. Match: رَأْس = head · أُذُن = ear · أَنْف = nose · شَعْر = hair
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/075.webp`: Trace the dotted letters on the balloons. Then write each one in a box.
6. Listen and find: ض ل ح غ ظ م

### 15. Writing the letters

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/076.webp`: Read these letters with your teacher. Copy them on the lines.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/077.webp`: Each girl is fishing for her letter. Join each girl to the fish with the same letter.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/078.webp`: Read the next letters. Now copy them on the lines.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/079.webp`: Each star has a colour name. Tap to hear it, then colour the star that colour.
5. Match: أَحْمَر = red · أَزْرَق = blue · أَخْضَر = green · أَصْفَر = yellow
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/080.webp`: More letters to read and copy. Take your time.
7. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/081.webp`: Some letters are missing from these chains. Write the letters that come in between.
8. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/082.webp`: Read these letters. Copy each one on the lines.
9. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/083.webp`: Help each mother find her babies. The camel walks on Jeem. The bird hops on Taa.
10. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/084.webp`: Read and copy these letters too.
11. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/085.webp`: Some train carriages have no letter. Write the missing letters on the trains.
12. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/086.webp`: These are the last letters. Read them and copy them.
13. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/087.webp`: Join the letters in order, from Alif to Ya. What animal do you get?
14. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/088.webp`: Write all the letters in order. Start at Alif and finish at Ya.
15. Listen and find: ب ج ر ش ع ن

### 16. All my letters

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/089.webp`: Join each picture to the letter its name starts with.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/090.webp`: Here are more pictures. Join each one to its first letter.
3. Match: لِسَان = ل · مَوْز = م · عَيْن = ع · وَجْه = و · جَمَل = ج
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/091.webp`: Here are all your letters with their pictures. Read them with your teacher. Can you name every picture?
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/092.webp`: Practise any letters you like on these lines.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/093.webp`: Which letter is your favourite? Write it lots of times here.
7. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-1/094.webp`: One more page to practise. Try to write the letters of your name.
8. Listen and find: ا ب ت ث ج ح خ د ذ ر ز س ش ص ض ط ظ ع غ ف ق ك ل م ن ه و ي
9. Quiz: Which word is a camel? **جَمَل** · Which word is a star? **نَجْم** · Which letter is the first letter of all? **ا**

## My First Arabic Letters 2 (Level 1, ages 4–6)

The 28 letters again, now with three picture words each, the short vowels and the joined letter shapes.

### 1. Alif and Ba

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/001.webp`: This is your second Arabic letter book. Let us read and colour!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/002.webp`: This is Alif. Arnab is a rabbit. Udhun is an ear. Ibreeq is a teapot. Colour them all, then trace the Alifs at the bottom.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/003.webp`: Join the letters that are the same. Then join the letters in order, from Alif to Ya. What do you see?
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/004.webp`: This is Ba. Bayt is a house. Burtuqala is an orange. Bint is a girl.
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/005.webp`: Look at the first letter in each row. Circle the one just like it. Then find the first letter of each picture. Circle it!

### 2. Ta and Tha

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/006.webp`: This is Ta. Taj is a crown. Tuffaha is an apple. Timsah is a crocodile. Snap!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/007.webp`: Some letters lost their dots. Put the dots back! Then write the first letter of each picture in its circle.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/008.webp`: This is Tha. Thawb is a dress. Thu'ban is a snake. Thiyab are clothes.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/009.webp`: The little line on top is a fatha. It says a. Read every letter with a fatha: ba, ta, tha. Then join the letters that match.
5. Listen and find: ا ب ت ث
6. Match: تَاج = crown · تِمْسَاح = crocodile · ثُعْبَان = snake · بِنْت = girl

### 3. Jeem and Haa

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/010.webp`: This is Jeem. Jamal is a camel. Jundi is a soldier. Jidar is a wall.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/011.webp`: These are doing words. Every letter has a fatha. Read each one with your teacher, then write it on the line.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/012.webp`: This is Haa. Haqeeba is a bag. Hoot is a whale. Himar is a donkey.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/013.webp`: Tap the words to hear them. Then circle only the letters that have a fatha on top.
5. Listen and find: ج ح ت ث ب
6. Match: قَمَر = moon · سَمَكَة = fish · جَرَس = bell · شَمْس = sun

### 4. Kha and Dal

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/014.webp`: This is Kha. Kharoof is a sheep. Khubz is bread. Khiyar is a cucumber.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/015.webp`: The little line under a letter is a kasra. It says i. Read the letters: bi, ti, thi. Circle the ones with a kasra.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/016.webp`: This is Dal. Darraja is a bicycle. Dubb is a bear. Deek is a rooster.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/017.webp`: More doing words. Look for the kasra in the middle of each one. Read them, then write them.
5. Listen and find: خ د ج ح
6. Quiz: Which one is bread? **خُبْز** · Which one is a bear? **دُبّ**

### 5. Dhal and Ra

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/018.webp`: This is Dhal. Dhayl is a tail. Dhura is corn. Dhi'b is a wolf. Awoo!
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/019.webp`: Lots of words start with a kasra. Tap them and listen for the i. Then write a fatha or a kasra in each circle.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/020.webp`: This is Ra. Rajul is a man. Rukba is a knee. Reesh is feathers.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/021.webp`: The little curl on top is a dhamma. It says u. Read bu, tu, thu. Circle all the dhamma letters on the tree.
5. Listen and find: ذ ر د خ ز
6. Match: قِرْد = monkey · فِيل = elephant · حِصَان = horse · ذِئْب = wolf

### 6. Zay and Seen

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/022.webp`: This is Zay. Zarafa is a giraffe. Zuhoor are flowers. Zirr is a button.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/023.webp`: These words start with a dhamma. Read them with your teacher. Then write each word on its line.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/024.webp`: This is Seen. Samaka is a fish. Sullam is a ladder. Sikkeen is a knife.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/025.webp`: Every word here starts with a dhamma. Tap and listen. Then fill in the missing letters at the bottom.
5. Listen and find: ز س ر ذ
6. Quiz: Which one is a giraffe? **زَرَافَة** · Which one is a ladder? **سُلَّم**

### 7. Sheen and Saad

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/026.webp`: This is Sheen. Shams is the sun. Shubbak is a window. Shira' is a sail on a boat.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/027.webp`: Put each letter in the right box: fatha, kasra or dhamma. Then join the letters that have the same vowel.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/028.webp`: This is Saad. Saboon is soap. Sundooq is a box. Seeniyya is a tray.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/029.webp`: Letters change shape inside a word. Read each letter with your teacher at the start, middle and end.
5. Listen and find: ش ص س ز

### 8. Daad and Taa

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/030.webp`: This is Daad. Dabu' is a hyena. Duroos are back teeth. Difda' is a frog.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/031.webp`: Here is how each letter looks at the start of a word. Circle the shape that matches the letter in each row.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/032.webp`: This is Taa. Ta'ira is an aeroplane. Tuyoor are birds. Tifl is a baby.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/033.webp`: Join each joined shape to its letter. Then write each letter the way it looks alone.
5. Listen and find: ض ط ص ش ت
6. Match: ضِفْدَع = ض · طَائِرَة = ط · صَابُون = ص · شَمْس = ش

### 9. Dhaa and Ain

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/034.webp`: This is Dhaa. Dharf is an envelope. Dhufr is a fingernail. Dhill is the shade of a tree.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/035.webp`: Look at each picture. Circle its name in the row. Then write each letter the way it starts a word.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/036.webp`: This is Ain. Ayn is an eye. Usfoor is a sparrow. Inab is grapes.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/037.webp`: Each letter has a few shapes. Join all the shapes of the same letter.
5. Listen and find: ظ ع ط ض
6. Quiz: Which one is an eye? **عَيْن** · Which one is a sparrow? **عُصْفُور**

### 10. Ghain and Fa

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/038.webp`: This is Ghain. Ghazal is a gazelle. Ghurab is a crow. Ghita' is a lid.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/039.webp`: Join each word to its picture. Then join two letters to make a little word. Qaf and Dal make qad.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/040.webp`: This is Fa. Fa'r is a rat. Furshah is a brush. Feel is an elephant.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/041.webp`: Which word goes with the picture? Circle it. Then split each little word into its two letters.
5. Listen and find: غ ف ع ظ ق
6. Match: غَزَال = gazelle · غُرَاب = crow · فَأْر = rat · فِيل = elephant

### 11. Qaf and Kaf

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/042.webp`: This is Qaf. Qalam is a pen. Qufl is a lock. Qird is a monkey.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/043.webp`: Circle the first letter of each picture. Then find the missing letter in each little word.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/044.webp`: This is Kaf. Al-Ka'ba is the Kaaba in Makkah. Kura is a ball. Kitab is a book.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/045.webp`: Write the two letters of each little word in the boxes. Then use the balloons to spell three pictures.
5. Listen and find: ق ك ف غ

### 12. Lam and Meem

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/046.webp`: This is Lam. Lahm is meat. Lu'ab are toys. Lisan is a tongue.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/047.webp`: Join each set of letters to the word they make. Then circle the letters you can see inside each big word.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/048.webp`: This is Meem. Mawz is a banana. Mudarris is a teacher. Miftah is a key.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/049.webp`: Join the letters to make one word. Then write the missing letter under each picture.
5. Listen and find: ل م ك ق ن
6. Match: لَحْم = ل · مَوْز = م · كِتَاب = ك · قَلَم = ق

### 13. The last four letters

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/050.webp`: This is Noon. Nakhla is a palm tree. Sweet dates grow at the top of the palm tree. Nujoom are stars. Nisf is a half.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/051.webp`: This is Waw. Wajh is a face. Wudoo' is the washing we do before we pray. Wisada is a pillow.
3. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/052.webp`: This is Ha. Hatif is a telephone. Hudhud is a hoopoe bird. The hoopoe has a crown of feathers on its head. Hirra is a cat.
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/053.webp`: This is Ya, the last letter. Yad is a hand. Yusroo' is a caterpillar. Wiggle wiggle!
5. Listen and find: ن و ه ي م
6. Quiz: Which one is a cat? **هِرَّة** · Which one is a pillow? **وِسَادَة** · Which one is a hand? **يَد**

### 14. All my letters

1. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/054.webp`: Join each picture to its word.
2. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/055.webp`: Join the colour words that are the same. Then colour each flag with its colour.
3. Match: أَحْمَر = red · أَخْضَر = green · أَصْفَر = yellow · أَزْرَق = blue
4. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/056.webp`: Here are the letters from Alif to Saad with their pictures. Read them with your teacher. How many pictures can you name?
5. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/057.webp`: Here are the rest of the letters, from Daad to Ya. Point to each picture and say its name.
6. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/058.webp`: Read every letter three ways. Use a fatha, then a kasra, then a dhamma. Ba, bi, bu!
7. Listen and find: ا ب ت ث ج ح خ د ذ ر ز س ش ص ض ط ظ ع غ ف ق ك ل م ن ه و ي
8. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/059.webp`: Practise any letters you like on these lines.
9. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/060.webp`: Write some words you know. Your teacher can help.
10. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/061.webp`: Which letter is the hardest? Practise it here.
11. Workbook page `books/first-arabic-2/062.webp`: Last page! Write the letters of your name. Well done, you finished the book!

## My Du'a Activity Book (Level 1, ages 4–6)

The little du'as of the day: hear them, say them, trace and colour them, then play.

### 1. My du'a book

1. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/001.webp`: This is your du'a book. Can you write your name on it?
2. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/002.webp`: Inside are the du'as we say every day. Let's start!

### 2. Bismillah

1. *Before we start anything.* Before we start anything, we say Bismillah. It means "In the name of Allah." We say it before we eat. We say it before we read and play too.
2. Ayah 1:1: "In the name of Allah."
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/003.webp`: Trace Bismillah with your crayon. Go slowly along the lines.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/004.webp`: She says Bismillah before she draws. Colour her picture!
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/005.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
6. Quiz: What do we say before we start something? **Bismillah** · Bismillah means... **In the name of Allah**

### 3. Before eating

1. Du'a (Before we eat): بِسْمِ اللَّهِ / Bismillah / "In the name of Allah." (Bukhari 5376, Muslim 2022)
2. Du'a (If we forget, we say it when we remember): بِسْمِ اللَّهِ أَوَّلَهُ وَآخِرَهُ / Bismillahi awwalahu wa akhirahu / "In the name of Allah, at the start and at the end." (Abu Dawud 3767, Tirmidhi 1858)
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/007.webp`: He is sitting down to eat. What does he say first? Colour his kitchen.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/006.webp`: Count each kind of fruit. Write how many on the lines.
5. Quiz: What do we say before we eat? **Bismillah** · Which hand do we eat with? **The right hand**

### 4. Before drinking

- **check:** Bukhari 5376 is the hadith about saying Bismillah before eating. The book uses the same Bismillah before drinking. Please confirm this source is fine for drinking.
1. Du'a (Before we drink): بِسْمِ اللَّهِ / Bismillah / "I drink in the name of Allah." (Bukhari 5376)
2. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/009.webp`: We say Bismillah before we drink too. Trace it!
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/010.webp`: He is having a drink of water. Colour the jug and the cup.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/011.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
5. Quiz: You want a drink of milk. What do you say first? **Bismillah** · Is it better to sit down when we drink? **Yes, we sit down**

### 5. When someone sneezes

1. Du'a (When someone sneezes and says Alhamdulillah): يَرْحَمُكَ اللَّهُ / Yarhamukallah / "May Allah have mercy on you!" (Bukhari 6224)
2. *Atchoo!.* When we sneeze, we say Alhamdulillah. Then our friend says Yarhamukallah. And we answer them like this: يَهْدِيكُمُ اللَّهُ وَيُصْلِحُ بَالَكُمْ Yahdikumullahu wa yuslihu balakum. It means "May Allah guide you and make things good for you." Bukhari 6224
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/012.webp`: Trace Yarhamukallah with your crayon.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/013.webp`: Atchoo! His friend sneezed. What does he say? Colour them both.
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/014.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
6. Match: I sneeze = Alhamdulillah · My friend hears me = Yarhamukallah

### 6. Saying goodbye

1. Du'a (When we leave our friends): السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُمْ / As-salamu alaykum / "Peace be on you." (Abu Dawud 5208, Tirmidhi 2706)
- **check:** The book teaches "Ma'a as-salamah", a kind Arabic goodbye but not a du'a from a hadith. The chapter opens with the Sunnah salam on leaving (Abu Dawud 5208, Tirmidhi 2706) and keeps Ma'a as-salamah as the book's word.
2. *Ma'a as-salamah.* When we part, we can also say Ma'a as-salamah. مَعَ السَّلَامَةِ It means "Go safely!"
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/015.webp`: Trace Ma'a as-salamah. Wave goodbye when you finish!
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/016.webp`: He is giving his dad a big hug goodbye. Colour them in.
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/017.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
6. Quiz: Your friend is going home. What can you say? **Ma'a as-salamah** · Ma'a as-salamah means... **Go safely**

### 7. Thank you

- **check:** The book teaches "Shukran" (thank you). The chapter opens with the Sunnah words Jazakallahu khayran (Tirmidhi 2035) and keeps Shukran on the next page.
1. Du'a (When someone does something kind for us): جَزَاكَ اللَّهُ خَيْرًا / Jazakallahu khayran / "May Allah give you good for it." (Tirmidhi 2035)
2. *Shukran.* In Arabic, thank you is Shukran. شُكْرًا Did someone help you? Say Shukran, or Jazakallahu khayran!
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/019.webp`: Trace Shukran with your crayon.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/020.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/021.webp`: She gives her friend a present. What does her friend say? Colour them.
6. Quiz: Mum gives you a drink. What do you say? **Jazakallahu khayran** · Shukran means... **Thank you**

### 8. Before sleeping

1. Du'a (When we lie down to sleep): بِاسْمِكَ اللَّهُمَّ أَمُوتُ وَأَحْيَا / Bismika Allahumma amutu wa ahya / "In Your name, O Allah, I die and I live." (Bukhari 6324)
2. *Sleeping and waking.* The du'a says "I die and I live." Sleep is a little like dying. In the morning, Allah gives us our life back. Then we wake up!
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/022.webp`: Trace the sleeping du'a with your crayon.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/023.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/024.webp`: Shh, she is fast asleep. Colour her cosy bedroom.
6. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/040.webp`: Here is the sleeping du'a again. Can you say it with me?
7. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/041.webp`: It is night time. The moon is out! Colour his room.
8. Quiz: It is bedtime. Which du'a do we say? **Bismika Allahumma amutu wa ahya** · When do we say Bismika Allahumma? **Before sleeping**

### 9. Before a lesson

1. *Before we learn.* Before a lesson, we say the first two ayat of Al-Fatihah. Listen, then say them with the reciter.
2. Ayah 1:1: "In the name of Allah, the Most Kind, the Most Merciful."
3. Ayah 1:2: "All praise is for Allah, the Lord of all the worlds."
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/025.webp`: Trace the words with your crayon. Start on the right side!
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/026.webp`: They are ready for their lesson. Colour their books and toys.
6. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/027.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
7. Quiz: Your lesson is starting. What do you say? **Bismillahir Rahmanir Rahim** · Who is the Lord of all the worlds? **Allah**

### 10. Leaving the house

1. Du'a (When we go out of the house): بِسْمِ اللَّهِ، تَوَكَّلْتُ عَلَى اللَّهِ، وَلَا حَوْلَ وَلَا قُوَّةَ إِلَّا بِاللَّهِ / Bismillah, tawakkaltu 'alallah, wa la hawla wa la quwwata illa billah / "In the name of Allah. I trust in Allah. Only Allah can help me and make me strong." (Abu Dawud 5095, Tirmidhi 3426)
2. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/028.webp`: This du'a is a long one! Trace it slowly.
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/029.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/030.webp`: She is opening the door to go out. Colour her picture.
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/008.webp`: He says Bismillah and salam as he goes. Colour him and his mum.
6. Quiz: We are going to the park. What do we say at the door? **Bismillah, tawakkaltu 'alallah** · Who keeps us safe when we go out? **Allah**

### 11. Going into the toilet

1. Du'a (Before we go into the toilet): اللَّهُمَّ إِنِّي أَعُوذُ بِكَ مِنَ الْخُبُثِ وَالْخَبَائِثِ / Allahumma inni a'udhu bika minal khubuthi wal khaba'ith / "O Allah, I ask You to keep me safe from the bad ones." (Bukhari 142, Muslim 375)
- **check:** Going in with the left foot is what the scholars teach; no hadith is quoted for it here.
2. *Left foot first.* We say the du'a outside the door. Then we step in with our left foot.
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/034.webp`: Trace the du'a with your crayon.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/035.webp`: He is going into the bathroom. Colour the tiles and the towel.
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/036.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
6. Quiz: When do we say Allahumma inni a'udhu bika? **Before going into the toilet** · Which foot goes in first? **The left foot**

### 12. Coming out of the toilet

1. Du'a (When we come out of the toilet): غُفْرَانَكَ / Ghufranak / "I ask for Your forgiveness." (Abu Dawud 30, Tirmidhi 7)
2. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/037.webp`: Ghufranak is a short du'a. Trace it!
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/038.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/039.webp`: All done! Now he washes his hands. Colour the bathroom.
- **check:** The words printed under this picture ("even if you are taking ablution from a stream") come from a narration in Ibn Majah 425 that many scholars grade weak. The reader does not read them out. A teacher may wish to reprint the page without them.
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/018.webp`: Use just a little water when you wash. Colour the picture.
6. Match: Allahumma inni a'udhu bika = Going in · Ghufranak = Coming out

### 13. A du'a for my parents

1. *Mum and Dad.* Mum and Dad looked after us when we were tiny. Allah teaches us a du'a for them in the Qur'an. Listen, then say it with the reciter.
2. Ayah 17:24: "My Lord, have mercy on them, as they looked after me when I was small."
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/031.webp`: Trace the du'a for Mum and Dad.
4. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/032.webp`: Join the shapes from the boy to his grandparents. Colour the hearts!
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/033.webp`: Look at the letters in the pink boxes. Copy each one into the boxes below.
6. Quiz: Who is this du'a for? **Mum and Dad** · What do we ask Allah to give them? **Mercy**

### 14. After waking up

1. Du'a (When we wake up): الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ الَّذِي أَحْيَانَا بَعْدَ مَا أَمَاتَنَا وَإِلَيْهِ النُّشُورُ / Alhamdu lillahil-ladhi ahyana ba'da ma amatana wa ilayhin-nushur / "All praise is for Allah, who gave us life after death. And to Him we all return." (Bukhari 6324)
- **check:** The last word on this page is printed "النشوء"; the sound wording is "النُّشُورُ" (Bukhari 6324). The du'a card uses the correct wording. The page may need fixing in Canva.
2. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/043.webp`: Good morning! Here is the waking up du'a.
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/044.webp`: The sun is up and so is he! Colour his room.
4. Quiz: You just woke up. What do you say? **Alhamdu lillahil-ladhi ahyana** · Who wakes us up every morning? **Allah**

### 15. Let's play

1. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/042.webp`: Draw a line from each du'a to when we say it.
2. Match: Bismika Allahumma amutu wa ahya = Before sleeping · Alhamdulillah = After I sneeze · Bismillah = Before eating
3. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/045.webp`: Help her get to the masjid. Draw her path with your crayon.
4. Match: Ghufranak = Leaving the toilet · Yarhamukallah = When a friend sneezes · Jazakallahu khayran = When someone helps me · Rabbirhamhuma = For Mum and Dad
5. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/046.webp`: The family is eating together. What did they say first?
6. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/047.webp`: Which du'a do we say before eating? Circle it!
7. Quiz: Which du'a do we say before eating? **Bismillah** · Which du'a do we say when we wake up? **Alhamdu lillahil-ladhi ahyana** · What do we say when we leave the toilet? **Ghufranak**
8. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/048.webp`: He raises his hands before his meal. Colour his plate.
9. Workbook page `books/dua-activity/049.webp`: Draw your favourite food on the plate. Then thank Allah for it!

## Alif Ba: I'm Learning Qur'an (Level 2, ages 6–8)

Learn to read the Qur'an step by step: the letters and their shapes, the vowel marks, madd, sukun, shadda and tanween, with workbook pages to write on.

### 1. Welcome

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/001.webp`: This is your Alif Ba book. With it you will learn to read the Qur'an, one letter at a time.
- **check:** Page 2 is a calligraphy panel. Please confirm what it says, so the spoken line can name it.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/002.webp`: Let's begin in the name of Allah. Say Bismillah!
3. *Before we start.* Assalamu alaikum! This book teaches you to read Arabic. Arabic is the language of the Qur'an. First you will meet the 28 letters. You will hear each one and trace it with your crayon. Then you will see how the letters join to make words. After that come the little marks. They tell each letter how to sound. Sit with your child for ten minutes at a time. Let them tap a letter to hear it, then say it back. Arabic is read from right to left, so point with your finger as you go. Ready? Turn the page and let's begin.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/003.webp`: Here are the parts of the book. Each part has pages to read and pages to write on.

### 2. Alif to Tha

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/004.webp`: Here are all the Arabic letters. The red ones are the four we learn first, and you can tap each one to hear it.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/005.webp`: Alif is the first letter. It is thin and long, like a pen. Trace the big Alifs, then write Alif in the little boxes.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/006.webp`: Some Alifs are hiding in this picture. Can you find them? Circle each one.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/007.webp`: Ba is wide, and Alif comes before it. Ba has one dot under its body. Trace it and say its name.
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/008.webp`: Look at all these letters. Circle every Ba, then read it out loud.
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/009.webp`: Ta looks like Ba and comes right after it. Ta has two dots on top. Colour it in with your favourite colour.
7. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/010.webp`: Find every Ta on this page. Colour it, then read it out loud.
8. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/011.webp`: Tha comes after Ba and Ta. It has three dots on top. Write a Tha in each little circle and read them all.
9. Listen and find: ا ب ت ث
10. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/012.webp`: Zaid wants to go to the mosque, so follow the path of Tha letters to take him there.
11. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/013.webp`: Trace each wavy line from the top down to the bottom. Read the letter at each end.
12. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/014.webp`: Colour the letters on the prayer mat. The colour dots at the bottom show which colour goes with which letter.
13. Quiz: I am the first letter. I am thin and long, like a pen. Who am I? **ا** · I am wide. I have one dot under me. Who am I? **ب** · I look like Ba, but my two dots are on top. Who am I? **ت** · I have three dots on top. Who am I? **ث**

### 3. Jim to Lam

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/015.webp`: These three letters have the same shape, and only the dot moves from place to place. Tap and listen.
2. Listen and find: ج ح خ ب ت
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/016.webp`: Dal and Dhal are a pair. So are Ra and Zay. The dot is the only difference.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/017.webp`: Sin has three little teeth. Shin has the same teeth with three dots on top. Ṣad and Ḍad have a round loop.
5. Listen and find: د ذ ر ز س ش ص ض
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/018.webp`: Ṭa and Ẓa stand up tall. ʿAyn and Ghayn open their mouths at the top.
7. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/019.webp`: Fa has one dot on top and Qaf has two, so look closely at the dots. Then come Kaf and Lam.
8. Listen and find: ط ظ ع غ ف ق ك ل
9. Match: ج = Jim · ذ = Dhal · ش = Shin · ع = ʿAyn · ق = Qaf · ك = Kaf

### 4. Mim to Ya

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/020.webp`: Here are the last five letters. Ya comes at the very end, so it is the last letter you will learn.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/022.webp`: Ha comes from deep in the throat. Trace the big Ha, colour it and say it out loud.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/023.webp`: Find the letters in the cloud and on the mosque. Write each one in the boxes below.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/024.webp`: I am small and round, and I have no dot. Can you guess who I am? I am Waw! Colour me in.
- **check:** The riddle on page 25 is printed in Turkish. The spoken line gives it in English.
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/025.webp`: Ya is the last letter of the alphabet. It curls like a little stream and has two dots under it.
6. Listen and find: م ن ه و ي
7. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/026.webp`: Write these letters in the shapes. Say each one as you write it.
8. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/027.webp`: Draw a line between the letters that are the same. Then read them out loud.
9. Match: م = Mim · ن = Nun · ه = Ha · و = Waw · ي = Ya

### 5. Where letters come from

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/021.webp`: Every letter comes from a place in your mouth. Some come from the throat. Most come from the tongue, and four come from the lips.
2. *Try it yourself.* Put your hand on your throat and say Ḥa. Can you feel it? Now say Ba. Your lips press together. Say Ta. The tip of your tongue touches the back of your top teeth. [table]
3. Sort into Throat / Tongue / Lips: ع (Throat) · ح (Throat) · غ (Throat) · خ (Throat) · ت (Tongue) · ك (Tongue) · ل (Tongue) · س (Tongue) · ب (Lips) · م (Lips) · ف (Lips) · و (Lips)

### 6. Letters in words

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/028.webp`: Here are all the letters together. Read them from the right, row by row.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/029.webp`: The letters are in a new order here. Start at the right and read each one.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/030.webp`: Copy each letter into the empty boxes.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/031.webp`: Letters change shape when they join. Look at Ba at the start of a word, in the middle or at the end.
5. Match: ب = بـ · ج = جـ · س = سـ · ع = عـ · ف = فـ · م = مـ
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/032.webp`: Write each letter as it looks at the start, in the middle or at the end.
7. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/033.webp`: Keep going with the rest of the letters. Say each shape out loud.
8. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/034.webp`: Look for the stars. Those six letters never join to the letter after them.
9. Quiz: Which letter never joins to the letter after it? **د** · Which letter never joins to the letter after it? **ر** · Which letter never joins to the letter after it? **و** · Which letter never joins to the letter after it? **ا**
10. Match: ب = ـب · ن = ـن · ه = ـه · ي = ـي · ك = ـك · ع = ـع
11. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/035.webp`: Now the joined letters have little marks on them. Read them slowly from the right.
12. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/036.webp`: Copy these letters into the space below. Take your time.

### 7. Fat-ha

1. *The little marks.* Arabic letters wear little marks. The marks are called harakat, and they tell us how to say each letter. There are three short vowels. [table] Let's start with fat-ha.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/037.webp`: Fat-ha is a small line on top of a letter. It makes an a sound, like in ba. Read each box from the right, and say the a sound clearly every time.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/038.webp`: Every letter here has a fat-ha. Read the words slowly, one sound at a time.
4. Match: بَ = ba · تَ = ta · دَ = da · رَ = ra · سَ = sa · مَ = ma
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/039.webp`: Keep reading! Can you read a whole row without stopping?
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/040.webp`: Copy each word into the box below it.
7. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/041.webp`: This is your homework. Write the words in the boxes, then read them to someone at home.

### 8. Kasra

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/042.webp`: Kasra is a small line under a letter. It makes an i sound, like in bi, so your mouth smiles a little when you say it. The book calls it kes-ra.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/043.webp`: Look for the line under the letter. That is where you say i.
3. Quiz: How do you read بِ? **bi** · How do you read مَ? **ma** · How do you read سِ? **si** · Where does the kasra go? **Under the letter**
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/044.webp`: Some letters have fat-ha and some have kasra, so look closely at each mark before you read.
5. Match: بِ = bi · مِ = mi · لِ = li · نِ = ni · فِ = fi · كِ = ki
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/045.webp`: Copy each word into the box below it.
7. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/046.webp`: Your homework: write the kasra words in the boxes, then read them out loud.

### 9. Damma

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/047.webp`: Damma looks like a tiny Waw on top of a letter. It makes a u sound, like in bu. The book calls it dom-ma.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/048.webp`: Round your lips for the u sound, then read every word on the page from the right.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/049.webp`: This page mixes all three vowels, so look at each mark before you read the word.
4. Sort into Fat-ha (a) / Kasra (i) / Damma (u): بَ (Fat-ha (a)) · تِ (Kasra (i)) · جُ (Damma (u)) · دَ (Fat-ha (a)) · رِ (Kasra (i)) · سُ (Damma (u)) · كَ (Fat-ha (a)) · لِ (Kasra (i)) · مُ (Damma (u)) · نَ (Fat-ha (a)) · هِ (Kasra (i)) · وُ (Damma (u))
5. Quiz: Which mark makes the u sound? **Damma** · Which mark sits under the letter? **Kasra** · How do you read تُ? **tu** · How do you read رَ? **ra** · Which mark makes the a sound? **Fat-ha**
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/050.webp`: Here the letters are joined and each one has a vowel. Read from the right, one sound at a time.
7. Match: جَ = ja · جِ = ji · جُ = ju · لَ = la · لِ = li · لُ = lu

### 10. The long Alif

1. *Long sounds.* Sometimes a letter is followed by Alif, Waw or Ya with no mark on it. That plain letter makes the sound longer. It is called a madd letter. [table] Hold a long sound for two counts.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/051.webp`: Alif with no mark is a madd letter. A fat-ha before it makes a long aa, like in maa.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/052.webp`: Read the word on each mushroom, and make the aa sound nice and long, like a song!
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/053.webp`: Copy the words into the boxes.
5. Quiz: Which one has a long aa sound? **بَا** · Which one has a long aa sound? **مَا** · Which mark comes before a madd Alif? **Fat-ha**
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/054.webp`: Here are more words with the long Alif, so read them slowly and hold each aa.
7. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/055.webp`: Read every word on this page. Stretch each aa for two counts.

### 11. The long Ya and Waw

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/056.webp`: Ya with no mark is a madd letter too. A kasra before it makes a long ii, like in fii.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/057.webp`: Copy the words in the big box.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/058.webp`: Copy each word into the empty box under it.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/059.webp`: More words with the long ii. Read them once, then read them again a bit faster.
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/060.webp`: Waw with no mark is a madd letter. A damma before it makes a long uu, like in nuur.
6. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/061.webp`: Keep reading the uu words. Round your lips and hold the sound.
7. Sort into aa (Alif) / ii (Ya) / uu (Waw): مَا (aa (Alif)) · قَالَ (aa (Alif)) · كِتَابُ (aa (Alif)) · فِى (ii (Ya)) · قِيلَ (ii (Ya)) · رَحِيمُ (ii (Ya)) · يَقُولُ (uu (Waw)) · رَسُولُ (uu (Waw)) · غَفُورُ (uu (Waw))
8. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/062.webp`: This page mixes all the madd letters, so give each word its own long sound.
9. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/063.webp`: Read each row from the right. Which madd letter do you see?
10. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/064.webp`: One more page! Read it slowly, then show your teacher how well you can read the long sounds.
11. Match: بَا = baa · بِي = bii · بُو = buu · تَا = taa · تِي = tii · تُو = tuu

### 12. Sukun

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/065.webp`: Sukun is a small circle on top of a letter. That letter has no vowel of its own. Join it to the letter before it.
2. Quiz: What does a sukun tell you? **The letter has no vowel** · How do you read مِنْ? **min** · How do you read لَمْ? **lam**
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/066.webp`: Read each word. When you reach a sukun, stop on that letter. Don't add a vowel.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/067.webp`: Copy the words above into the empty boxes.

### 13. Shadda

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/068.webp`: Shadda looks like a small w on top of a letter. It means you say the letter twice. First with a sukun, then with its vowel.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/069.webp`: Read each word. Press on the letter with the shadda and hold it a little.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/070.webp`: This page has words with sukun and with shadda. Read slowly and look at every mark.
4. Quiz: Which mark means you say the letter twice? **Shadda** · Which mark means the letter has no vowel? **Sukun** · How do you read اِنَّ? **in-na** · How do you read كُلَّ? **kul-la**

### 14. Tanween

1. *Two marks at the end.* Sometimes the last letter of a word has two vowel marks. This is called tanween. Tanween adds a little n sound at the end. [table]
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/071.webp`: Two fat-has make an an sound, like in waladan. An Alif often comes after them.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/072.webp`: Two kasras make an in sound, like in nuurin.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/073.webp`: Two dammas make an un sound, like in qamarun.
5. Sort into an / in / un: وَلَدًا (an) · حَسَنًا (an) · عَمَلًا (an) · نُورٍ (in) · خَوْفٍ (in) · لَهَبٍ (in) · قَمَرٌ (un) · نَجْمٌ (un) · رَحِيمٌ (un)
6. Match: بًا = ban · بٍ = bin · بٌ = bun · دًا = dan · دٍ = din · دٌ = dun

### 15. Special signs

- **check:** The second rule on page 74 is printed in Turkish. The spoken line gives it in English.
1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/074.webp`: The little Ha at the end of a word can be long or short. After a letter with a vowel, make it long. After a sukun or a madd letter, keep it short.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/075.webp`: Read the words again. For each one, decide: long Ha or short Ha?
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/076.webp`: A small standing line on top is a hidden Alif. Read it as a long aa. A small standing line underneath is a long ii. The wavy madd sign means hold the sound even longer.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/077.webp`: When a word ends in Waw and Alif, we do not read the Alif. In a few words, a Waw or a Ya is read as a long aa.
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/078.webp`: A small madd sign under a letter means make it long. The word qasr under a letter means keep it short.
6. Quiz: In قَالُوا, do we read the Alif at the end? **No** · What does the wavy madd sign tell you? **Hold the sound longer** · In ذٰلِكَ, how do you read the Dhal? **dhaa**

### 16. Reading the Qur'an

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/079.webp`: Twenty-nine surahs begin with single letters. We read each one by its name, like Alif Lam Mim. Tap to hear them.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/080.webp`: After a fat-ha or a damma, say the Lam in Allah full and heavy. After a kasra, say it light and thin, as in Bismillah. Try it now.
3. Quiz: In هُوَ اللّٰهُ, is the Lam in Allah heavy or light? **Heavy** · In بِسْمِ اللّٰهِ, is the Lam in Allah heavy or light? **Light** · In رَسُولُ اللّٰهِ, is the Lam in Allah heavy or light? **Heavy** · In عِبَادِ اللّٰهِ, is the Lam in Allah heavy or light? **Light**
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/081.webp`: When you stop at the end of a word, the last letter gets a sukun. Tanween with an Alif turns into a long aa. Try a few words.
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/082.webp`: Shamsiyya means sun letters. After al, we do not say the Lam before them. Qamariyya means moon letters, and we say the Lam clearly. Tap and listen.
6. Sort into Sun: no Lam sound / Moon: say the Lam: ت (Sun: no Lam sound) · د (Sun: no Lam sound) · ر (Sun: no Lam sound) · س (Sun: no Lam sound) · ش (Sun: no Lam sound) · ن (Sun: no Lam sound) · ب (Moon: say the Lam) · ج (Moon: say the Lam) · ح (Moon: say the Lam) · ق (Moon: say the Lam) · ك (Moon: say the Lam) · م (Moon: say the Lam)

### 17. Nun with sukun

1. *Four rules for Nun.* Here are four rules for a Nun with sukun (نْ) and for tanween. Which rule you use depends on the letter that comes next. [table]
- **check:** Page 83 prints the idgham letters as ب ج م و (with ghunna) and ا ل ر ز (without). The correct letters are ي ن م و with ghunna and ل ر without, as the examples on the page show. The spoken line and the table give the correct letters; please correct the page in the Canva book.
2. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/083.webp`: Idgham means merging. Before Ya, Nun, Mim or Waw, the Nun merges with a hum called ghunna. Before Lam or Ra, it merges with no hum.
3. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/084.webp`: Iqlab means changing. When Ba comes after the Nun, we say a Mim sound instead, with a hum.
4. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/085.webp`: Izhar means making clear. Before the six throat letters, say the Nun clearly with no hum.
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/086.webp`: Ikhfa means hiding. Before these fifteen letters, say the Nun softly through your nose.
6. Match: Izhar = Make it clear · Idgham = Merge it · Iqlab = Change it to Mim · Ikhfa = Hide it
7. Quiz: Which letter after a Nun makes iqlab? **ب** · In مِنْ خَوْفٍ, Kha comes after the Nun. Which rule is it? **Izhar** · Which rule means hiding? **Ikhfa** · Which letter merges with the Nun without a hum? **ر**

### 18. Du'as of the prayer

1. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/087.webp`: These are du'as we say in our prayer. Ask a grown-up to read them with you.
- **check:** Arabic typed from page 87; please compare. "Wa jalla thana'uk" is in brackets in the book.
2. Du'a (At the start of the prayer, after the first Allahu akbar.): سُبْحَانَكَ اللّٰهُمَّ وَبِحَمْدِكَ وَتَبَارَكَ اسْمُكَ وَتَعَالٰى جَدُّكَ وَجَلَّ ثَنَاؤُكَ وَلَا اِلٰهَ غَيْرُكَ / Subhanaka Allahumma wa bihamdika, wa tabarakasmuka, wa ta'ala jadduka, wa jalla thana'uka, wa la ilaha ghairuk. / "Glory be to You, O Allah, and all praise is Yours. Blessed is Your name and high is Your majesty. Great is Your praise, and there is no god but You."
- **check:** Arabic typed from page 87; please compare.
3. Du'a (When we sit in the prayer.): اَلتَّحِيَّاتُ لِلّٰهِ وَالصَّلَوَاتُ وَالطَّيِّبَاتُ، اَلسَّلَامُ عَلَيْكَ اَيُّهَا النَّبِيُّ وَرَحْمَةُ اللّٰهِ وَبَرَكَاتُهُ، اَلسَّلَامُ عَلَيْنَا وَعَلٰى عِبَادِ اللّٰهِ الصَّالِحِينَ، اَشْهَدُ اَنْ لَا اِلٰهَ اِلَّا اللّٰهُ وَاَشْهَدُ اَنَّ مُحَمَّدًا عَبْدُهُ وَرَسُولُهُ / At-tahiyyatu lillahi was-salawatu wat-tayyibat. As-salamu alaika ayyuhan-nabiyyu wa rahmatullahi wa barakatuh. As-salamu alaina wa ala ibadillahis-salihin. Ash-hadu an la ilaha illallah, wa ash-hadu anna Muhammadan abduhu wa rasuluh. / "All greetings, prayers and good words are for Allah. Peace be on you, O Prophet, and the mercy of Allah and His blessings. Peace be on us and on the good servants of Allah. I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and His Messenger." (Sahih al-Bukhari 831)
- **check:** Arabic typed from page 87; please compare.
4. Du'a (After at-tahiyyat, in the last sitting of the prayer.): اَللّٰهُمَّ صَلِّ عَلٰى مُحَمَّدٍ وَعَلٰى اٰلِ مُحَمَّدٍ، كَمَا صَلَّيْتَ عَلٰى اِبْرَاهِيمَ وَعَلٰى اٰلِ اِبْرَاهِيمَ، اِنَّكَ حَمِيدٌ مَجِيدٌ. اَللّٰهُمَّ بَارِكْ عَلٰى مُحَمَّدٍ وَعَلٰى اٰلِ مُحَمَّدٍ، كَمَا بَارَكْتَ عَلٰى اِبْرَاهِيمَ وَعَلٰى اٰلِ اِبْرَاهِيمَ، اِنَّكَ حَمِيدٌ مَجِيدٌ / Allahumma salli ala Muhammadin wa ala ali Muhammad, kama sallaita ala Ibrahima wa ala ali Ibrahim, innaka hamidun majid. Allahumma barik ala Muhammadin wa ala ali Muhammad, kama barakta ala Ibrahima wa ala ali Ibrahim, innaka hamidun majid. / "O Allah, send Your prayers on Muhammad and on the family of Muhammad, as You sent Your prayers on Ibrahim and on the family of Ibrahim. You are the Praised, the Glorious. O Allah, bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You blessed Ibrahim and the family of Ibrahim. You are the Praised, the Glorious." (Sahih al-Bukhari 3370)
- **check:** Page 88 also has the du'a of qunut and Amantu billahi. They are shown only in the page picture.
5. Workbook page `books/alif-ba-reading/088.webp`: Two of these du'as begin with Rabbana. Rabbana means our Lord.
