## Islamic Stories for Kids (Level 2, ages 6–9)

Grandpa Musa tells the village children seven stories under the olive tree, each about one good manner.

### 1. Story one: Ibrahim, the boy who never lied

1. *Under the olive tree.* "Come, children! Come and sit with me!" Grandpa Musa was calling from under the big olive tree. The children came running. There was Amina, Yusuf, Fatima and Hassan, with little Zayd at the back. "Today," said Grandpa Musa, "I will tell you about a very special boy. His name was Ibrahim."
2. *The boy who never lies.* Ibrahim was eight years old. He lived in a small, peaceful village. He had bright, honest eyes and a warm smile. Everyone in the village knew him. They had a special name for him. They called him "the boy who never lies."
3. *Mama's words.* Every morning before Madrasa, Ibrahim's mother held his hands. "Ibrahim," she said, "a Muslim's tongue should only speak the truth." "Allah loves people who are honest, even when it is hard." Ibrahim always nodded. "Yes, Mama. I promise."
4. *Crash!.* One day at Madrasa, a strong wind blew in through the window. CRASH! A clay vase fell off the shelf. It broke into pieces, right next to Ibrahim's desk. All the children turned and stared at him. The teacher asked, "Ibrahim, did you break the vase?"
5. *Ibrahim tells the truth.* Ibrahim stood up calmly. "No, teacher. I did not break it." "The wind blew it off the shelf. I saw it happen." Everyone was staring at him, but he told the truth anyway. The teacher smiled and put his hand on Ibrahim's shoulder. "Well done, Ibrahim. Your honesty is a gift from Allah." "The Prophet (peace be upon him) said that truthfulness leads to goodness, and goodness leads to Paradise." All the children clapped.
6. Hadith: إِنَّ الصِّدْقَ يَهْدِي إِلَى الْبِرِّ، وَإِنَّ الْبِرَّ يَهْدِي إِلَى الْجَنَّةِ "Truthfulness leads to goodness, and goodness leads to Paradise." (Bukhari 6094, Muslim 2607)
7. Fill the gap: The **wind** blew the vase off the shelf. · Ibrahim's mother said a Muslim's tongue should only speak the **truth**. · Truthfulness leads to goodness, and goodness leads to **Paradise**.
8. *Something shiny.* After Madrasa, Ibrahim was walking home. He saw something shiny on the ground. It was a small leather pouch, full of gold coins! "This is not mine," Ibrahim said to himself. "I must find out who lost it."
9. *Door to door.* Ibrahim went from door to door. "Excuse me, did you lose a pouch of coins?" "No, not me," said one man. "Not mine," said another. Ibrahim did not give up. He kept on searching.
10. *The last door.* At last, he knocked on the very last door. An old woman opened it. Her eyes went wide. "My pouch! I lost it this morning!" she cried. "May Allah bless you, honest child!" She gave Ibrahim a big, tight hug.
11. *Amanah.* The old woman held out some coins. "Please take these as a reward." But Ibrahim gently shook his head. "No, Auntie. I did not do it for money." "I did it because it was right. We call this Amanah. It means being someone people can trust."
12. *The mango garden.* A few days later, some boys came up to Ibrahim. "Come with us!" they whispered. "We are going to climb into the garden and take some mangoes." "No one will see us. It will be fun!" Ibrahim looked at the garden wall. The mangoes looked delicious.
13. *Allah sees us.* But Ibrahim shook his head. "No," he said firmly. "Even if no one sees us, Allah sees us." "Taking what is not ours is stealing. And if someone asks where we got the mangoes, we would have to lie." "I will not steal. And I will not lie."
14. *We are sorry.* The boys stood still. They felt ashamed. Then one of them said, "You are right, Ibrahim. We are sorry." Ibrahim smiled kindly. "It's okay. Let's go and ask the owner if we can buy some mangoes." And that is exactly what they did.
15. Quiz: What did Ibrahim do with the pouch of coins? **He looked for the owner** · Why did Ibrahim say no to the reward? **He did not do it for money** · Why did Ibrahim say no to the boys and the mangoes? **Taking them would be stealing**
16. *Baba's cup.* That evening, Ibrahim reached up for a book on the shelf. His elbow bumped his father's favourite cup. CRASH! It fell and broke into pieces. Ibrahim's heart beat fast. No one had seen him. He could hide the pieces. He could say the cat did it. Then he remembered: "A Muslim does not lie."
17. *Worth more than a cup.* Ibrahim went to his father. "Baba, I'm sorry. I broke your cup. It was an accident." His father looked at him for a moment. Then he smiled and hugged Ibrahim tight. "My son, I am not angry about the cup. We can always get another cup." "But your honesty? Nobody could ever buy that."
18. *The truth, told kindly.* Ibrahim learned something else too. You can tell the truth in a kind way. One day his friend showed him a drawing. It was not very good. Ibrahim did not say, "That is ugly." He said, "I like the colours! Maybe you can add more details next time." This is called Adab. It means good manners, even when you are being honest.
19. *Everyone trusts Ibrahim.* As Ibrahim grew older, everyone in the village trusted him. The shopkeeper asked him to take goods to people's homes. The imam asked him to count the charity money. The neighbours asked him to look after their houses while they were away. Why? Because they all knew that Ibrahim never lies.
20. Match: Amanah = Being someone people can trust · Adab = Good manners · Al-Amin = The Trustworthy, a name of our Prophet · Pouch = A small bag · Madrasa = A school where we learn about Islam
21. *What did Ibrahim teach us?.* Grandpa Musa stopped and looked at the children. "Now tell me. What did Ibrahim teach us?" Amina put up her hand. "That we should always tell the truth!" Yusuf added, "Even when no one is watching. Allah is always watching." Grandpa Musa smiled proudly.
22. *Close your eyes.* "Now close your eyes," Grandpa Musa said softly. "Think of a time you told the truth when it was hard. How did you feel afterwards?" The children closed their eyes and thought. Fatima whispered, "I felt clean inside." Hassan said, "I felt brave." Little Zayd smiled. "I felt close to Allah."
23. *Al-Amin.* Grandpa Musa stood up and opened his arms wide. "Children, honesty lives in your words. It lives in your heart too, and in the promises you keep." "Even before he became a prophet, the people of Makkah called our Prophet (peace be upon him) Al-Amin." "Al-Amin means the Trustworthy." "Be like Ibrahim. Be like our beloved Prophet. Be honest, always." The children smiled and said together, "In shaa Allah!"
24. Ayah 9:119: "O you who believe, be mindful of Allah, and be with those who are truthful."
25. Quiz: Ibrahim was known as "the boy who never lies." **True** · Ibrahim kept the pouch of coins for himself. **False** · Ibrahim's mother taught him about honesty. **True** · Ibrahim said the cat broke his father's cup. **False** · The teacher was angry when Ibrahim told the truth. **False** · Ibrahim would not take the mangoes with the boys. **True** · Amanah means being trustworthy. **True** · The Prophet (peace be upon him) was called Al-Amin, the Trustworthy. **True** · Ibrahim said mean things about his friend's drawing. **False** · Adab means good manners. **True**
26. Order: The wind knocks the vase off the shelf. → Ibrahim finds a pouch of gold coins. → The old woman gets her pouch back. → Ibrahim says no to taking the mangoes. → Ibrahim breaks Baba's cup and owns up. → The whole village trusts Ibrahim.
27. *Your turn.* Now it is your turn to choose. On the next page there are four problems. For each one, pick what Ibrahim would do.
28. Quiz: You step on your friend's toy by accident and it breaks. What would Ibrahim do? **Tell my friend the truth and say sorry** · Your sister ate the last cookie, but Mama asks if you ate it. What would Ibrahim do? **Tell the truth kindly** · You find some money in the playground. What would Ibrahim do? **Give it to the teacher to find the owner** · Your friend asks you to say he was at your house, but he wasn't. What would Ibrahim do? **Say no, because a true friend never asks you to lie**
29. *A promise to Allah.* The children are making a promise to Allah about being honest. You can make one too.
30. Write: Write your own promise to Allah about being honest. Finish the sentences in your own words.

### 2. Story two: Aminah and the rainy season

1. *Another story!.* The next day, the children came running back to Grandpa Musa. "Another story! Another story!" they called. Grandpa Musa laughed. "All right, all right! Sit down." "Today I will tell you about a brave girl named Aminah, and the long, long rain."
2. *Aminah's village.* Aminah was a cheerful girl. She lived in a small farming village. She loved helping her father in the fields. Every morning she fed the chickens. "Alhamdulillah!" she would say. "We have food, we have a home, and we have each other!" Life was simple and good.
3. *The rain comes.* One year the rains came early, and they did not stop. Day after day, water poured from the sky. The rivers rose. The fields flooded. Aminah watched from the doorway. "Baba, when will it stop?" Her father shook his head. "Only Allah knows, my dear."
4. *The crops are gone.* When the rain slowed down at last, Aminah's father went to check the fields. He came back with tears in his eyes. "The crops are gone," he said softly. "All of them." Aminah's heart sank. No crops meant no food to sell. And no food to sell meant no money for new clothes or books.
5. *Why us?.* That night, Aminah lay in bed. She felt angry. "Why us?" she whispered. "We worked so hard. We prayed every day." "Why did Allah let this happen?" A tear rolled down her cheek. She did not understand.
6. *What is sabr?.* Her mother came and sat beside her. "Aminah, do you know what sabr means?" "Patience?" Aminah sniffed. "Yes. And sabr also means trusting Allah's plan, even when we cannot see it yet." "Allah says that with hardship comes ease. The ease is coming, my love. We just need to be patient."
7. Ayah 94:6: "Indeed, with hardship comes ease."
8. *Soup for Auntie Khadijah.* The next morning, Mama made a small pot of soup from the food they had left. "Aminah, take some to old Auntie Khadijah next door. She lives alone." "But Mama, we hardly have enough for ourselves!" Mama smiled gently. "When you give for Allah's sake, He always gives you more." So off went Aminah, through the rain, with the soup and a smile.
9. *A hungry cat.* On her way home, Aminah saw a thin, wet cat. It was shivering by the road. She looked at her last piece of bread. Her tummy rumbled. But the cat looked so hungry. "Here, little one," she whispered, and she broke the bread in half. The Prophet (peace be upon him) taught us something about this. There is a reward for kindness to any living creature. You can find it in Bukhari (2363) and Muslim (2244).
10. Quiz: Who did Aminah take the soup to? **Auntie Khadijah** · What did Aminah share with the cat? **Half of her bread** · What does sabr mean? **Patience, and trusting Allah's plan**
11. *So bored!.* The rain kept falling, day after day after day. Madrasa was closed because the roads were flooded. Aminah missed her friends and her teacher. She missed running in the sunshine. "I am so bored!" she sighed. Then she had an idea.
12. *Madrasa at home.* "If we can't go to Madrasa, I will bring Madrasa to us!" Aminah gathered the little children from the houses nearby. She taught them the Arabic alphabet. She told them stories of the Prophets. She helped them learn short surahs by heart. The little ones loved it. Aminah smiled. "Alhamdulillah. I can still do good, even in the rain."
13. *Drip, drip, drip.* One day the roof began to leak. Drip. Drip. Drip. Aminah's father climbed up to fix it. "Baba, can I help?" Aminah asked. "Yes! Pass me the palm leaves." They worked together in the rain, and neither of them complained. Aminah thought about what Mama had told her. Sabr means doing your best while you wait.
14. *Aminah's dua.* That night, Aminah spread out her prayer mat and prayed. She raised her hands and whispered, "Ya Allah, I don't understand why this is happening. But I trust You." "Please help my family. Please send us ease." She felt warm inside, as if Allah was listening. And He was.
15. Fill the gap: "Ya Allah, help me be **patient** when things are hard." · "Indeed, with hardship comes **ease**." · "I **trust** in Your plan, Ya Allah." · "Thank You, Allah. I am **grateful** for everything." · "Ya Allah, Your **plan** is always better than mine."
16. *The rainbow.* Then one morning, everything was silent. No dripping. No drumming on the roof. Aminah ran outside. The rain had stopped! Across the sky stretched the most beautiful rainbow she had ever seen. "Alhamdulillah!" she cried. "Alhamdulillah!"
17. *A hidden blessing.* Aminah's father planted the fields again. Then something amazing happened. The new crops grew faster and stronger than before! The flood water had made the soil richer. "SubhanAllah," said her father. "Allah's plan is always better than ours." Aminah smiled. A blessing had been hiding inside the hard times all along.
18. *Help from the city.* A few days later, a truck rolled into the village. It was full of food, clothes and Madrasa books. Muslims in the city had sent them! "They heard about the flood and collected charity for us," the village elder explained. Aminah helped hand the things out to every family. She whispered, "Allah never forgets His people."
19. *Shukr.* That evening, the family sat down to dinner. It was their first full meal in weeks. Before they ate, Aminah's mother said, "Let us thank Allah for the food, and for everything else too." "For the rain that made us stronger. For the patience that brought us closer." "And for the help that showed us we are never alone. This is shukr. It means being thankful to Allah."
20. *Thank You for the rain.* That night, Aminah made a special dua. "Ya Allah, thank You for the rain." Her mother looked surprised. "You are thanking Allah for the rain? It destroyed our crops!" Aminah smiled. "Yes, Mama. But it taught me patience. It made our soil richer. It brought us closer together." "I am thankful for the hard times too."
21. *Sabr and shukr go together.* Grandpa Musa stopped and looked at the children. "Do you see? Sabr and shukr go together." "When times are hard, be patient and trust Allah. When times are good, be thankful to Allah." "Our Prophet (peace be upon him) said that everything that happens to a believer is good for him."
22. Hadith: عَجَبًا لِأَمْرِ الْمُؤْمِنِ، إِنَّ أَمْرَهُ كُلَّهُ خَيْرٌ، وَلَيْسَ ذَاكَ لِأَحَدٍ إِلَّا لِلْمُؤْمِنِ، إِنْ أَصَابَتْهُ سَرَّاءُ شَكَرَ فَكَانَ خَيْرًا لَهُ، وَإِنْ أَصَابَتْهُ ضَرَّاءُ صَبَرَ فَكَانَ خَيْرًا لَهُ "How wonderful is the affair of the believer! All of it is good for him, and this is only for the believer. If something good happens to him, he is thankful, and that is good for him. If something bad happens to him, he is patient, and that is good for him." (Muslim 2999)
23. Sort into Sabr (patience) / Shukr (thankfulness): Your favourite toy breaks and you stay calm. (Sabr (patience)) · You get a surprise gift from your uncle. (Shukr (thankfulness)) · You are ill, but you do not complain. (Sabr (patience)) · Mama makes your favourite food for dinner. (Shukr (thankfulness)) · Your friend is mean to you, but you forgive him. (Sabr (patience)) · You wake up healthy and say Alhamdulillah. (Shukr (thankfulness)) · It rains on your birthday, but you still smile. (Sabr (patience)) · You pass a hard test after studying for it. (Shukr (thankfulness)) · You wait for your turn without pushing. (Sabr (patience)) · You thank Allah before you eat your meal. (Shukr (thankfulness))
24. *When were you patient?.* Grandpa Musa asked, "Tell me. When have you been patient?" Amina said, "When my baby brother was ill, and I had to be quiet all week." Yusuf said, "When I studied hard for a test, but I still didn't get the best mark." Fatima said, "When my best friend moved away." "And did things get better?" asked Grandpa Musa. "Yes!" they all said together.
25. *Tomorrow, another story.* Grandpa Musa stood up and opened his arms. "Children, keep Aminah's story in your hearts." "Be patient when life is hard, because Allah has a plan. Be thankful when life is good, because Allah is generous." "And always trust that ease comes after hardship." The children smiled and said together, "In shaa Allah!" "Now," said Grandpa Musa with a twinkle in his eye, "tomorrow I have another story for you..."
26. Order: The rain comes and does not stop. → The flood washes away the crops. → Aminah takes soup to Auntie Khadijah. → Aminah teaches the little children at home. → The rain stops and a rainbow fills the sky. → A truck brings food from the city.
27. Quiz: Why did the new crops grow so well? **The flood made the soil richer** · What did Aminah do when Madrasa was closed? **She taught the little children at home** · Who sent the truck full of food and clothes? **Muslims in the city** · Why did Aminah thank Allah for the rain? **It taught her patience and brought her family closer**
28. Quiz: It rains on the day of your trip to the park. What would Aminah do? **Stay calm and find something good to do at home** · Your old neighbour lives alone, and your family has only a little food. What would Aminah do? **Share some with her** · Something you hoped for does not happen. What would Aminah do? **Say Alhamdulillah and trust Allah's plan**
29. Write: Draw your favourite scene from Aminah's story on a piece of paper. Then write what your drawing shows and how it makes you feel.
30. Write: Write your own dua. Ask Allah to help you be patient, and thank Him for something good He gave you.

### 3. Story three: Yusuf and the wounded bird

1. *A story about Yusuf.* "Grandpa, tell us another story!" the children begged. Grandpa Musa smiled. "Today's story is about a boy named Yusuf." "Yusuf was strong and fast. He was the best at football and the loudest in the playground." "But one day he found out that the strongest part of you is your heart."
2. *The champion.* Yusuf was the strongest boy in the village. He could run the fastest. He could climb the highest tree. He could kick the ball the farthest. All the boys wanted to be on his team. "I am the champion!" Yusuf would shout. But Yusuf was about to find out what real strength is.
3. *Cheep, cheep.* One afternoon, Yusuf was walking home from Madrasa. He heard a tiny sound. "Cheep... cheep..." There, at the side of the road, was a small bird. Its wing was bent. It could not fly. It looked up at Yusuf with frightened eyes. Yusuf knelt down slowly. "Oh no... you're hurt."
4. *Just leave it.* His friends came over. "Just leave it, Yusuf. It's only a bird. Come and play football!" But Yusuf shook his head. "No. It's hurt and scared. I can't leave it here." "The Prophet (peace be upon him) was kind to every creature, even the smallest ones. I will help it."
5. *The slowest walk.* Very gently, Yusuf scooped up the little bird. He held it close to his chest so it would feel safe. "Don't worry, little one," he whispered. "I'll take care of you." He walked home slowly, more carefully than ever before. The boy who ran the fastest was now walking the slowest, because he was being kind.
6. *A home for Noor.* Yusuf's mother smiled when she saw the bird. "Let's make it a little home," she said. Together, they lined a box with soft cloth and straw. They laid the bird gently inside. "There," said Mama. "Now it can rest and get better." Yusuf named the bird Noor. Noor means light.
7. *Every morning, every evening.* Every morning before Madrasa, Yusuf gave Noor seeds and water. Every evening, he checked the little wing. "Is it better yet?" he asked. "Not yet, habibi. Healing takes time." So Yusuf learned to care for Noor without rushing. Day after day, he was there for the little bird.
8. *Many small acts.* Some days, Yusuf felt impatient. "Why is it taking so long?" he sighed. His mother said, "Mercy is lots of small acts, Yusuf, done with love again and again." "Every seed you give and every gentle word is mercy." Yusuf nodded. He sat beside Noor and read to it softly.
9. *The cat.* One afternoon, the neighbour's cat crept into the house. It sniffed its way towards Noor's box. Yusuf jumped up. "No! Stay away!" He stood between the cat and the bird with his arms spread wide. The cat ran off. Yusuf's heart was pounding, but Noor was safe. "I will always protect you," he whispered.
10. Quiz: Why did Yusuf walk home so slowly? **He was carrying the hurt bird** · What does the name Noor mean? **Light** · What did Mama say mercy is? **Lots of small acts done with love**
11. *A tiny flutter.* Then one morning, Noor flapped its wings! It was only a tiny flutter. But Yusuf's eyes went wide. "Mama! Mama! Noor moved its wing!" Day by day, the little bird grew stronger. First short hops. Then small flutters. Then a flight across the room! "You're getting better!" Yusuf cheered.
12. *Kindness spreads.* One day Yusuf brought Noor to school. His friends crowded round. "You really saved it!" they said. "Can I hold it?" one boy asked gently. Yusuf smiled. "Be very soft. Like this." The boys who once said "leave it" now wanted to be kind too. Kindness spreads from one person to the next, like a smile.
13. *The new boy.* A few days later, a new boy joined their school. His name was Bilal, and he had just moved to the village. At break time, Bilal sat alone. No one talked to him. Yusuf watched from across the yard. He remembered Noor on the day he found it, small and scared and alone. "That boy needs kindness too," Yusuf thought.
14. *Sharing lunch.* Yusuf walked over and sat beside Bilal. "Assalamu alaikum! I'm Yusuf. Do you want to share my lunch?" Bilal looked surprised. Then he gave a shy smile. "Wa alaikum assalam. I'm Bilal." They shared bread and dates. Yusuf asked about Bilal's old village and his favourite games. He asked about his family. And he really listened to the answers.
15. *Bilal smiles.* By the end of the week, Bilal was smiling every day. He played football with Yusuf and the boys. He laughed at their jokes. He shared his mother's cookies with everyone. "Thank you, Yusuf," Bilal said one day. "You were the first person here to be kind to me." Yusuf grinned. "That's what Muslims do. We take care of each other."
16. *The teacher's hadith.* Their teacher had seen what Yusuf did. "Class, I want to share something the Prophet (peace be upon him) said." "The Most Merciful shows mercy to those who are merciful." "Be merciful to those on earth, and the One in the heaven will be merciful to you." He looked at Yusuf and smiled. "Mercy takes a strong heart. It is the greatest strength of all."
17. Hadith: الرَّاحِمُونَ يَرْحَمُهُمُ الرَّحْمَنُ، ارْحَمُوا مَنْ فِي الْأَرْضِ يَرْحَمْكُمْ مَنْ فِي السَّمَاءِ "The merciful are shown mercy by the Most Merciful. Be merciful to those on earth, and the One in the heaven will be merciful to you." (Abu Dawud 4941, Tirmidhi 1924)
18. *Time to let go.* Weeks went by. Noor was strong again. Its wing had healed, and it could fly high and far. "It is time to let Noor go," his mother said gently. Yusuf's heart felt heavy. He loved the little bird. But he knew Noor belonged in the sky.
19. *Fly free, Noor.* Yusuf carried Noor to the open field. He held the bird up high and opened his hands. Noor looked at him for a moment. Then it spread its wings and flew! Up, up, up into the blue sky. Yusuf's eyes were wet, but he was smiling. "Fly free, Noor. May Allah protect you." The bird circled once above him, as if to say thank you. Then it flew away.
20. *Two names of Allah.* Grandpa Musa smiled at the children. "Rahma means mercy. It is at the heart of Islam." "Allah calls Himself Ar-Rahman and Ar-Raheem. Both names tell us about His mercy." [table] "Be kind to a bird, to a new pupil or to an old neighbour. Then you are sharing Allah's mercy with your own hands." "And whoever shows mercy, Allah shows mercy to them."
21. Ayah 1:3: "The Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful."
22. *When have you shown mercy?.* "What about you?" asked Grandpa Musa. "When have you shown mercy?" Amina said, "I helped a lost kitten find its way home." Hassan said, "I forgave my brother when he broke my toy." Fatima said, "I sat with the new girl at school so she wouldn't be alone." Grandpa Musa beamed. "You see? You are all like Yusuf. There is mercy in every one of you."
23. *Be the first.* Grandpa Musa put his hand on his heart. "Children, be kind to every creature, big and small. Be gentle with anyone who is hurting." "Be the first to show mercy. Don't wait for someone else to start." "The Prophet (peace be upon him) said: Whoever does not show mercy will not be shown mercy." The children nodded and said together, "We will be merciful, in shaa Allah!"
24. Hadith: مَنْ لَا يَرْحَمُ لَا يُرْحَمُ "Whoever does not show mercy will not be shown mercy." (Bukhari 5997, Muslim 2318)
25. Order: Yusuf finds a bird with a hurt wing. → His friends say, "Just leave it." → Yusuf and Mama make a box home for Noor. → Yusuf chases the cat away. → Yusuf shares his lunch with Bilal. → Noor flies away into the sky.
26. Quiz: What did Yusuf do when his friends said, "Just leave it"? **He helped the bird anyway** · Why did Yusuf go and sit with Bilal? **Bilal was alone, like Noor had been** · Why did Yusuf let Noor go? **Noor was healed and belonged in the sky** · What do the names Ar-Rahman and Ar-Raheem tell us about? **Allah's mercy**
27. Quiz: You see a puppy with a hurt paw. What would Yusuf do? **Ask a grown-up to help me look after it** · A new child is sitting alone at lunch. What would Yusuf do? **Go and sit with them** · Your little brother breaks your toy by mistake. What would Yusuf do? **Forgive him**
28. *Kindness bingo.* How many of these kind things can you do this week? Help Mama with the chores.; Smile at someone.; Share your snack.; Say salam to someone new.; Feed an animal.; Forgive someone.; Help carry something heavy.; Make a card for a friend.; Let someone go first.; Pick up litter.; Say JazakAllahu khairan.
29. Write: Write six kind things you will do this week, one on each line. Can you make your chain reach twenty this week?
30. Write: Imagine Noor the bird can read! Write Noor a letter telling it how you feel.

### 4. Story four: Hassan and the busy morning

1. *Honouring parents.* "Who here loves their mother and father?" asked Grandpa Musa. "We do!" all the children shouted. "Good! But do you know what it really means to honour your parents?" The children looked at each other. "Let me tell you about a boy named Hassan. He almost forgot."
2. *Grumpy mornings.* Hassan was a good boy. But in the mornings... he was grumpy. "Hassan! Wake up, habibi! Time for school!" "Five more minutes!" he groaned. "Hassan, breakfast is ready!" "I'm not hungry!" he mumbled into his pillow. Every morning was the same battle.
3. *Before the sun comes up.* Here is something Hassan did not know. Every morning, his mother woke up at Fajr, before the sun. She prayed. She cooked breakfast. She ironed his clothes and packed his bag. All this happened before Hassan even opened his eyes. When he was grumpy, it hurt her heart. But she never complained.
4. *No goodbye.* One morning, Hassan was extra grumpy. "Mama, where are my shoes?!" he shouted. "By the door, habibi." "Why is my bag so heavy?! You packed too much!" His mother said nothing. She just gave a sad little smile. Hassan grabbed his bag and left. He did not say goodbye. He did not say thank you.
5. *Your mother.* At school that day, the teacher told them a hadith. "A man asked the Prophet (peace be upon him) who deserved his best treatment." "He said: Your mother. Then your mother. Then your mother. Then your father." Hassan felt a twist in his tummy. He thought about that morning, and about his mother's sad smile.
6. Hadith: جَاءَ رَجُلٌ إِلَى رَسُولِ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ فَقَالَ: يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ، مَنْ أَحَقُّ النَّاسِ بِحُسْنِ صَحَابَتِي؟ قَالَ: أُمُّكَ. قَالَ: ثُمَّ مَنْ؟ قَالَ: ثُمَّ أُمُّكَ. قَالَ: ثُمَّ مَنْ؟ قَالَ: ثُمَّ أُمُّكَ. قَالَ: ثُمَّ مَنْ؟ قَالَ: ثُمَّ أَبُوكَ "A man came to the Messenger of Allah and asked, 'Who has the most right to my good company?' He said, 'Your mother.' The man asked, 'Then who?' He said, 'Your mother.' The man asked, 'Then who?' He said, 'Your mother.' The man asked, 'Then who?' He said, 'Your father.'" (Bukhari 5971, Muslim 2548)
7. *Another hard morning.* The next morning, Hassan still did not want to listen. "Hassan, wake up! You'll be late for school!" "Five more minutes!" "Hassan, please put your shoes on." "I don't want to!" "Hassan, eat your breakfast." "I'm not hungry!" He pushed away everything his mother offered.
8. *Mama looks tired.* His mother said nothing more. She sat down at the table without a word. Then Hassan noticed something new. His mother looked... tired. Her eyes were heavy. Her shoulders drooped. She had been awake since Fajr, cooking and cleaning and ironing his uniform. All for him.
9. *A heavy heart.* At school, Hassan could not concentrate. He kept thinking about his mother's face. He remembered how she looked when he said, "I don't want to." He remembered her smile disappearing. "Is everything okay, Hassan?" asked his teacher. Hassan nodded. But his heart felt heavy. "I wasn't kind to Mama this morning," he thought.
10. *Not even "uff".* That afternoon, the imam told them something important. "Do you know what Allah says about parents?" "He says: do not even say uff to them. Not even a small sound of being fed up." "And lower your wing of humility to them, out of mercy." Hassan's eyes went wide. Not even "uff"? He had said much worse than that this morning!
11. Ayah 17:23: "Your Lord has commanded that you worship none but Him, and that you be good to your parents. If one or both of them reach old age with you, do not say "uff" to them and do not shout at them, but speak to them with kind words."
12. *Walking home.* Hassan walked home slowly that day. He thought about everything his mother did for him. She woke up early every single day.; She made his favourite food.; She washed his clothes.; She helped him with his homework.; She kissed him goodnight. And what had he done this morning? He had pushed her away. "I need to put this right," Hassan decided.
13. *Ya Allah.* That evening, Hassan really looked at his mother. Her eyes were tired. Her shoulders drooped. She had been cooking and cleaning and washing all day long. And Hassan had complained. He had rushed past her. He had not even said thank you. "Ya Allah," Hassan whispered. "I have not been kind to her."
14. Quiz: When did Hassan's mother wake up every morning? **At Fajr, before the sun** · What did the imam say Allah tells us not to say to our parents? **Uff** · How did Hassan feel at school? **His heart felt heavy**
15. *A new day.* The next morning, Hassan woke up before everyone else. He made his bed neatly, and nobody had asked him to. He folded his pyjamas too. "Today will be different," he said to himself. "Today I will show Mama and Baba that I love them. I will show them with what I do."
16. *Breakfast for Maryam.* Hassan went to the kitchen. He poured cereal and milk for his little sister Maryam. "Here you go, Maryam!" he said cheerfully. Maryam's eyes went wide. "You made breakfast for me?!" "Yes! Sit and eat. I'll clean up afterwards." Maryam giggled. "You're the best brother!" Hassan felt warm inside. Helping felt good.
17. *Hard work.* Next, Hassan tidied his room. He folded his clothes, put away his toys and swept the floor. Then he went to the living room and folded the family's washing too. It was hard work. His arms got tired. "Mama does this every single day," he realised. "And she never complains. Not once." Hassan's respect for his mother grew and grew.
18. *The best gift.* When Mama came downstairs, she stopped. The kitchen was clean. Maryam had eaten. The washing was folded. She looked at Hassan with tears in her eyes. "Hassan... did you do all of this?" He nodded shyly. "I wanted to help you, Mama. You do so much for us." Mama hugged him tight. "May Allah bless you, my son. This is the best gift you could give me."
19. *Please rest, Mama.* Later, Hassan found Mama washing the dishes. "Mama, please sit down. I'll finish these." His mother looked surprised. "Are you sure, habibi?" "Yes, Mama. You've been working all day. Please rest." She sat down with a cup of tea, and her tired eyes began to shine.
20. *Welcome home, Baba.* When Baba came home from work, Hassan ran to greet him. "Assalamu alaikum, Baba! Here are your slippers." He brought Baba a glass of water before anyone asked. Baba looked at Mama. Mama smiled. "What has happened to our Hassan?" Baba asked gently. "I'm trying to be the son you deserve, Baba," said Hassan. Baba's eyes filled with happy tears.
21. *Paradise under her feet.* Grandpa Musa stopped the story. "Children, do you know what the Prophet (peace be upon him) said about mothers?" "Once a man came to ask him if he should go away to fight. The Prophet asked if he had a mother." "The man said yes. Then the Prophet said: Stay with her, for Paradise is under her feet." "Our parents give so much for us. The least we can do is honour them."
- **check:** Nasa'i 3104 (Mu'awiyah ibn Jahimah) is graded hasan by al-Albani. The book had the popular wording "Paradise lies at the feet of mothers", which is weak, so the Nasa'i wording is used instead.
22. Hadith: فَالْزَمْهَا فَإِنَّ الْجَنَّةَ تَحْتَ رِجْلَيْهَا "Stay with her, for Paradise is beneath her feet." (Nasa'i 3104)
23. *What can we do?.* "What can we do for our parents?" asked Grandpa Musa. Amina said, "I can make Mama's bed for her!" Hassan said, "I can be quiet when Baba is resting." Fatima said, "I can say thank you more, for everything." Omar said, "I can stop complaining when they say no." Grandpa Musa clapped his hands. "Wonderful! Every small act of respect is worship."
24. *A dua for parents.* Grandpa Musa stood and opened his arms wide. "My dear ones, your parents are a gift from Allah." "Honour them with your words and with what you do. And be patient with them." "Never raise your voice. Never roll your eyes. And always make dua for them." Then the children said a dua from the Qur'an together: "Rabbir-hamhuma kama rabbayani sagheera." It means: "My Lord, have mercy on them, as they raised me when I was small."
25. Ayah 17:24: "And lower to them the wing of humility, out of mercy, and say: "My Lord, have mercy on them, as they raised me when I was small.""
26. Order: Hassan leaves for school without saying goodbye. → The teacher tells the hadith about mothers. → The imam says not even to say "uff." → Hassan makes breakfast for Maryam. → Mama sees the clean kitchen and hugs Hassan. → Hassan brings Baba his slippers.
27. Match: Fajr = The dawn prayer · Honour = To treat with great respect · Uff = A sound of being fed up · Grumpy = Cross and in a bad mood
28. Quiz: In the hadith, who deserves our best treatment first? **Our mother** · What did Hassan make for Maryam? **Cereal and milk** · Why did Hassan's arms get tired? **He did the jobs Mama does every day** · What did Hassan say to Baba? **"I'm trying to be the son you deserve."**
29. Quiz: Mama calls you to get up for school. What would Hassan do now? **Get up and say good morning** · Baba says no to something you want. What would Hassan do? **Stay calm and not say "uff"** · Mama looks tired after a long day. What would Hassan do? **Offer to help her**
30. Write: Interview your parents! Ask them these questions and write down their answers.
31. Write: Now write your own special dua for your parents.
32. *My respect thermometer.* How respectful were you this week? Count the ones you did. I said Assalamu alaikum to my parents every morning.; I said JazakAllahu khairan to Mama or Baba.; I helped without being asked.; I spoke softly and did not raise my voice.; I made dua for my parents.; I did what I was asked the first time.; I smiled at my parents.; I did not complain about food or chores.; I gave Mama or Baba a hug.; I told my parents, "I love you." Seven to ten: superstar! Four to six: getting there! Three or fewer: try again tomorrow, in shaa Allah!
33. Write: How many did you do this week? Which one will you try harder at next week?

### 5. Story five: the giving hands of Amina

1. *Twelve dolls.* Amina had everything a girl could want. Toys filled her shelves. Dresses filled her wardrobe. Her books were piled so high they almost touched the ceiling. But somehow, Amina always wanted more. "Mama, can I have a new doll?" she asked every week. "Amina, you already have twelve dolls," Mama would say. But Amina did not listen.
2. *A story about me?.* That afternoon, the children gathered under the olive tree. Grandpa Musa smiled warmly. "Today I will tell you about a girl named Amina. It is about the day she found the secret of happiness." Amina's eyes went wide. "A story about me?" Grandpa Musa winked. "Let's see if you know who she is."
3. *On the way to the market.* One day, Amina walked to the market with Mama. On the way, she saw a family she had never noticed before. A mother sat on the ground with two small children. Their clothes were torn, and they had no shoes. The little girl held a broken doll with one arm missing. Amina stared. Her heart felt heavy. "Mama... why don't they have anything?"
4. *A test from Allah.* Mama knelt down beside Amina. "Not everyone has what we have, habibi. Allah tests some people with less, and He tests us with more." "Tests us?" Amina asked. "Yes. When Allah gives you plenty, He sees what you do with it. Will you keep it all, or will you share?" Amina looked back at the little girl with the broken doll. She thought of her twelve perfect dolls at home.
5. *A sleepless night.* That night, Amina could not sleep. She kept thinking about the little girl. "She would love any of my dolls," Amina whispered. But then a selfish voice inside her said, "But they are yours!" Amina pulled the blanket over her head. "What would the Prophet (peace be upon him) do?" she asked herself. Deep in her heart, she already knew the answer.
6. *The box.* The next morning, Amina made up her mind. She opened her wardrobe and looked at all her things. "I don't need all of this," she said bravely. She picked three dolls: her second, third and fourth favourites. She folded two dresses she had hardly worn. She stacked five books she had already read twice. "These will make someone else happy," she whispered.
7. *The upper hand.* Amina carried the box down the road. Her hands were shaking a little. "What if I miss them?" she thought. Mama walked beside her. "The Prophet (peace be upon him) said that the upper hand is better than the lower hand." "The upper hand is the hand that gives." Amina held the box tighter. "I want to be the upper hand, Mama."
8. Hadith: الْيَدُ الْعُلْيَا خَيْرٌ مِنَ الْيَدِ السُّفْلَى، فَالْيَدُ الْعُلْيَا هِيَ الْمُنْفِقَةُ، وَالسُّفْلَى هِيَ السَّائِلَةُ "The upper hand is better than the lower hand. The upper hand is the one that gives, and the lower hand is the one that asks." (Bukhari 1429, Muslim 1033)
9. *A doll for the little girl.* When they reached the family, Amina knelt down and held out a doll. "This is for you," she said softly. The girl's eyes went wide. She looked at her mother, and her mother nodded. Slowly, the girl took the doll and hugged it tight. Amina felt warm and happy inside. It was better than getting a hundred new toys.
10. *Lighter.* Grandpa Musa looked at the children. "Do you know what happened next?" "Amina went home, and her room felt bigger. Her heart felt lighter." "She had found the secret. The more you give, the more Allah gives you." "Sometimes He gives toys or money. More often, He gives peace and love and barakah." Amina smiled shyly. She remembered that day very well.
11. *Sadaqah.* "Now," said Grandpa Musa, "a gift for Allah can be very small." "A smile is sadaqah. Sharing your lunch is sadaqah. A kind word is sadaqah." "Even moving something harmful out of the road is sadaqah." "Allah loves a generous heart, however small the gift." A smile is sadaqah: Tirmidhi 1956. A kind word, and moving harm from the road: Bukhari 2989 and Muslim 1009.
12. Quiz: What did Amina see on the way to the market? **A family with no shoes and a broken doll** · What is "the upper hand"? **The hand that gives** · Which of these is sadaqah? **A smile**
13. *Safiya.* "The next day at school," Grandpa went on, "a new girl arrived. Her name was Safiya." Safiya had no friends yet, and she looked lonely. Amina went up to her. "Here, you can have my favourite pencil case. I want you to feel welcome." Safiya's eyes lit up like stars.
14. *Half a sandwich.* At lunchtime, Amina saw a boy named Bilal sitting alone. He had forgotten his lunch. Without thinking twice, Amina broke her sandwich in two. She gave him the bigger half. "But that's your lunch!" said Bilal. Amina smiled. "Sharing makes it taste better."
15. *Heavy bags.* On her way home, Amina saw old Aunty Khadijah carrying some heavy bags. Amina ran over. "Let me help you, Aunty!" She carried the bags all the way to Aunty Khadijah's door. "May Allah bless your hands, my dear," the old woman said. Her eyes were shining.
16. *The sadaqah box.* Amina had saved her pocket money all week. She could have bought sweets. She could have bought a new notebook. But that Friday at the masjid, she dropped every coin into the sadaqah box. "For people who need it more than me," she whispered. Her heart felt full, fuller than any sweet could make it.
17. *How it starts.* "So you see," said Grandpa Musa, "Amina gave and gave, and she never ran out." "You don't need to have a lot to be generous. You give from what you have, with love." Little Zayd put up his hand. "Grandpa, can I share my dates with everyone?" Grandpa Musa laughed. "That, my boy, is exactly how it starts."
18. *Zayd's dates.* Zayd jumped up and pulled a little bag of dates from his pocket. One for Amina. One for Yusuf. One for Fatima. One for Hassan. The last one he placed gently in Grandpa Musa's hand. "For you, Grandpa. The biggest one!" Grandpa Musa's eyes shone. "JazakAllahu khairan, my little one."
19. *Giving never makes you poorer.* "Now listen carefully," said Grandpa Musa. "The Prophet (peace be upon him) said: Charity never makes your wealth any less." "When you give for Allah's sake, you never lose." The children sat very still and thought about his words.
- **check:** The book quoted "The generous one is close to Allah, close to people, close to Paradise" (Tirmidhi 1961, weak) and "Give, and do not fear poverty". Both were replaced with this sound hadith.
20. Hadith: مَا نَقَصَتْ صَدَقَةٌ مِنْ مَالٍ "Charity never makes wealth less." (Muslim 2588)
21. *The Giving Garden.* "I have an idea!" said Amina, jumping to her feet. "Let's start a Giving Garden! We can grow vegetables together and share them with anyone who needs food." "Yes!" the children cheered. "We can give cucumbers to Aunty Khadijah!" "And tomatoes to Uncle Idris!" Grandpa Musa smiled. "Now that is generosity that keeps on growing."
22. *Door to door.* Weeks passed. The seeds sprouted, and the plants grew tall. One sunny morning, the Giving Garden was ready. The children picked baskets full of tomatoes, cucumbers and fresh mint. They took them from door to door. Some went to Aunty Khadijah and Uncle Idris, and some to the new family on the corner. Every neighbour smiled. Each "JazakAllahu khairan" made the children glow inside.
23. *Under the olive tree.* That evening, Grandpa Musa gathered the children close under the olive tree. "My dear ones," he said softly, "when you give, Allah gives you more." "Sometimes it is something you can hold. Often it is love, and barakah, and peace in your heart." The children hugged him tight. And they kept on giving, every day after that.
24. Order: Amina asks Mama for another new doll. → Amina sees a family with a broken doll. → Amina fills a box with dolls, dresses and books. → The little girl hugs her new doll. → Amina shares her sandwich with Bilal. → The children start a Giving Garden.
25. Match: Sadaqah = A gift or good deed for Allah's sake · Barakah = A blessing from Allah · Selfish = Thinking only about yourself · Generous = Happy to give and share
26. Quiz: What did Amina give Safiya? **Her favourite pencil case** · What did Amina do with her pocket money? **She put it all in the sadaqah box** · Who got the biggest date? **Grandpa Musa** · What was the Giving Garden for? **Sharing vegetables with people who need food**
27. Quiz: You have two biscuits, and your friend has none. What would Amina do? **Give one to my friend** · You have toys you never play with any more. What would Amina do? **Give them to a child who needs them** · An old neighbour is carrying heavy shopping. What would Amina do? **Offer to help carry it**
28. Write: Write three things you will give or share this week. A smile or a kind word counts too!

### 6. Story six: the day Mariam chose to forgive

1. *A story for Mariam.* The children gathered under the olive tree again. Grandpa Musa looked round at their faces and smiled. "Today I will tell you about a girl named Mariam," he said. "Mariam found out that forgiving someone does not mean pretending nothing happened. It means choosing peace when your heart is hurt." Mariam sat very still. It was her story, and she knew it well.
2. *The spoiled picture.* Mariam loved drawing more than anything. For a whole week she worked on a picture for the school exhibition. It showed a peaceful garden with bright flowers and a little path under the trees. Then Safa reached across the table for a pencil. Her sleeve caught the paper, and the picture fell to the floor. A dark mark spread across the garden. Mariam stared at it and said nothing. Safa's eyes filled with tears. "I am so sorry," she whispered.
3. *Not ready yet.* Mariam picked up the spoiled picture and held it close. She knew Safa had not meant to spoil it. But knowing that did not make the hurt go away. When Safa came to talk to her, Mariam turned away. "I don't want to talk right now." Safa looked down and nodded. Mariam was not ready to forgive. Her heart felt too heavy.
4. *The teacher's advice.* After class, Mariam's teacher found her. "You are allowed to feel hurt," she said gently. "Forgiving does not mean saying the mistake was fine. It means letting go of the anger, little by little." "And it means choosing not to hurt the other person back." Mariam looked at the smudged garden. "What if I am still angry?" "Then ask Allah to help your heart, one step at a time," her teacher said.
5. *Safa tries to help.* The next morning, Safa came to Mariam with fresh paper and coloured pencils. "I can't undo what happened," she said softly. "But I want to help you make another picture, if you'll let me." Mariam looked at the paper and pencils. Safa had not made excuses. She had owned up to her mistake, and now she was trying to put it right. Mariam was still hurt. But a small space opened up in her heart.
6. *Mariam's dua.* That evening, Mariam spread out her prayer mat and raised her hands. "Ya Allah," she whispered, "You know my heart is hurt. Please help me forgive Safa without pretending nothing happened." "Help me speak kindly. Help us both learn from this." Her anger did not all disappear. But after her dua, she felt ready to take one small step.
7. Quiz: How did Mariam's picture get spoiled? **Safa's sleeve caught it by accident** · What did the teacher say forgiving means? **Letting go of the anger, little by little** · What did Safa bring the next morning? **Fresh paper and coloured pencils**
8. *Starting again.* The next day, Mariam asked Safa to sit beside her. "You can help me start again." Safa smiled with relief. Together they drew a new garden. Mariam drew a wide path, and Safa added bright flowers along the edges. At first they worked without talking. Then Safa told Mariam how frightened she had felt after the accident. Mariam listened. Forgiving began with listening.
9. *I forgive you.* Mariam took a deep breath. "I forgive you," she said. "But please be careful with my work next time." "And if something goes wrong, tell me straight away." Safa nodded. "I will. I'm sorry, Mariam." Mariam could open her heart again and still be sensible. Forgiving and being careful can go together.
10. *The new picture.* By the end of the week, the new picture was finished. It was different from the first one. Mariam thought it was even more special, because two friends had made it together. Their teacher put it in the middle of the exhibition. "This picture shows a garden," she said. "It also shows patience, honesty, owning up and forgiving." Mariam and Safa looked at each other and smiled.
11. *Passing it on.* A few days later, a younger pupil tore a paper model by accident during art. The little girl began to cry. She thought everyone would be angry. Mariam knelt beside her. "It was an accident," she said. "Let's see how we can fix it." Safa brought glue and fresh paper. As they worked, Mariam saw that she could pass forgiveness on to others too.
12. *The broken pencil.* That evening, Mariam found little Hamza holding her favourite coloured pencil. It had snapped in two. Hamza's eyes filled with tears. For a moment, Mariam felt the old anger rising. Then she remembered Safa's sorry, her teacher's advice and her own dua. "I'm sad that it broke," Mariam said. "But I know you didn't do it on purpose." "Next time, please ask before you use my things." Hamza promised he would.
13. *A heavy stone.* Mariam's mother saw how her daughter was changing. One day they visited their old neighbour. She told Mariam about a quarrel she had carried in her heart for many years. "Anger can become a heavy stone," the neighbour said. "When we forgive for Allah's sake, the stone gets lighter." Mariam thought about the stone she had carried since her picture was spoiled. She did not want to carry it forever.
14. *The kindness basket.* At school, Mariam had an idea: a kindness basket for the class. Anyone who made a mistake could write a note and put it inside. So could anyone who wanted to mend a friendship. Safa wrote the first note. It said, "I am grateful that Mariam gave me another chance." Mariam wrote one too. "A mistake can become a lesson when we own up to it." Bit by bit, the class saw that forgiving and owning up belong together.
15. *Does forgiving mean forgetting?.* When the story was finished, Grandpa Musa nodded slowly. "Forgiving can take time," he said. "It may start with hurt. Then it grows with honesty, patience and dua." Amina asked, "Does forgiving mean we forget everything?" "No," Grandpa Musa replied. "We remember the lesson, but we don't let anger rule our hearts." Mariam knew he was right.
16. Ayah 42:43: "And whoever is patient and forgives, that is one of the things that take real strength."
- **check:** Added: the book has no hadith or ayah for this story. This part of Muslim 2588 and Surah 42:43 were chosen.
17. Hadith: وَمَا زَادَ اللَّهُ عَبْدًا بِعَفْوٍ إِلَّا عِزًّا "Allah only raises in honour a person who forgives." (Muslim 2588)
18. Order: Safa's sleeve knocks the picture to the floor. → Mariam turns away from Safa. → Mariam makes dua for help to forgive. → Mariam and Safa draw a new garden together. → Mariam helps a younger pupil fix a torn model. → Safa writes the first note for the kindness basket.
19. Quiz: What did Mariam ask Safa to do next time? **Be careful and tell her straight away** · What did the old neighbour say anger can become? **A heavy stone** · What did Mariam say to Hamza about the broken pencil? **She knew he did not do it on purpose** · Does forgiving mean forgetting everything? **No, we remember the lesson**
20. Quiz: Your friend spills juice on your book and says sorry. What would Mariam do? **Forgive her and ask her to be careful** · You still feel angry with someone. What would Mariam do? **Ask Allah to help my heart** · A younger child breaks something by accident and cries. What would Mariam do? **Help them fix it**
21. Write: Write a note for a kindness basket. You could say sorry for a mistake, or forgive someone who said sorry to you.

### 7. Story seven: Layla, the brave heart

1. *What is courage?.* The children gathered under the olive tree, ready for another story. Grandpa Musa smiled and looked at Layla, who was sitting quietly beside the others. "Today I will tell you about a girl who found out what courage is," he said. "Being brave does not mean you feel no fear. It means doing what is right, and trusting Allah." Layla listened carefully. She used to think brave people never felt afraid. Soon she would find out the truth.
- **check:** The source book ends after two pages of this story. The first two sentences here come from the picture on page 273 (a flooded path and a small boy standing in the water), to join the two pages.
2. *Sami in the water.* One stormy day, rain poured down on the school. The path outside turned into a rushing stream. Little Sami was stuck in the water. He looked very scared. Layla felt scared too. But she took a deep breath and walked over to Sami. "Don't worry," she said softly. "I'll help you." Sami looked at her in surprise. Layla held out her hand, and together they walked carefully through the water. The other children came to help too. They made a line to help him across, and soon Sami was safe. The teacher smiled proudly. "This is the courage Allah gives us when we care for others," she said. The rain kept falling, but everyone felt warm inside.
3. Ayah 9:51: "Say: "Nothing will happen to us except what Allah has written for us. He is our Protector." And in Allah let the believers put their trust."
- **check:** Added: the book has no hadith or ayah for this story. Muslim 2664 and Surah 9:51 were chosen.
4. Hadith: الْمُؤْمِنُ الْقَوِيُّ خَيْرٌ وَأَحَبُّ إِلَى اللَّهِ مِنَ الْمُؤْمِنِ الضَّعِيفِ، وَفِي كُلٍّ خَيْرٌ "The strong believer is better and more beloved to Allah than the weak believer, and there is good in both." (Muslim 2664)
5. Quiz: What did Grandpa Musa say courage means? **Doing what is right and trusting Allah** · What did Layla do before she walked over to Sami? **She took a deep breath** · How did the other children help? **They made a line to help him across**
6. Order: Grandpa Musa says courage means doing right and trusting Allah. → The rain turns the path into a stream. → Layla takes a deep breath. → Layla holds out her hand to Sami. → The other children make a line to help. → Sami is safe.
7. Quiz: A friend is stuck somewhere and looks frightened. What would Layla do? **Shout for a grown-up and stay with my friend** · You feel nervous about reading in front of the class. What would Layla do? **Say Bismillah, take a deep breath and try** · Some children are teasing a younger child. What would Layla do? **Stand by the younger child and tell a teacher**
8. Write: Write about a time you felt scared but still did the right thing. Who did you trust to help you?

## Arabic for Beginners (Level 3, ages 8–10)

Unit One of the Arabic textbook: greetings, pronouns, this and that, the classroom, numbers, animals, colours, places, sun and moon letters, families and short stories, with the pages to write on and practice after each lesson.

### 1. Greetings

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/001.webp`: This is your Arabic book. Unit One starts with greetings: the words we say when we meet someone and when we leave.
2. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/002.webp`: Read each greeting and look at its picture. When would you say it? Colour the pictures that happen in the morning.
3. *Greetings and their replies.* Many Arabic greetings have their own reply. When someone says صَبَاحُ الْخَيْرِ, you answer صَبَاحُ النُّورِ. [table]
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/003.webp`: Two teachers meet at school. Listen to what they say, then say it with a friend. One of you starts with السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُمْ.
5. Match: صَبَاحُ الْخَيْرِ = Good morning · مَسَاءُ الْخَيْرِ = Good evening · مَعَ السَّلَامَةِ = Goodbye · تَشَرَّفْنَا = Pleased to meet you · كَيْفَ حَالُكَ؟ = How are you? · تُصْبِحُ عَلَى خَيْرٍ = Good night
6. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/004.webp`: Look at the six pictures. Write the letter of the right picture in the small box next to each greeting.
7. Quiz: It is 8 o'clock in the morning and you meet your teacher. What do you say? **صَبَاحُ الْخَيْرِ** · Your friend says السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُمْ. What do you answer? **وَعَلَيْكُمُ السَّلَامُ** · Someone asks you كَيْفَ حَالُكَ؟ Which is a good answer? **بِخَيْرٍ، الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ** · Your friend is going home after school. What do you say? **مَعَ السَّلَامَةِ** · You meet a new classmate for the first time. Which word means "pleased to meet you"? **تَشَرَّفْنَا** · It is bedtime. What do you say to your family? **تُصْبِحُ عَلَى خَيْرٍ**
8. Fill the gap: مَسَاءُ الْخَيْرِ. مَسَاءُ **النُّورِ**. · السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُمْ. وَعَلَيْكُمُ **السَّلَامُ**. · **كَيْفَ** حَالُكَ؟ بِخَيْرٍ، الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ. · إِلَى **اللِّقَاءِ**. · صَبَاحُ **الْخَيْرِ**.

### 2. Personal pronouns

1. *Words for people.* A pronoun is a short word we use in place of a name. Instead of saying أَحْمَدُ again and again, we say هُوَ (he). Arabic has more pronouns than English. English has one word, "you". Arabic says أَنْتَ to a boy and أَنْتِ to a girl. Look at the last letter: a fatha for a boy, a kasra for a girl. [table]
- **check:** The printed table labels the أَنْتَ row الْمُخَاطَبَةُ (it should be الْمُخَاطَبُ). The typed table on the page before is correct.
2. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/005.webp`: Look at the wheel. Which pronoun goes with each picture? In the box of examples at the bottom, write what each sentence means in English.
3. *He and she for things too.* In Arabic every thing is a boy word or a girl word. Most girl words end in ة. So a table, طَاوِلَةٌ, is هِيَ, and a television, تِلْفَازٌ, is هُوَ. That is why the wheel puts a table next to هِيَ and a television next to هُوَ. [table]
4. Match: أَنَا = I · نَحْنُ = we · هُوَ = he · هِيَ = she · أَنْتَ = you (a boy) · أَنْتِ = you (a girl) · هُمْ = they (a group)
5. Sort into Boy word (هُوَ) / Girl word (هِيَ): بَابٌ (Boy word (هُوَ)) · سَيَّارَةٌ (Girl word (هِيَ)) · قَلَمٌ (Boy word (هُوَ)) · طَاوِلَةٌ (Girl word (هِيَ)) · كِتَابٌ (Boy word (هُوَ)) · مَدْرَسَةٌ (Girl word (هِيَ)) · بَيْتٌ (Boy word (هُوَ)) · حَقِيبَةٌ (Girl word (هِيَ)) · تِلْفَازٌ (Boy word (هُوَ)) · سَاعَةٌ (Girl word (هِيَ))
6. Fill the gap: أَحْمَدُ طَالِبٌ. **هُوَ** فِي الْفَصْلِ. · زَيْنَبُ مُعَلِّمَةٌ. **هِيَ** فِي الْمَدْرَسَةِ. · يَا عَلِيُّ، مَنْ **أَنْتَ**؟ · يَا مَرْيَمُ، مَنْ **أَنْتِ**؟ · **هُمَا** عِيسَى وَمَحْمُودٌ.

### 3. Pronoun endings

- **check:** The top table mixes قَلَمُهُ, قَلَمُهَا, قَلَمُكَ and قَلَمُهُمْ into the إِيَّا column, and two of its examples (هُوَ أَحْمَدُ, هُمَا عِيسَى وَمَحْمُودٌ) are not إِيَّا sentences. The typed tables in this lesson give the forms in full.
1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/006.webp`: The top table shows pronouns that start with إِيَّا. The bottom table shows the short endings that join onto a word. Read both tables aloud.
2. *Endings for my, your, his and her.* Some pronouns are so short that they join onto the end of a word. بَيْتٌ is "a house". Add ـِي and it becomes بَيْتِي, "my house". When an ending joins on, the tanween (the double mark at the end) goes away. A word that ends in ة changes it to ت: حَقِيبَةٌ becomes حَقِيبَتِي. [table]
3. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/007.webp`: Here are the endings joined to بَيْتٌ and قَلَمٌ. Cover one column with your hand and try to say it from memory.
4. *Pronouns that start with إِيَّا.* When a pronoun is the one something is done to, Arabic can use a word that starts with إِيَّا. أَعْطَيْتُهُ إِيَّاهُ means "I gave it to him". [table]
5. Match: قَلَمِي = my pen · قَلَمُكَ = your pen (a boy) · قَلَمُكِ = your pen (a girl) · قَلَمُهُ = his pen · قَلَمُهَا = her pen · قَلَمُنَا = our pen
6. Fill the gap: هَذَا كِتَابُ أَحْمَدَ. هَذَا **كِتَابُهُ**. · هَذِهِ حَقِيبَةُ مَرْيَمَ. هَذِهِ **حَقِيبَتُهَا**. · يَا عَلِيُّ، أَيْنَ **قَلَمُكَ**؟ · يَا فَاطِمَةُ، أَيْنَ **قَلَمُكِ**؟

### 4. Furniture at home

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/008.webp`: Read the furniture words. Then read the four sentences in the box. Is each one about هُوَ or هِيَ?
- **check:** The sink is printed مَغْسَل; it is typed here as مَغْسَلَةٌ.
2. *Things in the home.* Some things have two names, written with a slash between them. أَرِيكَةٌ and كَنَبَةٌ are both a sofa. [table]
3. *Sentences about the home.* Notice the verbs. A verb about هِيَ often ends in ـَتْ or starts with تَـ. A verb about هُوَ often starts with يُـ or يَـ. [table]
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/010.webp`: Point to each picture and say its name in Arabic. Then colour the things you have in your own home.
5. Match: طَاوِلَةٌ = table · كُرْسِيٌّ = chair · سَرِيرٌ = bed · ثَلَّاجَةٌ = fridge · مِرْآةٌ = mirror · مِصْبَاحٌ = lamp · فُرْنٌ = oven · مِرْوَحَةٌ = fan
6. Quiz: Where do you keep the milk cold? **ثَلَّاجَةٌ** · What do you sleep on? **سَرِيرٌ** · What do you boil water in? **غَلَّايَةٌ** · Where do your books go? **مَكْتَبَةٌ** · What do you look in to comb your hair? **مِرْآةٌ**

### 5. This and that

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/009.webp`: These words point at people and things. Look at the bottom of the page. For a group of animals, objects or plants we use the girl word هَذِهِ, even when there are many.
2. *Near and far.* Close by, a boy word takes هَذَا and a girl word takes هَذِهِ. Far away, a boy word takes ذَلِكَ and a girl word takes تِلْكَ. [table] For a group of things or animals, Arabic uses هَذِهِ and تِلْكَ: هَذِهِ كُتُبٌ means "these are books". We keep هَؤُلَاءِ for people. [table]
3. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/011.webp`: Read the two tables, near and far. In the box of examples, write what each sentence means in English.
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/012.webp`: Read each question and answer. Circle هَذِهِ every time you see it. Who are the girls and women on this page?
5. Quiz: A woman is teaching at the board. مَنْ هَذِهِ؟ **هِيَ مُعَلِّمَةٌ** · A man leads the prayer in the mosque. أَهَذَا إِمَامٌ؟ **نَعَمْ، هَذَا إِمَامٌ** · A girl is carrying her school bag. أَهَذَا وَلَدٌ؟ **لَا، هَذِهِ طَالِبَةٌ** · A man is working in a field with a fork. مَنْ هَذَا؟ **هَذَا فَلَّاحٌ** · A nurse is smiling at you. مَنْ هَذِهِ؟ **هِيَ مُمَرِّضَةٌ**
6. Sort into هَذَا / هَذِهِ: وَلَدٌ (هَذَا) · طَالِبَةٌ (هَذِهِ) · مُعَلِّمٌ (هَذَا) · مُمَرِّضَةٌ (هَذِهِ) · طَبِيبٌ (هَذَا) · بِنْتٌ (هَذِهِ) · إِمَامٌ (هَذَا) · مُعَلِّمَةٌ (هَذِهِ) · مُهَنْدِسٌ (هَذَا) · سَيَّارَةٌ (هَذِهِ) · رَجُلٌ (هَذَا) · حَقِيبَةٌ (هَذِهِ)
7. Fill the gap: **هَذَا** قَلَمٌ. · **هَذِهِ** سَاعَةٌ. · **هَؤُلَاءِ** طُلَّابٌ. · **ذَلِكَ** نَجْمٌ بَعِيدٌ. · **تِلْكَ** طَائِرَةٌ بَعِيدَةٌ. · **هَذِهِ** كُتُبٌ.

### 6. A student at Al-Noor School

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/013.webp`: Meet Aisha and her friends. Use the words in the box at the top to answer each مَنْ question, like the example: مَنْ أَنَا؟ أَنْتِ عَائِشَةُ.
- **check:** Line 2 is printed وَهَذا صَدِيقَتِي and line 6 وَهَذا أَصْدِقَاؤُهَا. The typed version on the next page uses هَذِهِ and هَؤُلَاءِ.
2. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/014.webp`: Aisha tells us about her friend Rayhana. Read it aloud.
3. *Aisha and her friends.* أَنَا عَائِشَةُ، وَهَذِهِ صَدِيقَتِي. اسْمُهَا رَيْحَانَةُ، وَعُمُرُهَا اثْنَتَا عَشْرَةَ سَنَةً. هِيَ فِي مَدْرَسَةِ النُّورِ، وَهَؤُلَاءِ أَصْدِقَاؤُهَا. مِنَ الْيَمِينِ عَلِيٌّ، وَمِنَ الْيَسَارِ سُلَيْمَانُ. هُمَا فِي نَفْسِ الْفَصْلِ. Aisha points at her friend Rayhana, who is twelve. Rayhana goes to Al-Noor School. Ali stands on the right and Sulayman on the left. The two boys are in the same class. [table]
4. Quiz: How old is Rayhana? **١٢** · Which school do the friends go to? **مَدْرَسَةُ النُّورِ** · Who is standing on the right? **عَلِيٌّ** · Why does Aisha say صَدِيقَتِي and not صَدِيقِي? **Her friend is a girl.**
5. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/015.webp`: Choose the right name for each pronoun and write the whole sentence, like the two examples. Then answer the questions under the pictures.
6. Fill the gap: هَذِهِ **خَدِيجَةُ**. · هِيَ **مَرْيَمُ**. · هُوَ **طَالِبٌ**. · هُوَ **مُعَلِّمٌ**. · هَذَا **صَدِيقٌ**. · هَلْ هِيَ طَبِيبَةٌ؟ نَعَمْ، **هِيَ** طَبِيبَةٌ.
7. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/016.webp`: Aisha's teacher phones her home. Listen to the call, say it again, then read it with a partner.
8. Order: آلُو، مَرْحَبًا. → مَرْحَبًا، تَفَضَّلْ! → أَنَا مُعَلِّمُ عَائِشَةَ. → أَهْلًا يَا أُسْتَاذُ! → عَائِشَةُ غَائِبَةٌ الْيَوْمَ. هَلْ هِيَ فِي الْبَيْتِ؟ → هِيَ مَرِيضَةٌ، وَلِذَلِكَ ذَهَبَتْ إِلَى الْمُسْتَشْفَى. → اللهُ يَشْفِيهَا. → شُكْرًا.
9. Quiz: Why is Aisha not at school today? **She is ill.** · Where did Aisha go? **To the hospital** · What does اللهُ يَشْفِيهَا mean? **May Allah make her well.**

### 7. Things in the classroom

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/017.webp`: Read the labels in the classroom. Point to each thing and say its name.
2. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/018.webp`: Here are more classroom words. Colour the coloured pencils, the school bag and the map.
3. *Classroom words.* Most of these are boy words. The girl words end in ة, like سَبُّورَةٌ and نَافِذَةٌ. [table]
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/019.webp`: Write the Arabic name of each thing in the box with the same letter.
5. Match: مِقَصٌّ = scissors · مِمْحَاةٌ = eraser · مِسْطَرَةٌ = ruler · كِتَابٌ = book · دَفْتَرٌ = notebook · كُرْسِيٌّ = chair · نَافِذَةٌ = window · سَبُّورَةٌ = board

### 8. Numbers

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/020.webp`: Say each number aloud with your teacher, from ١ to ٥٠.
2. *Counting in Arabic.* Arabic has its own digits: the 2 we write in English is ٢ in Arabic. From 13 to 19 you say the small number first, then عَشَرَ. So ثَلَاثَةَ عَشَرَ is 13. For 25 you say "five and twenty": خَمْسَةٌ وَعِشْرُونَ. [table]
3. Match: ٣ = ثَلَاثَةٌ · ٧ = سَبْعَةٌ · ٩ = تِسْعَةٌ · ١٢ = اثْنَا عَشَرَ · ٢٠ = عِشْرُونَ · ٤٠ = أَرْبَعُونَ · ١٠٠ = مِئَةٌ
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/021.webp`: Write each number in Arabic words. Then finish the sentences about the things in your school bag.
5. Quiz: How do you write 53 in Arabic words? **ثَلَاثَةٌ وَخَمْسُونَ** · How do you write 17? **سَبْعَةَ عَشَرَ** · How do you write 100? **مِئَةٌ** · How do you write 20? **عِشْرُونَ** · Which number is خَمْسَةٌ وَأَرْبَعُونَ? **٤٥**

### 9. Talking in class

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/022.webp`: Read how the children ask for things politely. Then ask your partner for a pen, then a book.
2. *Polite words.* Say مِنْ فَضْلِكَ to a boy and مِنْ فَضْلِكِ to a girl. It is the same fatha and kasra you saw in أَنْتَ and أَنْتِ. [table]
3. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/023.webp`: These are things the teacher says in class. Act each one out when your teacher says it.
- **check:** Page 23 prints اغْلِقِ الْبَابَ without the hamza, and the second word for "stand up" looks like كُم. They are typed as أَغْلِقِ and قُمْ.
4. *What the teacher says.* When the teacher speaks to the whole class, the word gets ـُوا on the end. اِجْلِسْ is "sit down" to one child, and اِجْلِسُوا is "sit down" to everyone. [table]
5. Match: اِجْلِسْ = Sit down. · قُمْ = Stand up. · اُسْكُتْ = Be quiet. · اِفْتَحِ النَّافِذَةَ = Open the window. · أَغْلِقِ الْبَابَ = Close the door. · اُنْظُرْ إِلَى السَّبُّورَةِ = Look at the board.
6. Quiz: Your classmate says أَعْطِنِي الْمِقَصَّ، مِنْ فَضْلِكَ. You hand over the scissors. What do you say? **تَفَضَّلْ** · Now they say شُكْرًا. What do you answer? **عَفْوًا** · You did not hear the teacher. What can you say? **قُلْ ذَلِكَ مَرَّةً أُخْرَى، مِنْ فَضْلِكَ.** · The room is dark. What does the teacher ask? **أَشْعِلِ النُّورَ**

### 10. What is this?

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/024.webp`: Write the meaning of مَا and هَذَا in the table. Then read the sentence under each picture.
2. *Asking about things.* مَا means "what" and we use it for things. مَنْ means "who" and we use it for people. To ask a yes or no question, put أَ in front: أَهَذَا بَيْتٌ؟ means "Is this a house?" Answer with نَعَمْ (yes) or لَا (no). A plural of things takes هَذِهِ, even when the single word is a boy word: هَذَا بَابٌ, هَذِهِ أَبْوَابٌ. [table]
- **check:** Row 9 is printed هَذَا رَصَاصَةٌ / هَذِهِ رَصَاصَاتٌ, which means a bullet. The typed table uses قَلَمُ رَصَاصٍ (a pencil). كُرَاسٍ is typed as كَرَاسِيُّ.
3. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/025.webp`: Write the English meaning next to each pair. Notice that every plural uses هَذِهِ.
4. Match: بَيْتٌ = بُيُوتٌ · مَسْجِدٌ = مَسَاجِدُ · دَفْتَرٌ = دَفَاتِرُ · بَابٌ = أَبْوَابٌ · مِفْتَاحٌ = مَفَاتِيحُ · حَقِيبَةٌ = حَقَائِبُ · مَكْتَبٌ = مَكَاتِبُ
5. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/026.webp`: Answer the question next to each picture. If the guess is wrong, start your answer with لَا.
6. Quiz: You are looking at a hospital. أَهَذَا بَيْتٌ؟ **لَا، هَذَا مُسْتَشْفًى** · You are looking at a red notebook. أَهَذَا كُرْسِيٌّ؟ **لَا، هَذَا دَفْتَرٌ** · You are looking at a door. أَهَذَا بَيْتٌ؟ **لَا، هَذَا بَابٌ** · You are looking at a bed. أَهَذَا سَرِيرٌ؟ **نَعَمْ، هَذَا سَرِيرٌ** · You are looking at a school bag. مَا هَذِهِ؟ **هَذِهِ حَقِيبَةٌ**
7. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/027.webp`: Work with a friend. One of you asks مَا هَذَا؟ or أَهَذَا ...؟ about things in the room. The other answers with a whole sentence, starting with نَعَمْ or لَا.
8. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/028.webp`: Listen to your teacher. Write the words you hear, then write the sentences.
9. Write: Look around your room. Write three sentences in Arabic about things you can see, like هَذَا بَابٌ.

- **check:** The rooster is printed أ هذه ديك؟ نعم، هذه دجاجة. The children are told the right answer is أَهَذَا دِيكٌ؟ نَعَمْ، هَذَا دِيكٌ. The goat is printed مِعْزَى; this book uses مَاعِزٌ, as on page 51.

2. *Animal words.* Animals are boy words or girl words too. Most of them are boy words, but نَمْلَةٌ, قِطَّةٌ, بَطَّةٌ and دَجَاجَةٌ are girl words. [table]

Sort into Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ) / Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ): كَلْبٌ (Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ)) · أَسَدٌ (Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ)) · قِطَّةٌ (Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ)) · فِيلٌ (Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ)) · خَرُوفٌ (Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ)) · قِرْدٌ (Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ)) · بَقَرَةٌ (Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ)) · نَمِرٌ (Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ)) · حِصَانٌ (Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ)) · دُبٌّ (Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ)) · دَجَاجَةٌ (Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ)) · تِمْسَاحٌ (Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ)) · جَمَلٌ (Lives with people (مَنْزِلِيٌّ)) · ثُعْبَانٌ (Lives in the wild (بَرِّيٌّ))
6. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/034.webp`: Talk with a friend. Say what the animal is, then say whether it lives with people or in the wild, like the two examples.

### 13. Colours

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/036.webp`: Read the colours. Colour a small patch next to each name with your crayons.
- **check:** Turquoise is printed تُركواز; it is typed as تُرْكُوَازِيٌّ so it matches the other colour words.
2. *Colours for boy words and girl words.* A colour changes when it describes a girl word. The main colours have a special girl form, like أَحْمَرُ and حَمْرَاءُ. Colours that end in ـِيٌّ just add ة. The colour comes after the thing: قَمِيصٌ أَحْمَرُ is "a red shirt", and حَقِيبَةٌ خَضْرَاءُ is "a green bag". [table] [table]
3. Match: أَحْمَرُ = red · أَزْرَقُ = blue · أَخْضَرُ = green · أَصْفَرُ = yellow · أَسْوَدُ = black · أَبْيَضُ = white · بُرْتُقَالِيٌّ = orange · وَرْدِيٌّ = pink
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/040.webp`: Write the name of each thing and its colour, like طَائِرٌ أَصْفَرُ.
5. Fill the gap: طَائِرٌ **أَصْفَرُ** · قَمِيصٌ **أَحْمَرُ** · حَقِيبَةٌ **خَضْرَاءُ** · فِيلٌ **رَمَادِيٌّ** · قُبَّعَةٌ **زَرْقَاءُ** · سَيَّارَةٌ **بَيْضَاءُ**

Quiz: Which colour does the son want for the sitting room? **Blue** · Which colour does the daughter like? **Dark pink** · Which colours does the mother like? **Calm colours, like light green** · Why is the paint good for the mother, who wipes the walls every month? **It can be washed and has no smell.**

### 14. Places in town

1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/037.webp`: Read the names of the places. Which ones do you pass on the way to school?
2. *Places.* [table]
3. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/038.webp`: These words tell us where something is. Look at where the ball is next to each box.
4. *Where is it?.* Look carefully at two words that look alike. شِمَالٌ with a kasra means "left", and شَمَالٌ with a fatha means "north". [table]
5. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/039.webp`: Look at the town. Write sentences about where things are, using words like أَمَامَ and بِجَانِبِ.
6. Match: الْمَسْجِدُ = the mosque · الْمُسْتَشْفَى = the hospital · الْمَطَارُ = the airport · السُّوقُ = the market · الْمَكْتَبَةُ = the library · الْجِسْرُ = the bridge · الْمَطْعَمُ = the restaurant
7. Match: فَوْقَ = تَحْتَ · أَمَامَ = خَلْفَ · دَاخِلَ = خَارِجَ · يَمِينٌ = يَسَارٌ · قَبْلَ = بَعْدَ · شَرْقٌ = غَرْبٌ
8. Quiz: Where do you go to buy medicine? **الصَّيْدَلِيَّةُ** · Where do planes land? **الْمَطَارُ** · Where do we pray Jumu'ah? **الْمَسْجِدُ** · Where can you borrow books? **الْمَكْتَبَةُ** · The ball is under the box. Which word means "under"? **تَحْتَ** · Which word means "north"? **شَمَالٌ**

### 15. Sun and moon letters

1. *Can you hear the lam?.* الـ means "the". With some letters you can hear the ل clearly: الْقَمَرُ is said "al-qamar". These are the moon letters, because قَمَرٌ means "moon". With the other letters the ل goes silent and the next letter is said twice, with a shadda: الشَّمْسُ is said "ash-shams". These are the sun letters, because شَمْسٌ means "sun". [table] Look at the letter after الـ. If it has a shadda, it is a sun letter. If the ل has a sukun, it is a moon letter.
2. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/042.webp`: Read the two lists of letters. In the empty boxes, write how each example word sounds.

Sort into Moon letter / Sun letter: الشَّمْسُ (Sun letter) · الْقَلَمُ (Moon letter) · السَّمَاءُ (Sun letter) · الْمَكْتَبُ (Moon letter) · التُّفَّاحَةُ (Sun letter) · الْكِتَابُ (Moon letter) · الضُّيُوفُ (Sun letter) · الْمَنْزِلُ (Moon letter) · الرَّجُلُ (Sun letter) · الْعَالَمُ (Moon letter) · النُّورُ (Sun letter) · الْبَابُ (Moon letter)

### 16. My family and me

- **check:** Pages 63 to 65 (the family words, a story about a boy's family and a family exercise) have broken Arabic letters from the picture maker, for example ليتر and عاية أجمد, and some labels sit under the wrong person. They are not shown. The family words are typed here instead.
1. *Family words.* Arabic has two words for "uncle" and two for "aunt". عَمٌّ and عَمَّةٌ are your father's brother and sister. خَالٌ and خَالَةٌ are your mother's brother and sister. [table]
2. Match: أَبٌ = father · أُمٌّ = mother · أَخٌ = brother · أُخْتٌ = sister · جَدٌّ = grandfather · جَدَّةٌ = grandmother · عَمٌّ = uncle (father's brother) · خَالَةٌ = aunt (mother's sister)
3. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/045.webp`: Read about Fatima and her family. Then fill in the table at the bottom: who has which job?
4. *Fatima and her family.* اسْمِي فَاطِمَةُ. لَدَيَّ أَخَوَانِ وَأُخْتٌ صَغِيرَةٌ. اسْمُ أَبِي عَبْدُ اللهِ، وَاسْمُ أُمِّي أَمِينَةُ. أَبِي طَبِيبٌ، وَأُمِّي مُعَلِّمَةٌ. اسْمُ أَخِي الْكَبِيرِ مُحَمَّدٌ، وَاسْمُ أَخِي الصَّغِيرِ جَمَالٌ. أَخِي مُحَمَّدٌ مُهَنْدِسٌ. نَحْنُ نَعِيشُ فِي سَانْت جُونْز فِي بَيْتٍ كَبِيرٍ. أَنَا طَالِبَةٌ. أُحِبُّ عَائِلَتِي. [table]
5. Quiz: What is Fatima's father's job? **طَبِيبٌ** · What is her mother's name? **أَمِينَةُ** · Who is the engineer? **مُحَمَّدٌ** · How many brothers does Fatima have? **Two** · What does أُحِبُّ عَائِلَتِي mean? **I love my family.**
- **check:** The first person's name is hard to read in the Arabic (it looks like زُهَاء), while the English says Zuhair. The page is shown as printed.

8. *Who is Muhammad?.* اسْمِي مُحَمَّدٌ، أَنَا مِنْ بَنْغْلَادِيش. عُمُرِي أَحَدَ عَشَرَ عَامًا. أَنَا فِي الصَّفِّ السَّادِسِ فِي مَدْرَسَةِ النُّورِ. مَادَّتِي الْمُفَضَّلَةُ هِيَ الرِّيَاضِيَّاتُ. لَدَيَّ شَقِيقَانِ. اسْمُ أُخْتِي الصَّغِيرَةِ جَمِيلَةُ، وَاسْمُ أَخِي الْكَبِيرِ حَافِظٌ. كِلَاهُمَا طَالِبٌ. هِوَايَتِي تِنِسُ الطَّاوِلَةِ وَالسَّفَرُ. أُحِبُّ قَضَاءَ الْوَقْتِ مَعَ وَالِدَيَّ. أَنَا أَتَعَلَّمُ اللُّغَةَ الْعَرَبِيَّةَ. أَقْرَأُ الْكُتُبَ الْعَرَبِيَّةَ جَيِّدًا. أَسْكُنُ فِي مَدِينَةِ سَانْت جُونْز. [table]

### 19. The and a; describing words

- **check:** The sentence under the gate is printed الْبَابُ الْمَزْرَعَةِ مَفْتُوحٌ; it is typed as بَابُ الْمَزْرَعَةِ مَفْتُوحٌ. The new words غَلْقٌ / مَغْلُوقٌ and فَتْحٌ are typed as أَغْلَقَ / مُغْلَقٌ and فَتَحَ.
1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/053.webp`: Write the English meaning next to each pair: الْكِتَابُ is "the book" and كِتَابٌ is "a book".
2. *The and a.* الـ at the start of a word means "the". The double mark at the end (tanween) means "a". A word has one or the other, never both. [table] A doing word can also become a describing word. جَلَسَ الْوَلَدُ means "the boy sat down", and الْوَلَدُ جَالِسٌ means "the boy is sitting". [table] [table]
3. Sort into the (مَعْرِفَةٌ) / a (نَكِرَةٌ): الْكِتَابُ (the (مَعْرِفَةٌ)) · كِتَابٌ (a (نَكِرَةٌ)) · مَزْرَعَةٌ (a (نَكِرَةٌ)) · الْخَرُوفُ (the (مَعْرِفَةٌ)) · مَاعِزٌ (a (نَكِرَةٌ)) · الْبَابُ (the (مَعْرِفَةٌ)) · قَلَمٌ (a (نَكِرَةٌ)) · الشَّمْسُ (the (مَعْرِفَةٌ))
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/054.webp`: Fill each gap with a word from the box. Then write two sentences of your own with وَقَفَ / وَاقِفٌ and two with جَلَسَ / جَالِسٌ.
5. Fill the gap: الطَّالِبُ **جَالِسٌ** عَلَى الْكُرْسِيِّ. · الْمُعَلِّمُ **وَاقِفٌ** فِي الْفَصْلِ. · الْبَابُ **مَفْتُوحٌ** لِلدُّخُولِ. · الشُّبَّاكُ **مُغْلَقٌ** مِنَ الرِّيحِ. · الطِّفْلُ **نَائِمٌ** عَلَى السَّرِيرِ.
6. *Describing words.* Describing words come in pairs of opposites. For a girl word, add ة: كِتَابٌ جَدِيدٌ, but حَقِيبَةٌ جَدِيدَةٌ. [table] Two more words from the box: نَظِيفٌ means "clean" and جَمِيلٌ means "beautiful".
7. Match: جَدِيدٌ = قَدِيمٌ · طَوِيلٌ = قَصِيرٌ · كَبِيرٌ = صَغِيرٌ · ثَقِيلٌ = خَفِيفٌ · قَرِيبٌ = بَعِيدٌ
