### 20. Belonging and place words

- **check:** The Arabic explanation calls the second noun الْمُضَاف; it is usually called الْمُضَافُ إِلَيْهِ. The example الْبَيْتُ عَلِيٍّ is typed as بَيْتُ عَلِيٍّ (no الـ on the first word).
1. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/056.webp`: This page explains two things: how one noun can belong to another, and the small words like فِي and عَلَى.
2. *Whose is it?.* Put two nouns side by side to show that something belongs to someone. كِتَابُ مُحَمَّدٍ means "Muhammad's book". The first word loses its الـ and its tanween. The second word ends with a kasra. Some names, like أَحْمَدُ and فَاطِمَةُ, take a fatha instead. [table]
3. *Little place words.* After these little words, the noun ends with a kasra: الْبَيْتُ becomes فِي الْبَيْتِ. [table]
4. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/057.webp`: Read the table, then ask and answer the أَيْنَ questions with a partner.
5. Quiz: أَيْنَ السَّاعَةُ؟ **هِيَ عَلَى الْجِدَارِ.** · أَيْنَ الْقِطُّ؟ **هُوَ عِنْدَ الْبَابِ.** · أَيْنَ الْحَقِيبَةُ؟ **هِيَ عَلَى الْمَكْتَبِ.** · أَيْنَ خَدِيجَةُ؟ **هِيَ فِي الْمَطْبَخِ.**
6. Workbook page `books/arabic-beginners/058.webp`: Look at each picture and make a sentence with فِي, عَلَى or عِنْدَ. For example: الْقِطُّ فِي الصُّنْدُوقِ.
7. Fill the gap: الْكِتَابُ **عَلَى** الطَّاوِلَةِ. · عَمَّارٌ **مَعَ** حَامِدٍ. · أَنَا **فِي** الْبَيْتِ. · ذَهَبَ مُحَمَّدٌ **إِلَى** الْمَدْرَسَةِ. · السَّاعَةُ **عَلَى** الْجِدَارِ. · أَيْنَ خَدِيجَةُ؟ هِيَ **عِنْدَ** الْبَابِ.

### 23. Food and drink

- **check:** Pages 66 to 68 (Food and Drink, In the kitchen, and the food exercise) were made with a picture maker that broke the Arabic letters, for example جبنز under the bread and موز under the milk. They are not shown. The food words are typed here.
1. *Food words.* Some food words are about "some" of a thing, not one. مَوْزٌ means bananas in general, and one banana is مَوْزَةٌ. [table]
2. Match: خُبْزٌ = bread · أَرُزٌّ = rice · لَحْمٌ = meat · سَمَكٌ = fish · حَلِيبٌ = milk · مَاءٌ = water · بَيْضٌ = eggs · مَوْزٌ = bananas
- **check:** This short kitchen story is retold from page 67, whose letters are broken. Please check that it matches what the teacher meant the page to say.
3. *In the kitchen.* أُمِّي تَطْبُخُ الطَّعَامَ كُلَّ يَوْمٍ. الْيَوْمَ طَبَخَتْ أَرُزًّا، وَنَحْنُ نُحِبُّ الْأَرُزَّ. أُخْتِي تُحِبُّ الْفَوَاكِهَ. هِيَ تَأْكُلُ التُّفَّاحَ وَالْمَوْزَ كُلَّ يَوْمٍ. نَشْرَبُ الْحَلِيبَ فِي الصَّبَاحِ. Mum cooks every day. Today she made rice, which the whole family loves. The sister eats apples and bananas every day, and everyone drinks milk in the morning.
4. Quiz: مَاذَا طَبَخَتِ الْأُمُّ الْيَوْمَ؟ **أَرُزًّا** · مَاذَا تَأْكُلُ الْأُخْتُ كُلَّ يَوْمٍ؟ **التُّفَّاحَ وَالْمَوْزَ** · Which one is a drink? **حَلِيبٌ** · What does نَشْرَبُ mean? **we drink**
5. Write: Write two sentences in Arabic about food you like. Start each one with أُحِبُّ.

## The Book of Hadith (Level 3, ages 9–11)

Forty-four hadith on family, friends, worship, character and knowledge, each with its words, a story and things to do.

### 1. Parents 1: Obedience to parents

1. *Relationship with parents, family and relatives.* The first part of this book is about the people closest to you. That means your parents and your brothers and sisters, and also your grandparents, aunts, uncles and cousins. Islam asks a lot of us here. Allah tells us to be good to our parents straight after He tells us to worship Him alone. In each chapter you will learn one hadith and its words. Then you will read a story and try the activities.
2. Hadith: عَنِ الْمُغِيرَةِ بْنِ شُعْبَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: إِنَّ اللَّهَ حَرَّمَ عَلَيْكُمْ عُقُوقَ الْأُمَّهَاتِ "Allah has forbidden you to be undutiful to your mothers." (Bukhari 5975, Muslim 593)
3. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Being undutiful means hurting your parents, ignoring them, or refusing to do the good things they ask. Allah has made this haram. The hadith names mothers because they carry so much for us. Other hadith show the same is true for fathers.
4. *Hamna's lesson about obedience.* It was Friday, and Jumu'ah was getting close. Hamna's parents had told her at least ten times to get ready. Her older brother Waleed had already had his ghusl. He was waiting at the door with Baba and Ummi. The night before, Baba had told them that Friday is a special day for making du'a. Waleed could not wait to ask Allah for things. Hamna had other plans. She kept finding excuses, because she wanted to stay home with her tablet and her games. The masjid was close by, and Hamna still was not ready. So Baba left her at home. Hamna grinned. A whole hour to herself!
5. *A cracked screen.* Things went wrong fast. Hamna tried to sign up for Minecraft on a website, and it turned out to be a scam. She was so cross that she stamped her foot, right onto her tablet. The screen cracked. At the masjid, Waleed made du'a for his parents, asking Allah to give them everything good. Baba was praying next to him and heard every word. When they got home, Hamna ran to Baba with fake tears. "My tablet broke! I need a new one!" Baba spoke gently. "Hamna, you missed Jumu'ah because you did not listen to Ummi and me. Crying will not fix that. Think about how to put things right." Waleed had an idea. "Why don't you make du'a, like I did at the masjid?"
6. *Two surprises.* That evening Hamna sat quietly in her room. She asked Allah for a new tablet. She also asked Him to help her listen to her parents, like her brother did. At breakfast, Baba gave Waleed a new football. "This is for you, because you were kind and respectful." Hamna felt a little sad, but she remembered her du'a. Then Ummi came in, smiling, with a new tablet. "This is for you," she said. "Look after it, and listen to us." Hamna hugged her. "Thank you, Ummi! I will try. I really will."
7. Quiz: Why did Baba leave Hamna at home? **She kept making excuses and was not ready for Jumu'ah** · What did Waleed ask Allah for at the masjid? **Good things for his parents** · What did Waleed tell Hamna to do? **Make du'a to Allah** · Besides a tablet, what else did Hamna ask Allah for? **To help her obey her parents**
8. Quiz: Abdullah ibn Mas'ud asked the Prophet ﷺ which deed Allah loves most. What came first? **Prayer on time** · What came second? **Being good to your parents**
9. Sort into Obedient / Undutiful: Saying "yes" straight away when Ummi asks for help (Obedient) · Raising my voice at my parents (Undutiful) · Putting my phone down when Baba talks to me (Obedient) · Getting up when they wake me for Fajr (Obedient) · Complaining every time they say "no" (Undutiful) · Saying sorry when I am wrong (Obedient) · Pretending I did not hear them (Undutiful) · Doing my chores without being asked (Obedient)
10. Write: For one whole day, do what your parents ask (in anything halal) without complaining or delaying. Write down what they asked and what you did. How did they react? How did it make you feel?

### 2. Parents 2: Adhering to your father

- **check:** The printed book has "طاعة الله طاعة الوالد ومعصية الله معصية الوالد" from Abu Hurayrah (al-Tabarani). That narration is weak, so this page gives the sound hadith of Tirmidhi 1899, which has the same meaning.
1. Hadith: عَنْ عَبْدِ اللَّهِ بْنِ عَمْرٍو قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: رِضَى الرَّبِّ فِي رِضَى الْوَالِدِ، وَسَخَطُ الرَّبِّ فِي سَخَطِ الْوَالِدِ "The pleasure of the Lord is in the pleasure of the father, and the anger of the Lord is in the anger of the father." (Tirmidhi 1899)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] When you please your father in good things, Allah is pleased with you. When you make him angry by disobeying him, you have also angered Allah.
3. *Amir and his father's fields.* Amir lived in a village among green hills. His father was a farmer, and their small farmhouse stood at the edge of a big field. Every morning at sunrise his father woke him. "Up you get, my son. There is a lot of work today." Amir dressed quickly and followed him out. He dug the soil, planted the seeds and watered the crops, doing each job the way his father showed him. His father did not say much. When Amir got something wrong, he took his hands and showed him again, slowly. "We work for our family and for our neighbours too," he would say.
4. *A hot day.* One summer day the sun was blazing. A fence had broken, and Amir's father went to mend it. "Water the crops for me," he told Amir. "They are thirsty." Amir was hot and tired. He went anyway, without a word of complaint, and carried water to every row. He knew his father's advice came from the teachings of Islam. Doing what his father asked was a way of pleasing Allah too. Amir grew up into a young man. He still went to his father for advice, and he still listened. He had seen how much his parents gave up to raise him.
5. Quiz: What work did Amir's father do? **He was a farmer** · What did Amir do on the hot day? **He watered the crops without complaining** · Why did Amir see obeying his father as worship? **Pleasing his father in good things pleases Allah**
6. Fill the gap: The pleasure of the Lord is in the pleasure of the **father**. · The Arabic word for "anger" in the hadith is **سَخَطُ**. · When my father corrects me, I should feel **thankful** that he cares. · We obey our parents in everything that is **halal**.
7. Write: Ask your father (or another grown-up in your family) these questions and write his answers. What was the hardest thing you went through as a young man? What do you wish you had known at my age? What makes you proud of me?

### 3. Parents 3: Keeping family ties

1. Hadith: عَنْ جُبَيْرِ بْنِ مُطْعِمٍ أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ ﷺ قَالَ: لَا يَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ قَاطِعٌ "The one who cuts off his family ties will not enter Paradise." (Bukhari 5984, Muslim 2556)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Your relatives have a right over you. Visiting them, calling them and helping them is called silat ar-rahim, keeping the ties of the womb. The Prophet ﷺ also said: "Whoever wishes for more provision and a longer life, let him keep his family ties." (Bukhari 5986, Muslim 2557)
3. *The Malik family.* The Malik family lived in a small flat in a big, busy city. Fatima and her husband Hasan had three children: Zaid, Leila and little Aisha. Zaid was the eldest and loved his books. Leila was kind and always tried to make people happy. Aisha laughed all day and was full of mischief. Fatima often told her children about this hadith. "The Prophet warned that someone who cuts off his family will not enter Paradise," she said. "So we hold on to each other." Of course they argued sometimes. Someone took the last biscuit. Someone said something unkind. When that happened, Fatima sat them all down together. Each person said what was wrong, and each one said sorry.
4. *Growing up together.* "Our family is like a boat," Fatima liked to say. "We are all in it together. Don't let a silly quarrel sink it." The years went by. When Zaid married and had worries of his own, he came to his parents for advice. When Leila was sad, her brother and sister stayed by her side. Aisha grew up too, and she never lost her laugh. She knew her family would always be there. They visited their grandparents and phoned their aunts and cousins. They hoped Allah would bring them together again in Paradise.
5. Quiz: What did Fatima do when her children argued? **Sat them down to talk and make up** · What did Fatima compare the family to? **A boat** · Who did Zaid go to for advice when he was grown up? **His parents**
6. Sort into Keeping family ties / Cutting family ties: Calling my grandmother every week (Keeping family ties) · Not speaking to a cousin for months after a fight (Cutting family ties) · Visiting my aunt when she is ill (Keeping family ties) · Making excuses every time we visit relatives (Cutting family ties) · Sending Eid greetings to my uncles (Keeping family ties) · Saying salam to an uncle I find difficult (Keeping family ties) · Telling others bad things about my relatives (Cutting family ties)
7. Quiz: The Prophet ﷺ said two things grow when you keep family ties. What are they? **Your provision and your life** · A relative is hard to get along with. What would the Prophet ﷺ advise? **Stay kind and keep in touch anyway**
8. Write: Which relatives have you not spoken to for more than a month? Choose two. Write how you will get back in touch with each one, and by when.

### 4. Parents 4: Pleasing your parents

1. Hadith: عَنْ عَبْدِ اللَّهِ بْنِ عَمْرٍو قَالَ: أَتَى رَجُلٌ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ ﷺ فَقَالَ: يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ، إِنِّي جِئْتُ أُبَايِعُكَ عَلَى الْهِجْرَةِ، وَتَرَكْتُ أَبَوَيَّ يَبْكِيَانِ. قَالَ: ارْجِعْ إِلَيْهِمَا فَأَضْحِكْهُمَا كَمَا أَبْكَيْتَهُمَا "A man came to the Messenger of Allah and said: 'O Messenger of Allah, I have come to pledge to you that I will make Hijrah, and I left my parents crying.' He said: 'Go back to them and make them laugh as you made them cry.'" (Abu Dawud 2528, Nasa'i 4163)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Hijrah was a great deed. Even so, the Prophet ﷺ sent the man home, because his parents were sad. Making them happy came first.
3. *Abdullah ibn Mas'ud's question.* Abdullah ibn Mas'ud was one of the first people to accept Islam. He loved learning from the Prophet ﷺ, and he became a great teacher of the Qur'an. One day he asked the Prophet ﷺ: "Which deed does Allah love most?" The Prophet ﷺ answered: "Prayer at its proper time." "Then what?" Abdullah asked. "Being good to your parents." "Then what?" "Striving in the path of Allah." Think about that order. Being good to your parents comes straight after the prayer. Helping your mother and smiling at your father are deeds that Allah loves very much. (Bukhari 527, Muslim 85)
4. Quiz: Your mother looks worn out after cooking dinner. She has not asked for help. **Clear the table and wash up without being asked** · Your father is worried about money. You want a new phone, but yours still works. **Keep my phone and not ask for now** · Your parents want to visit Grandma this weekend. Your friends invited you out. **Go to Grandma's and see my friends another day** · Your parent corrects you in front of your friends. You feel embarrassed. **Stay calm, say "OK", and talk to them later in private** · Your mother asks you to stop your game and help tidy up. You are in the middle of a match. **Pause the game and go to help**
5. Fill the gap: The man came to make a pledge to do **Hijrah**. · He had left his parents **crying**. · The Prophet ﷺ told him to go back and make them **laugh**. · The deed Allah loves second, after prayer on time, is being good to your **parents**.
6. Write: Pick one of the "What would you do?" situations, or one like it, and really do it this week. Write what you did and how your parent reacted.

### 5. Parents 5: Treating your parents well

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: جَاءَ رَجُلٌ إِلَى رَسُولِ اللَّهِ ﷺ فَقَالَ: يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ، مَنْ أَحَقُّ النَّاسِ بِحُسْنِ صَحَابَتِي؟ قَالَ: أُمُّكَ. قَالَ: ثُمَّ مَنْ؟ قَالَ: أُمُّكَ. قَالَ: ثُمَّ مَنْ؟ قَالَ: أُمُّكَ. قَالَ: ثُمَّ مَنْ؟ قَالَ: ثُمَّ أَبُوكَ "A man came to the Messenger of Allah and said: 'O Messenger of Allah, who has the most right to my good company?' He said: 'Your mother.' The man said: 'Then who?' He said: 'Your mother.' 'Then who?' 'Your mother.' 'Then who?' 'Then your father.'" (Bukhari 5971, Muslim 2548)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Your mother is named three times before your father. She carried you, fed you and stayed up with you at night. Your father comes next, and both deserve your very best.
3. *The man who waited with the milk.* The Prophet ﷺ told his Companions about three men who lived long ago. They were travelling when it began to rain, so they went into a cave in the mountain. A huge rock fell and blocked the way out. They could not move it. So they agreed that each one would call on Allah by a good deed he had done only for Allah. The first man had two old parents. Every evening he brought them milk from his animals, and he never gave any to his own children before them.
4. *Waiting until dawn.* One day he went far to find grazing and came back late. By the time he had milked the animals, his parents were asleep. He did not want to wake them. He also did not want his children to drink before them, even though the children were crying from hunger. So he stood there with the bowl in his hands, all night, until dawn. Then his parents woke up and drank. "O Allah," he said, "if I did that only for You, open this rock for us." The rock moved a little. Then the other two men called on Allah by their good deeds, and the rock moved away until they could walk out. (Bukhari 2272, Muslim 2743)
5. Quiz: Obeying parents is only needed until you turn 18. **False** · Paradise is at the feet of the mother. **True** · You must obey parents even if they tell you to disobey Allah. **False** · The Prophet ﷺ named the mother three times before the father. **True** · It is fine to raise your voice at your parents if they are wrong. **False** · Treating parents well only counts if they are Muslim. **False** · Saying "uff" to parents is forbidden. **True** · You can still be good to your parents after they die. **True** · The Prophet ﷺ named being good to parents before striving in the path of Allah. **True** · If your parents are unfair, you do not need to respect them. **False**
6. Order: Three men shelter in a cave → A rock blocks the way out → They agree to call on Allah by their good deeds → The first man tells how he waited all night with the milk → The rock moves a little → The rock moves away and they walk out
7. Write: Think of one thing your mother does for you every day that nobody thanks her for. Write it down, then write how you will thank her this week.

### 6. Parents 6: Prayer for those who treat their parents well

- **check:** The printed book has "من بر والديه طوبى له، زاد الله في عمره" from Sahl ibn Mu'adh from his father. That narration is weak, so this page gives the sound hadith of Uwais al-Qarani (Muslim 2542), which goes with the chapter's story.
1. Hadith: عَنْ عُمَرَ بْنِ الْخَطَّابِ قَالَ: سَمِعْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ ﷺ يَقُولُ: إِنَّ خَيْرَ التَّابِعِينَ رَجُلٌ يُقَالُ لَهُ أُوَيْسٌ، وَلَهُ وَالِدَةٌ، وَكَانَ بِهِ بَيَاضٌ، فَمُرُوهُ فَلْيَسْتَغْفِرْ لَكُمْ "The best of the Tabi'in is a man called Uwais. He has a mother, and he had white patches on his skin. Ask him to pray for forgiveness for you." (Muslim 2542)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Uwais was not a Companion, but the Prophet ﷺ praised him. In another narration he gave the reason: Uwais was very good to his mother, and Allah answered him.
- **check:** Told as Muslim 2542 tells it. The book's reasons why Uwais stayed in Yemen and its dialogue between Umar and Uwais were left out.
3. *Uwais al-Qarani.* Uwais lived in Yemen in the time of the Prophet ﷺ, but he never met him. He lived with his mother and was very good to her. The Prophet ﷺ told Umar about him. Uwais would come with the helpers from Yemen. He had a skin illness that Allah had cured, except for one small spot. "He has a mother, and he is very good to her," said the Prophet ﷺ. "If he swore by Allah, Allah would make it come true. If you can, ask him to pray for forgiveness for you."
4. *Umar finds Uwais.* Years later Umar was the khalifah. Whenever groups of helpers came from Yemen, he asked them: "Is Uwais ibn Amir with you?" At last he found him. Umar asked him about his tribe, his skin and his mother. Every answer matched what the Prophet ﷺ had said. Then Umar, the leader of all the Muslims, asked this quiet man from Yemen to pray for his forgiveness. Uwais did. Umar offered to write to the governor of Kufa for him. Uwais said he would rather live among ordinary poor people. (Muslim 2542)
5. Quiz: Where did Uwais come from? **Yemen** · Why did the Prophet ﷺ praise Uwais? **He was very good to his mother** · What did Umar ask Uwais to do? **Pray for Allah to forgive him** · Did Uwais meet the Prophet ﷺ? **No, he lived in his time but never met him**
6. Ayah 17:24: "And lower to them the wing of humility out of mercy, and say: "My Lord, have mercy on them as they brought me up when I was small.""
7. Fill the gap: Allah tells us to lower to our parents the wing of **humility**. · We say: "My Lord, have **mercy** on them." · "...as they brought me up when I was **small**."
8. Write: Write your own du'a for your mother and one for your father. Ask Allah to bless them, protect them and forgive them. Then choose a prayer after which you will say it every day this month.

### 7. Parents 7: Mercy for the young, respect for the old

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسِ بْنِ مَالِكٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَيْسَ مِنَّا مَنْ لَمْ يَرْحَمْ صَغِيرَنَا وَيُوَقِّرْ كَبِيرَنَا "He is not one of us who does not show mercy to our young ones and respect to our elders." (Tirmidhi 1919)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] A Muslim is gentle with children and honours older people. Someone who does neither is not following the way of the Prophet ﷺ.
3. *The Prophet and his grandson.* Once the Prophet ﷺ kissed his grandson al-Hasan ibn Ali. A man called al-Aqra' ibn Habis was sitting with him. Al-Aqra' said: "I have ten children, and I have never kissed any of them." The Prophet ﷺ looked at him and said: "Whoever does not show mercy will not be shown mercy." (Bukhari 5997, Muslim 2318) Once, while the Prophet ﷺ was in sujud, one of his grandsons climbed onto his back. He stayed down longer so as not to rush the child. (Nasa'i 1141) When he led the prayer and heard a baby crying, he made the prayer shorter so the mother would not worry. (Bukhari 707)
4. *Let the older one speak.* He taught respect for elders too. Once some men came to speak to the Prophet ﷺ about a problem, and the youngest began to talk first. The Prophet ﷺ said: "Let the older one speak first." (Bukhari 6142) So the young man waited, and the older men spoke. Being strong does not mean being harsh. We are gentle with little ones, and we listen to older people and help them.
5. Sort into Mercy to the young / Respect to the old: Helping a little child tie his shoes (Mercy to the young) · Giving my seat on the bus to an old man (Respect to the old) · Letting my grandfather speak first (Respect to the old) · Not shouting when a 5-year-old spills juice on my homework (Mercy to the young) · Walking patiently behind an old lady who is slow (Respect to the old) · Playing a game with my little sister (Mercy to the young) · Listening to Grandpa's story, even the tenth time (Respect to the old) · Sharing my sweets with a younger cousin (Mercy to the young)
6. Quiz: Your little brother keeps bothering you while you study. **Kindly give him something to do, or play for five minutes** · Your grandfather starts a story you have heard many times. **Listen and smile**
7. Write: Which is harder for you: being merciful to younger people, or respectful to older people? Why? Then write one thing you will do this week for someone younger, and one for someone older.

### 8. Parents 8: Good manners

- **check:** The printed book has "ما نحل والد ولدا من نحل أفضل من أدب حسن" (Tirmidhi 1952). Tirmidhi calls it mursal and it is weak, so this page gives the sound hadith that the book quotes in this chapter's activity (Tirmidhi 2002).
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي الدَّرْدَاءِ أَنَّ النَّبِيَّ ﷺ قَالَ: مَا شَيْءٌ أَثْقَلُ فِي مِيزَانِ الْمُؤْمِنِ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ مِنْ خُلُقٍ حَسَنٍ "Nothing is heavier on the believer's scale on the Day of Judgement than good character." (Tirmidhi 2002, Abu Dawud 4799)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Good manners are how you treat people: your words, your face and what you do. They count at home and outside, and when nobody is looking. The best thing parents can give a child is good manners. The best thing a child can give back is to keep them.
- **check:** The book tells the story of a woman who threw rubbish on the Prophet's path. It has no reliable source, so it is replaced with the Bedouin who pulled his cloak (Bukhari 3149, Muslim 1057).
3. *The best in character.* Someone asked Aisha about the character of the Prophet ﷺ. She said: "His character was the Qur'an." (Muslim 746) She meant that he lived by what the Qur'an teaches, every day. He did not swear at people or call them names. Anas was once walking with him. The Prophet ﷺ was wearing a cloak with a thick, rough edge. A Bedouin came up and pulled the cloak so hard that it left a mark on his neck. "Muhammad, order them to give me some of Allah's wealth that you have!" The Prophet ﷺ turned to him, smiled, and told them to give the man something. (Bukhari 3149, Muslim 1057)
4. *Close to the Prophet.* The Prophet ﷺ said: "The ones I love most, and who will sit closest to me on Judgement Day, have the best manners." That hadith is in Tirmidhi 2018. He also said that the believer with the most complete faith is the one with the best character. (Abu Dawud 4682) Good manners start with small things. A smile. A salam. A "please" and a "thank you". Waiting your turn to speak.
5. Quiz: What did Aisha say about the Prophet's character? **His character was the Qur'an** · What did the Prophet ﷺ do when the Bedouin pulled his cloak? **He smiled and gave him something** · What is heaviest on the scale on the Day of Judgement? **Good character**
6. Sort into Good manners / Bad manners: Smiling at people (Good manners) · Interrupting when someone is talking (Bad manners) · Saying salam first (Good manners) · Complaining about everything (Bad manners) · Helping without being asked (Good manners) · Shouting when I am cross (Bad manners) · Saying "please" and "thank you" (Good manners)
7. Write: For one week, take one manner a day: smiling, please and thank you, not interrupting, salam first, no complaining, helping without being asked, speaking gently. Which day was easiest? Which was hardest? Which manner will you keep?

### 9. Parents 9: Causing your parents to cry

- **check:** The printed book gives "بكاء الوالدين من العقوق والكبائر" as words of the Prophet ﷺ. It is a saying of Ibn Umar (al-Adab al-Mufrad 31), so it is shown under the card as his words. The card gives the sound hadith on the same subject (Bukhari 5976).
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي بَكْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: أَلَا أُنَبِّئُكُمْ بِأَكْبَرِ الْكَبَائِرِ؟ قُلْنَا: بَلَى يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ. قَالَ: الْإِشْرَاكُ بِاللَّهِ، وَعُقُوقُ الْوَالِدَيْنِ "Shall I not tell you about the greatest of the major sins? We said: 'Yes, O Messenger of Allah.' He said: 'Associating partners with Allah, and being undutiful to parents.'" (Bukhari 5976, Muslim 87)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Hurting your parents is one of the biggest sins. The Prophet ﷺ named it right after shirk. Rude words, ignoring them and making them cry are all part of it.
- **check:** Told as Muslim 1748 tells it. The book's speech by Sa'd ("even if you had a hundred souls...") and the mother's name were left out.
3. *Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas and his mother.* Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas was one of the first Companions to become Muslim. His mother was very angry when she found out. She swore she would not speak to him, or eat, or drink, until he left Islam. "You say Allah tells you to be good to your parents," she said. "I am your mother, and I am telling you to do this." Sa'd would not leave his religion. His mother went three days without food, until she fainted.
4. Ayah 31:15: "But if they strive to make you join others with Me in worship, things of which you have no knowledge, do not obey them. And stay with them in this world in a good way."
5. *Kind, even when you cannot obey.* Allah revealed ayat about Sa'd and his mother. (Muslim 1748) Sa'd could not obey his mother in leaving Islam. Nobody is obeyed when they ask us to disobey Allah. But Allah still told him to stay with her in a good way. Even when we cannot do what a parent asks, we stay gentle and respectful.
6. Sort into Tears of joy / Tears of pain: Shouting "Leave me alone!" when they ask about my day (Tears of pain) · Making them breakfast as a surprise (Tears of joy) · Lying about where I went (Tears of pain) · Learning a surah and reciting it to them (Tears of joy) · Rolling my eyes at their advice (Tears of pain) · Writing them a thank-you letter (Tears of joy) · Slamming my door after an argument (Tears of pain) · Making du'a for them when they don't know (Tears of joy) · Ignoring their phone calls (Tears of pain) · Hugging them and saying "I love you" (Tears of joy)
7. Quiz: What did Sa'd's mother want him to do? **Leave Islam** · What does Allah tell us to do when parents ask us to disobey Him? **Not obey them, but still treat them kindly** · Which sin did the Prophet ﷺ name together with shirk? **Being undutiful to parents**
8. Write: Have you ever made a parent sad? Did you say sorry? Now plan something this week that could bring tears of joy to your parent. Write what you will do, and afterwards, what happened.

### 10. Parents 10: Caring for your father's friends

1. Hadith: عَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ قَالَ: سَمِعْتُ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ ﷺ يَقُولُ: إِنَّ أَبَرَّ الْبِرِّ أَنْ يَصِلَ الرَّجُلُ أَهْلَ وُدِّ أَبِيهِ "The finest kind of goodness is that a man keeps up good ties with the people his father loved." (Muslim 2552)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Being good to your parents includes being good to the people they love. It carries on even after your parents have died.
3. *Ibn Umar and the Bedouin.* Abdullah ibn Umar was the son of Umar ibn al-Khattab. One day, after his father had died, he was travelling to Makkah. On the road he met a Bedouin. Ibn Umar greeted him and let him ride the donkey he had with him. Then he took the turban off his own head and gave it to him. The people with Ibn Umar were surprised. "May Allah make you good! These are Bedouins. They would be happy with much less." "This man's father was a friend of my father, Umar," said Ibn Umar. "And I heard the Messenger of Allah ﷺ say: 'The finest kind of goodness is keeping up ties with the people your father loved.'" (Muslim 2552)
4. Quiz: Where was Ibn Umar going? **To Makkah** · What did he give the Bedouin? **A ride on his donkey and his turban** · Why was he so generous to this man? **The man's father had been his father's friend**
5. Quiz: Your father's old friend comes to visit. **Greet him, offer him a drink and sit with him for a while** · You see your mother's friend at the shops. **Say salam and ask how she is**
6. Write: Ask your father about his oldest friend: who he is, how they met, and why they are still friends. Then write a short, polite note to one of your father's friends or relatives, thanking them for something.

### 11. Unit 1 test: Parents and family (test)

1. Quiz: The Prophet ﷺ said prayer on time is the deed Allah loves most. What came second? **Being good to parents** · "Who has the most right to my good company?" How many times did the Prophet ﷺ say "your mother"? **Three times** · What did the Prophet ﷺ tell the man who left his parents crying? **Go back and make them laugh** · Who will not enter Paradise, according to Hadith 3? **The one who cuts off his family ties** · Which of these is a way to be good to parents after they have died? **Making du'a for them** · You must obey parents even if they ask you to disobey Allah. **False** · Saying "uff" to parents is forbidden in the Qur'an. **True** · Keeping family ties increases your provision. **True**
2. Fill the gap: He is not one of us who does not show **mercy** to our young ones. · ...and does not show **respect** to our elders. · Paradise is beneath the feet of your **mother**. · The pleasure of the Lord is in the pleasure of the **father**. · "My Lord, have mercy on them as they brought me up when I was **small**."
3. Match: عُقُوق = being undutiful · الْجَنَّة = Paradise · أُمُّكَ = your mother · يَرْحَمْ = shows mercy · أَخْلَاق = manners · الْكَبَائِر = the major sins
4. Match: Uwais al-Qarani = Was very good to his mother in Yemen · Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas = Stayed Muslim but stayed kind to his mother · Ibn Umar = Gave his turban to his father's friend's son · Al-Aqra' ibn Habis = Had never kissed his children · Abdullah ibn Mas'ud = Asked which deed Allah loves most
5. Write: How has this unit changed the way you treat your parents? Give real examples.

### 12. Social 1: Brotherhood

1. *Social relations.* This part of the book is about the people around you: your friends, your classmates, your neighbours and every other Muslim. The Prophet ﷺ taught that all Muslims are brothers and sisters. He showed us how to treat each other, and he lived it himself.
2. Hadith: عَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: الْمُسْلِمُ أَخُو الْمُسْلِمِ، لَا يَظْلِمُهُ وَلَا يُسْلِمُهُ "A Muslim is the brother of a Muslim. He does not wrong him, and he does not abandon him." (Bukhari 2442, Muslim 2580)
3. *The words of the hadith.* [table] A brother in Islam is someone you never treat unfairly. When he is in trouble, you do not leave him alone with it.
4. *Six rights of a Muslim.* The Prophet ﷺ said: "A Muslim has six rights over another Muslim." (Muslim 2162) When you meet him, greet him with salam.; When he invites you, accept.; When he asks for your advice, give him honest advice.; When he sneezes and says "Alhamdulillah", say "Yarhamuk Allah".; When he is ill, visit him.; When he dies, follow his funeral. These are small things. Doing them again and again is what makes people feel like brothers and sisters.
5. Match: When you meet him = Say salam · When he invites you = Accept the invitation · When he asks your advice = Give him honest advice · When he sneezes and praises Allah = Say "Yarhamuk Allah" · When he is ill = Visit him · When he dies = Follow his funeral
6. Quiz: Some boys are picking on a new student. What does "he does not abandon him" mean here? **Stand up for him or get a teacher** · Your friend asks if his story for class is good. It has lots of mistakes. **Tell him kindly and help him fix it** · You could get a better mark by copying your friend's work and blaming him. **Never, that would be wronging him**
7. Write: This week, choose one classmate you do not usually talk to. Try to keep as many of the six rights with them as you can. Who did you choose? What did you do, and how did they respond?

### 13. Social 2: Be brothers

- **check:** The printed book gives this hadith from Abu Hurayrah and ends it "ثلاثة أيام". In Bukhari 6076 and Muslim 2559 it is from Anas ibn Malik, with "ثلاث ليال" in Bukhari. The narrator and wording here follow Bukhari.
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسِ بْنِ مَالِكٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَا تَبَاغَضُوا، وَلَا تَحَاسَدُوا، وَلَا تَدَابَرُوا، وَكُونُوا عِبَادَ اللَّهِ إِخْوَانًا، وَلَا يَحِلُّ لِمُسْلِمٍ أَنْ يَهْجُرَ أَخَاهُ فَوْقَ ثَلَاثِ لَيَالٍ "Do not hate one another, do not envy one another, and do not turn your backs on one another. Be servants of Allah, as brothers. It is not allowed for a Muslim to stay away from his brother for more than three nights." (Bukhari 6076, Muslim 2559)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Friends fall out sometimes. Islam gives us three days to calm down. After that, we must make up.
- **check:** The book tells of a Bedouin who insulted the Prophet ﷺ, with a parable about a runaway camel. That report is weak (al-Bazzar), so it is replaced with the Bedouin in the masjid from Bukhari and Muslim.
3. *The Bedouin in the masjid.* One day a Bedouin came into the Prophet's masjid in Madinah. He did not know how to behave in a masjid, and he urinated on the floor. The Companions were shocked. They jumped up and shouted at him to stop. The Prophet ﷺ said: "Leave him alone. Do not interrupt him." When the man had finished, the Prophet ﷺ asked for a bucket of water to be poured over the place. Then he called the man over. He explained gently that a masjid is for remembering Allah, for prayer and for reading the Qur'an. (Muslim 285) To his Companions he said: "You were sent to make things easy, not to make them hard." (Bukhari 6128)
4. Quiz: What did the Companions do when they saw the Bedouin? **Shouted at him to stop** · What did the Prophet ﷺ tell them? **Leave him alone, do not interrupt him** · How did the Prophet ﷺ teach the man? **Gently, after the place was cleaned**
5. Sort into Builds brotherhood / Breaks brotherhood: Feeling glad when my friend gets a prize (Builds brotherhood) · Wishing my friend would lose his new bike (Breaks brotherhood) · Not speaking to a friend for two weeks after a fight (Breaks brotherhood) · Saying sorry first, even if it was not all my fault (Builds brotherhood) · Turning my back when he says salam (Breaks brotherhood) · Making du'a for my friend (Builds brotherhood) · Keeping a promise to my friend (Builds brotherhood)
6. Fill the gap: Do not hate, do not **envy**, and do not turn your backs on one another. · Be servants of Allah, as **brothers**. · A Muslim must not stay away from his brother for more than **three** nights.
7. Write: When the Prophet ﷺ came to Madinah, he made pairs of Muslims into brothers. Choose a friend and write your own contract. What will you promise each other? Think about du'a, honest advice, no gossip and forgiving quickly.

### 14. Social 3: Getting along with others

- **check:** Not in the six books. Graded hasan by al-Albani (as-Silsilah as-Sahihah 426). Please confirm the reference.
1. Hadith: عَنْ جَابِرٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: الْمُؤْمِنُ يَأْلَفُ وَيُؤْلَفُ، وَلَا خَيْرَ فِيمَنْ لَا يَأْلَفُ وَلَا يُؤْلَفُ "The believer is friendly and easy to be friends with. There is no good in someone who is not friendly and whom no one can be friends with." (al-Tabarani (al-Awsat) from Jabir; Ahmad 9198 from Abu Hurayrah)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] A believer makes friends easily and people feel comfortable around him. Someone who pushes everyone away is missing something good. In the same hadith the Prophet ﷺ said: "The best of people are those who are most useful to people."
3. *Anas: ten years with the Prophet.* When the Prophet ﷺ came to Madinah, Anas ibn Malik was a boy of about ten. His mother, Umm Sulaym, brought him to the Prophet ﷺ to be his helper. Anas lived close to the Prophet ﷺ. He ran errands, carried things and helped with guests. Years later Anas said: "I served the Prophet for ten years. He never once said 'uff' to me." "If I did something, he never said, 'Why did you do that?'" "If I left something, he never said, 'Why didn't you do that?'" (Bukhari 6038, Muslim 2309) Ten years, and not one harsh word. Anas was a child, and children make mistakes. The Prophet ﷺ was patient with all of them.
4. Quiz: How long did Anas serve the Prophet ﷺ? **Ten years** · Who brought Anas to the Prophet ﷺ? **His mother, Umm Sulaym** · What did the Prophet ﷺ never say to Anas? **"Uff", or "Why did you do that?"**
5. Quiz: The Prophet ﷺ named something better than extra fasting, prayer and charity. What is it? **Making peace between people** · Two friends argued and have not spoken for two weeks. Each wants the other to say sorry first. **Remind them of the three-day rule and help them make up** · A student spread a rumour about a classmate and says "it was only a joke". **He should say sorry and tell people it was not true** · Two brothers fight over the computer every day. **Agree on fair turns and stick to them**
6. Write: Think of a real disagreement you had recently. What happened? How did you handle it? How could you have handled it the way the Prophet ﷺ would?

### 15. Social 4: An exemplary believer

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: الْمُؤْمِنُ مِرْآةُ الْمُؤْمِنِ "The believer is the mirror of the believer." (Abu Dawud 4918)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] A mirror shows you what is on your face, quietly, so you can fix it. A good friend does the same. He tells you kindly about a mistake, in private, and he wants the best for you.
3. *Abu Bakr as-Siddiq.* Abu Bakr was the closest friend of the Prophet ﷺ and the first grown man to accept Islam. He was a rich merchant, and he spent his wealth on freeing Muslims who were slaves. One of them was Bilal. Bilal's master tortured him to make him leave Islam, but Bilal kept saying "Ahad, Ahad": Allah is One. Abu Bakr bought him and set him free. The Prophet ﷺ said: "If I were to take a closest friend from my Ummah, I would take Abu Bakr." (Bukhari 3656)
4. *Everything for Allah.* Once the Prophet ﷺ asked the Companions to give charity. Umar thought, "Today I will beat Abu Bakr." He brought half of everything he owned. Abu Bakr brought everything he had. The Prophet ﷺ asked him: "What have you left for your family?" He said: "I have left them Allah and His Messenger." (Tirmidhi 3675, Abu Dawud 1678) Abu Bakr had a soft heart. When he read the Qur'an in his prayer, he could not hold back his tears. (Bukhari 3905)
5. Quiz: Who did Abu Bakr free from slavery? **Bilal** · What did Abu Bakr leave for his family when he gave all his wealth? **Allah and His Messenger** · How is a believer like a mirror? **He kindly shows his friend a mistake so he can fix it** · Your friend has spinach in his teeth before a speech. What does a "mirror" do? **Tells him quietly before he goes up**
6. Sort into A good mirror / Not a good mirror: Telling my friend about a mistake in private (A good mirror) · Pointing out his mistake in front of the class (Not a good mirror) · Praising what he does well (A good mirror) · Hiding his faults from others (A good mirror) · Making fun of his mistakes (Not a good mirror) · Saying "everything is perfect" when it is not (Not a good mirror)
7. Write: Give yourself a mark out of 10 for how you treat Allah, your parents, your brothers and sisters, friends and teachers. Ask someone who knows you well to do the same. Where are the biggest differences? Pick your lowest area and write three things you will do every day for a month.

### 16. Social 5: No one is better by colour or race

- **check:** The printed book has "من فرق فليس منا" from Ma'qil ibn Yasar. We could not find it in a sound collection, so this page gives the Farewell Sermon words that the book quotes in its activity (Ahmad 23489, graded sahih by al-Albani in as-Silsilah as-Sahihah 2700).
1. Hadith: يَا أَيُّهَا النَّاسُ، أَلَا إِنَّ رَبَّكُمْ وَاحِدٌ، وَإِنَّ أَبَاكُمْ وَاحِدٌ، أَلَا لَا فَضْلَ لِعَرَبِيٍّ عَلَى أَعْجَمِيٍّ، وَلَا لِعَجَمِيٍّ عَلَى عَرَبِيٍّ، وَلَا لِأَحْمَرَ عَلَى أَسْوَدَ، وَلَا أَسْوَدَ عَلَى أَحْمَرَ، إِلَّا بِالتَّقْوَى "O people, your Lord is One and your father is one. An Arab is not better than a non-Arab, and a non-Arab is not better than an Arab. A red-skinned man is not better than a black man, and a black man is not better than a red-skinned man, except through taqwa." (Ahmad 23489)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] We all come from Adam, and we all worship one Lord. Skin colour, country and family name do not make anyone better. Taqwa means being mindful of Allah. It is the only thing that makes one person better than another, and only Allah can see it.
3. *Bilal on the Ka'bah.* Bilal ibn Rabah was a Black man from Abyssinia. He had been a slave in Makkah, and he was tortured for being Muslim. In Madinah, the Prophet ﷺ chose Bilal to call the adhan. He was the first mu'adhdhin in Islam. When the Muslims entered Makkah in victory, the Prophet ﷺ told Bilal to climb on top of the Ka'bah and call the adhan from there. Some of the Quraysh leaders were unhappy to see a former slave up there. But this was the way of Islam. The Prophet ﷺ once said to Bilal: "I heard the sound of your footsteps ahead of me in Paradise." (Bukhari 1149)
4. Ayah 49:13: "O mankind, We created you from a male and a female, and made you into nations and tribes so that you may know one another. The most noble of you in the sight of Allah is the one with the most taqwa. Allah is All-Knowing, All-Aware."
5. Quiz: "I don't want to sit next to him. His family is poor." **Judging by wealth** · "She can't lead the group. She's too young." **Judging by age** · "People from that country are all lazy." **Judging by country** · "He's a new Muslim, so he doesn't know real Islam." **Looking down on a new Muslim** · What is the only thing that makes one person better than another? **Taqwa (being mindful of Allah)**
6. Fill the gap: Your Lord is One, and your **father** is one. · Allah made us into nations and tribes so that we may **know** one another. · Bilal was the first **mu'adhdhin** in Islam. · One person is better than another only through **taqwa**.
7. Write: What does the story of Bilal teach you about judging people by how they look? Write about a time you saw someone left out because of where they come from, how they look or how much money they have. What could you do next time?

### 17. Social 6: Unity

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي مُوسَى الْأَشْعَرِيِّ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: الْمُؤْمِنُ لِلْمُؤْمِنِ كَالْبُنْيَانِ يَشُدُّ بَعْضُهُ بَعْضًا "A believer to another believer is like a building whose parts hold each other up." (Bukhari 481, Muslim 2585)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] One brick on its own is weak. Bricks joined together make a wall that stands for years. Muslims are strong when they stand together.
3. *The Ansar and the Muhajirun.* In Makkah the Muslims were hurt and bullied. Many of them left their homes and moved to Madinah. They were called the Muhajirun, the emigrants. They arrived with almost nothing. The Muslims of Madinah, the Ansar, welcomed them. The Prophet ﷺ paired each emigrant with one of the Ansar and made them brothers. Sa'd ibn ar-Rabi' of the Ansar was paired with Abdur-Rahman ibn Awf. Sa'd offered him half of everything he owned. Abdur-Rahman said: "May Allah bless you in your family and your wealth. Show me the way to the market." He traded honestly and earned his own living. (Bukhari 3780)
4. Ayah 59:9: "And those who settled in the city and in faith before them love those who emigrated to them. They feel no envy for what the emigrants were given, and they put others before themselves, even when they are in need."
5. *Like one body.* The Prophet ﷺ also said: "The believers in their love, mercy and kindness to each other are like one body." "When one part of it hurts, the whole body stays awake with fever." (Muslim 2586) Try this. Snap one thin stick. Easy! Now tie ten sticks together and try again. Together they are too strong to break.
6. Sort into Brings us together / Pulls us apart: Praying in a row, shoulder to shoulder (Brings us together) · Making fun of how others pray (Pulls us apart) · Saying salam to everyone (Brings us together) · Only being friends with people from my own country (Pulls us apart) · Helping Muslims in need far away (Brings us together) · Gossip and rumours (Pulls us apart) · Breaking our fast together in Ramadan (Brings us together)
7. Quiz: What did Sa'd ibn ar-Rabi' offer Abdur-Rahman ibn Awf? **Half of everything he owned** · What did Abdur-Rahman ask for instead? **The way to the market** · In the hadith, believers are like a building. What holds it up? **Its parts holding each other up**
8. Write: Write one thing you will do this week to bring people together at school, one at the masjid, and one in your family. Is there someone you have been leaving out? What will you do about it?

### 18. Social 7: Following good examples

1. Hadith: عَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: مَنْ تَشَبَّهَ بِقَوْمٍ فَهُوَ مِنْهُمْ "Whoever imitates a people is one of them." (Abu Dawud 4031)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] We become like the people we copy. If we copy good people, we become good. If we copy people who disobey Allah, we start to be like them. The Prophet ﷺ also said: "A person follows the religion of his close friend, so be careful who you befriend." You can find this in Abu Dawud 4833 and Tirmidhi 2378.
3. *Umar ibn Abdul Aziz.* Umar ibn Abdul Aziz grew up as a prince. He wore the finest clothes and lived in palaces. As a young man he studied with the scholars of Madinah. He learned about his great-grandfather, the khalifah Umar ibn al-Khattab. That Umar had walked the streets at night to check on the poor. He wore patched clothes, even though he ruled a huge land. Umar ibn Abdul Aziz wanted to be like him.
4. *A khalifah like his great-grandfather.* When he became khalifah, he gave the palace treasures and fine horses back to the public treasury. His wife Fatimah gave back her jewels too. He wore simple clothes and ate plain food. At night he prayed and asked Allah to help him be fair. He lowered taxes on the poor and treated everyone fairly, Muslim or not. He ruled for only about two and a half years. The history books tell us that by the end, the zakat collectors could hardly find a poor person to give zakat to.
5. Quiz: Who did Umar ibn Abdul Aziz want to be like? **His great-grandfather, Umar ibn al-Khattab** · What did he do with the palace treasures? **Gave them back to the public treasury** · A person follows the religion of his... **close friend**
6. Sort into Good to copy / Better not to copy: My cousin who prays on time (Good to copy) · A YouTuber who swears in every video (Better not to copy) · My friend who always tells the truth (Good to copy) · Classmates who make fun of others (Better not to copy) · My grandmother who reads Qur'an every morning (Good to copy) · A footballer who cheats to win (Better not to copy)
7. Write: Who do you copy most, and what do you copy from them? Is it good for your deen? A younger child is watching you too. What are they learning? Write one thing you will change so you are a better example.

### 19. Social 8: Being grateful

- **check:** The printed book gives this from an-Nu'man ibn Bashir with the words "من لا يشكر الناس لا يشكر الله" (in Ahmad, inside a longer hadith). This page uses the Abu Hurayrah narration of Abu Dawud and Tirmidhi.
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَا يَشْكُرُ اللَّهَ مَنْ لَا يَشْكُرُ النَّاسَ "Whoever does not thank people does not thank Allah." (Abu Dawud 4811, Tirmidhi 1954)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Every good thing comes from Allah, and often He sends it through people. Saying "thank you" to them is part of being thankful to Him. Muslims often say "Jazak Allahu khayran": may Allah reward you with good.
3. *Dates for Aunt Fatima.* One morning in Madinah, Aisha's mother packed a basket of fresh dates from their garden. "Take these to Aunt Fatima," she said. "Why do we have to give them away?" Aisha grumbled. "We hardly have enough." Her little brother Tariq remembered the hadith their father had taught them the night before: "Whoever does not thank people does not thank Allah." "Aunt Fatima looked after you when you were ill last month," he said. "Don't you remember?" Aisha only shrugged. Aunt Fatima hugged them at the door. Aisha handed over the basket without looking at her.
4. *A plate and a guest.* Aunt Fatima brought out a lovely serving plate. "This is for your mother, to thank her for the medicine she made when my joints hurt." Aisha had not known about that. She felt her face go hot. Then an old man came to the gate. Aunt Fatima helped him in, gave him water and offered him the dates. He was Uncle Yusuf, who had taught hadith to the children of the masjid for years. "Now that he is old, people have forgotten him," Aunt Fatima said quietly. "When we thank people, we are thanking Allah for the good He sends through them."
5. *Aisha says thank you.* That evening Aisha went to her mother. "Thank you for teaching me to share. I'm sorry I complained about the dates." "Can I help you bake some bread for Aunt Fatima tomorrow?" Tariq grinned. "See? Thanking people goes round and round, like a circle." Aisha started thanking one person every day. She kept a little notebook of the kind things she noticed people doing.
6. Quiz: Why did Aisha not want to take the dates? **She wanted to keep them for her own family** · What had Aunt Fatima done for Aisha? **Looked after her when she was ill** · Who was Uncle Yusuf? **An old man who had taught hadith to children for years** · What did Aisha start doing every day? **Thanking one person**
7. Ayah 14:7: "And remember when your Lord announced: "If you are grateful, I will surely give you more. But if you are ungrateful, My punishment is severe.""
8. Write: Set a timer for three minutes and write every blessing you can think of, big and small. Then, for the next seven nights, write down three things you are thankful for before you sleep.

### 20. Social 9: Having others at heart

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسِ بْنِ مَالِكٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَا يُؤْمِنُ أَحَدُكُمْ حَتَّى يُحِبَّ لِأَخِيهِ مَا يُحِبُّ لِنَفْسِهِ "None of you truly believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for himself." (Bukhari 13, Muslim 45)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Before you act, ask: "Would I want this for me?" If you would like to be helped, help others. If you would hate to be laughed at, never laugh at others.
3. *Abu Talha and the guest.* A hungry man came to the Prophet ﷺ. The Prophet ﷺ sent to his wives, but none of them had anything except water. "Who will host this man tonight?" he asked. Abu Talha of the Ansar said: "I will." At home, his wife Umm Sulaym said: "We only have enough food for the children." They made a plan. She would settle the children to sleep. When the guest sat down to eat, she would put out the lamp.
4. *A meal in the dark.* So the guest ate in the dark. Abu Talha and his wife moved their hands as if they were eating too, so he would not feel shy. They went to bed hungry. In the morning the Prophet ﷺ told Abu Talha that Allah was pleased with what the two of them had done for their guest. Allah revealed: "...they put others before themselves, even when they are in need." (Qur'an 59:9; Bukhari 3798, Muslim 2054)
5. Quiz: Why did the Prophet ﷺ ask someone else to host the man? **His own house had nothing but water** · Why did Umm Sulaym put out the lamp? **So the guest would not see they had no food** · What did Allah say about people like them? **They put others before themselves**
6. Match: I love having friends = so I will be a friend to the new student · I love being respected = so I will speak politely to everyone · I love being forgiven = so I will forgive people quickly · I love getting help = so I will help people who are stuck · I love being included = so I will ask others to join our game
7. Write: This week, do something kind for someone without them knowing it was you. What did you do, and for whom? How did it feel to give without anyone thanking you?

### 21. Social 10: Building good relationships

- **check:** The printed book has "لا تمار أخاك ولا تمازحه ولا تعده موعدة فتخلفه" from Ibn Abbas (Tirmidhi 1995). That narration is weak, so this page gives Abu Dawud 4800, a sound hadith about the same thing: not arguing with your brother.
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي أُمَامَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: أَنَا زَعِيمٌ بِبَيْتٍ فِي رَبَضِ الْجَنَّةِ لِمَنْ تَرَكَ الْمِرَاءَ وَإِنْ كَانَ مُحِقًّا "I guarantee a house on the edge of Paradise for whoever stops arguing, even when he is right." (Abu Dawud 4800)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Some people argue just to win, and it hurts friendships. The Prophet ﷺ promised a house in Paradise to anyone who lets it go, even when they are right. Good relationships also need promises that are kept. Breaking promises is a sign of a hypocrite (Bukhari 33).
3. *The Prophet visits a sick boy.* A Jewish boy used to help the Prophet ﷺ. One day the boy fell ill, and the Prophet ﷺ went to visit him. He sat by the boy's head and said: "Accept Islam." The boy looked at his father, who was there. His father said: "Obey Abul-Qasim." (That was a name of the Prophet ﷺ.) The boy became Muslim. The Prophet ﷺ left, saying: "Praise be to Allah, who has saved him from the Fire." (Bukhari 1356)
4. *What the visit teaches.* The Prophet ﷺ noticed when someone was missing, and he went to see them. He visited the sick, even a boy who was not a Muslim. He cared about the boy in this life and in the next, so he invited him to Islam with gentle words.
5. Quiz: Who did the Prophet ﷺ visit? **A sick Jewish boy who used to help him** · What did the boy's father tell him? **Obey Abul-Qasim** · What is promised to someone who stops arguing even when he is right? **A house on the edge of Paradise** · Your friend says the capital of Australia is Sydney. You know it is Canberra, and he will not listen. **Tell him once, kindly, then drop it**
6. Write: Which of your relationships needs the most care right now? It could be with a parent, a brother or sister, a friend, a teacher or a neighbour. Write a short message to someone you have not spoken to for a while, and send it today.

### 22. Unit 2 test: Social relations (test)

1. Quiz: Which of these is one of the six rights of a Muslim over another Muslim? **Visiting him when he is ill** · The Prophet ﷺ named one thing better than extra fasting, prayer and charity. What is it? **Making peace between people** · In the Farewell Sermon, what is the only thing that makes one person better than another? **Taqwa** · "The believers are like one body." What happens when one part hurts? **The whole body stays awake with fever** · A person follows the religion of his close friend. What does this teach? **Choose your friends carefully** · It is allowed to stop speaking to a Muslim brother for six months. **False** · Being grateful brings more blessings. **True** · Being kind only counts if people see you do it. **False**
2. Fill the gap: None of you truly believes until he loves for his brother what he loves for **himself**. · A Muslim is the **brother** of a Muslim. · The believer is the **mirror** of the believer. · Whoever does not thank **people** does not thank Allah. · A believer to another believer is like a **building** whose parts hold each other up.
3. Match: Anas ibn Malik = Served the Prophet ﷺ for ten years · Abu Bakr = Gave everything he had in charity · Bilal = Called the adhan from the Ka'bah · Abdur-Rahman ibn Awf = Asked the way to the market · Abu Talha = Fed his guest in the dark · Umar ibn Abdul Aziz = Followed the example of his great-grandfather
4. Match: أَخُو = brother of · تَحَاسَدُوا = envy one another · مِرْآةُ = mirror · يَشْكُرُ = thanks · التَّقْوَى = being mindful of Allah · الْمِرَاءَ = arguing
5. Write: Which hadith from this unit has made you think the most? How has it changed the way you treat people?

### 23. Worship 1: Reading the Qur'an

1. *Prayers and worship.* This part of the book is about worship: the Qur'an, prayer, du'a, fasting and Hajj. You will also learn about repentance, about using your life well, and about doing everything for Allah alone.
- **check:** In Musnad Ahmad, the start of a longer hadith. Graded sahih by al-Albani (as-Silsilah as-Sahihah 260). Please confirm the number.
2. Hadith: عَنْ عَبْدِ الرَّحْمَنِ بْنِ شِبْلٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: اقْرَءُوا الْقُرْآنَ وَاعْمَلُوا بِهِ "Read the Qur'an and act upon it." (Ahmad 15529)
3. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Reading the Qur'an brings great reward. But the Qur'an came to be lived. We read it, try to understand it, and then do what it says.
4. *Juju and Al-Fatihah.* Every Saturday, Juju and his brother Waleed went to madrasah. One morning Ustadh Issa asked the class: "Who will tell us about their favourite surah?" Juju's hand shot up. Waleed slouched in his seat and doodled. He had not practised his Qur'an all week. He had been playing video games instead. "My favourite is Al-Fatihah," said Juju. "It's a du'a that Allah Himself taught us." Waleed rolled his eyes. "Everyone picks Al-Fatihah." Ustadh Issa asked the class: "Is it enough to read the Qur'an and not act on it?" Everyone called out: "No!"
5. *Living the ayah.* "Which ayah of Al-Fatihah do you try to live by?" Ustadh Issa asked Juju. "Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in: You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help," said Juju. "I was scared before I recited at the Eid party. I whispered it to myself, and I felt calm." That evening Juju led the family in Maghrib. Waleed tried to follow and stumbled over words he should have known. His cheeks went red. Their mother told them that the Prophet ﷺ called Al-Fatihah Umm al-Kitab, the Mother of the Book. Later Waleed found Juju reading a tafsir book. "Why are you still studying Al-Fatihah? You know it already." "I want to understand it," said Juju. "When we say 'Guide us to the straight path', we ask Allah to help us choose well every day."
6. *Waleed's change.* Waleed was quiet for a moment. "Juju, will you help me practise?" Every night that week, the brothers sat together with the Qur'an. Juju helped with tajwid and talked about what the ayat mean. The next Saturday, Waleed stood up in class. "My friends wanted me to skip practice for football. I asked Allah to guide me. I asked them to practise with me first, and two of them came!" Their parents gave them each a new mushaf. "Because you're both trying to live the Qur'an," said their mother.
7. *Abdullah ibn Mas'ud recites.* One day the Prophet ﷺ said to Abdullah ibn Mas'ud: "Recite the Qur'an to me." "Shall I recite it to you, when it was revealed to you?" asked Abdullah. The Prophet ﷺ said: "I love to hear it from someone else." So Abdullah recited Surah an-Nisa. He reached the ayah: "How will it be when We bring a witness from every nation, and bring you as a witness over these people?" The Prophet ﷺ said: "That is enough for now." Abdullah looked up, and the Prophet's eyes were full of tears. (Bukhari 5050, Muslim 800)
8. Quiz: Why was Waleed embarrassed at Maghrib? **He stumbled over words he should have known** · Which ayah helped Juju when he was scared? **"You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help"** · What did the Prophet ﷺ call Al-Fatihah? **Umm al-Kitab, the Mother of the Book** · Why did the Prophet ﷺ cry when Ibn Mas'ud recited? **The ayah about him being a witness moved him** · How many rewards for one letter of the Qur'an? **Ten** · Who are the best of you, said the Prophet ﷺ? **Those who learn the Qur'an and teach it**
9. Write: Read at least one page of the Qur'an every day for seven days. Then choose one ayah you read. Write it down and explain how it connects to your life right now.

### 24. Worship 2: Repentance

1. Hadith: عَنْ عَبْدِ اللَّهِ بْنِ مَسْعُودٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: التَّائِبُ مِنَ الذَّنْبِ كَمَنْ لَا ذَنْبَ لَهُ "The one who repents from a sin is like someone who has no sin." (Ibn Majah 4250)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Tawbah means turning back to Allah after doing wrong. When it is real, Allah wipes the sin away, as if it never happened.
3. *The lost camel.* The Prophet ﷺ told a story to show how happy Allah is when someone repents. A man was travelling through the desert. His camel carried all his food and water. He lay down to rest, and when he woke up, his camel was gone. He searched until he was worn out with heat and thirst. He gave up, lay down and waited to die. When he opened his eyes, there was his camel, standing right next to him, with all his food and water! The Prophet ﷺ said that Allah is even happier with the tawbah of His servant than this man was with his camel. (Bukhari 6308, Muslim 2744)
4. Ayah 39:53: "Say: "O My servants who have wronged themselves, do not give up hope of the mercy of Allah. Allah forgives all sins. He is the Forgiving, the Merciful.""
5. Fill the gap: First, feel **sorry** in your heart for what you did. · Second, **stop** the sin straight away. · Third, make up your mind not to **go back** to it. · If you wronged a person, you also put it right with **them**.
6. Quiz: In the story, how happy was the man when he found his camel? **Overjoyed, after he had given up hope** · Who is happier than that man? **Allah, when His servant repents** · You did something wrong yesterday. What should you do? **Say sorry to Allah, stop, and try not to do it again** · After a big sin, there is no point in repenting. **False**
7. Write: Write a letter to Allah, between you and Him. He is al-Ghaffar, the Forgiving, and at-Tawwab, the One who accepts repentance. What do you want to say sorry for? What will you try to do from now on? What do you ask Him to help you with?

### 25. Worship 3: Prayer and Qur'an at home

- **check:** The printed book has "نوروا منازلكم بالصلاة وقراءة القرآن" from Anas (al-Bayhaqi). Its chain is weak, so this page gives Muslim 780 with the same meaning. The Prophet ﷺ also said: "Pray some of your prayers in your houses" (Bukhari 432, Muslim 777).
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ أَنَّ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ ﷺ قَالَ: لَا تَجْعَلُوا بُيُوتَكُمْ مَقَابِرَ، إِنَّ الشَّيْطَانَ يَنْفِرُ مِنَ الْبَيْتِ الَّذِي تُقْرَأُ فِيهِ سُورَةُ الْبَقَرَةِ "Do not make your houses into graveyards. Shaytan runs away from the house in which Surah al-Baqarah is recited." (Muslim 780)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Nobody prays or reads Qur'an in a graveyard. A home where the family prays and recites is full of light, and Shaytan stays away from it.
3. *The shepherd boy.* Abdullah ibn Mas'ud was a young shepherd in Makkah. He looked after the sheep of a Quraysh chief, Uqbah ibn Abi Mu'ayt. One day the Prophet ﷺ and Abu Bakr came by and asked if he had any milk. "I cannot," said Abdullah. "The sheep are not mine. I am only looking after them." The Prophet ﷺ liked his honesty. Abdullah soon became Muslim. (Musnad Ahmad) From then on he stayed close to the Prophet ﷺ, helped him, and learned the Qur'an straight from him.
4. *The first to recite aloud.* One day in Makkah, the Companions said: "Quraysh have never heard the Qur'an recited out loud. Who will do it?" "I will," said Abdullah ibn Mas'ud. They were worried, because he had no strong family to protect him. "Let me go," he said. "Allah will protect me." He stood by the Ka'bah and recited Surah ar-Rahman out loud. The Quraysh jumped on him and beat him, but he kept reciting. He came back with his face hurt. "If you like, I will do it again tomorrow," he said. (Sirah of Ibn Ishaq)
5. Quiz: Why did Abdullah refuse to give the milk? **The sheep were not his** · What did Ibn Mas'ud recite out loud at the Ka'bah? **Surah ar-Rahman** · What is the first thing we will be asked about on the Day of Judgement? **Our prayer** · What does Shaytan run away from? **A house where Surah al-Baqarah is recited**
6. Sort into Helps my focus / Takes my focus away: Learning what the words of the prayer mean (Helps my focus) · Checking my phone just before I pray (Takes my focus away) · Rushing to finish quickly (Takes my focus away) · Praying in a quiet place (Helps my focus) · Looking at the place of sujud (Helps my focus) · Thinking about my homework (Takes my focus away) · Making wudu calmly first (Helps my focus)
7. Write: Give your last five prayers a mark out of 10 for focus. What distracted you most? Write three things you will do to fix it, and try them for a week.

### 26. Worship 4: Du'a

1. Hadith: عَنِ النُّعْمَانِ بْنِ بَشِيرٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: إِنَّ الدُّعَاءَ هُوَ الْعِبَادَةُ "Du'a is worship." (Abu Dawud 1479, Tirmidhi 2969)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] When you ask Allah for something, you show that you need Him and that only He can give. That is worship. Allah loves to be asked.
3. *The du'a that healed a chief.* Some Companions were travelling and stopped near an Arab tribe. They asked to be treated as guests, but the tribe refused. Then the chief of the tribe was stung by a scorpion. His people tried everything. Nothing worked. So they came to the Companions: "Can any of you treat him?" "You would not host us," said one of the Companions. "We will only do it for a payment." They agreed on a flock of sheep. He recited Surah al-Fatihah over the chief and blew gently on him. The chief got up as if nothing had happened. The Companions did not touch the sheep until they had asked the Prophet ﷺ. He smiled and said: "How did you know it is a ruqyah? Take them, and give me a share." (Bukhari 2276, 5736)
- **check:** This is a popular story about Imam Ahmad. We know of no reliable source for it, so it is told as "a story people tell".
4. *The baker's du'a.* People tell a story about Imam Ahmad ibn Hanbal, the great scholar. He came to a town where nobody knew him and wanted to sleep in the masjid. The caretaker did not know him either, and sent him away. A baker saw the old man and invited him to stay in his bakery. All night the baker kneaded dough and baked bread. And all night his lips kept moving: "Astaghfirullah, astaghfirullah." "Has this brought you anything?" asked the Imam. "Allah has answered every du'a I made, except one," said the baker. "I want to meet Imam Ahmad." "I am Ahmad ibn Hanbal," said the Imam. "Allah brought me to your door."
5. Quiz: What did the Companion recite over the chief? **Surah al-Fatihah** · Why did the Companions wait before taking the sheep? **To ask the Prophet ﷺ if it was allowed** · What did the baker say all night? **Astaghfirullah**
6. Sort into A special time for du'a / Not one of them: The last third of the night (A special time for du'a) · In sujud (A special time for du'a) · Between the adhan and the iqamah (A special time for du'a) · A special hour on Friday (A special time for du'a) · Only on my birthday (Not one of them) · Only when I am in the masjid (Not one of them)
7. Write: Write your own du'as. Make one for your parents and one for your studies. Add one for a habit you want to change, one for the Ummah and one for the Akhirah. Choose one and make it every day for thirty days.

### 27. Worship 5: Hajj

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: مَنْ حَجَّ لِلَّهِ فَلَمْ يَرْفُثْ وَلَمْ يَفْسُقْ رَجَعَ كَيَوْمِ وَلَدَتْهُ أُمُّهُ "Whoever does Hajj for Allah, and does not speak indecently or sin, comes back like the day his mother gave birth to him." (Bukhari 1521, Muslim 1350)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] A newborn baby has no sins. A Hajj done for Allah, with good behaviour, wipes a person clean like that.
3. *Ibrahim, Hajar and Isma'il.* Allah told Ibrahim to take his wife Hajar and their baby Isma'il to an empty valley in Arabia. There was no water and nobody lived there. "Has Allah told you to do this?" Hajar asked. "Yes," said Ibrahim. "Then He will not let us be lost," she said. When their water ran out, Hajar ran between the hills of Safa and Marwah seven times, looking for help. Then an angel struck the ground, and water gushed out. It was the well of Zamzam. Travellers from the tribe of Jurhum saw the water and asked to live there. (Bukhari 3364) Years later, Ibrahim and Isma'il built the Ka'bah together, for the worship of Allah alone.
4. *The days of Hajj.* Hajj is one of the five pillars of Islam. Every Muslim who is able must do it once in a lifetime. Pilgrims put on ihram. For men it is two plain white sheets, so rich and poor look the same. They call out: "Labbayk Allahumma labbayk: Here I am, O Allah, here I am." On the 9th of Dhul-Hijjah they stand on the plain of Arafat all day, making du'a. They spend the night at Muzdalifah under the sky. At Mina they throw pebbles at the Jamarat, remembering how Ibrahim turned away from Shaytan. They sacrifice an animal and share the meat with the poor. They walk around the Ka'bah seven times (tawaf) and walk between Safa and Marwah (sa'i), like Hajar did.
5. Order: Put on ihram at the miqat → Stand at Arafat → Spend the night at Muzdalifah → Throw pebbles at the Jamarah → Sacrifice an animal → Shave or cut the hair → Tawaf around the Ka'bah → Sa'i between Safa and Marwah
6. Match: Arafat = Standing in du'a all day · Muzdalifah = Spending the night under the sky · Mina = Throwing pebbles at the Jamarat · The Ka'bah = Walking around it seven times · Safa and Marwah = Walking between them like Hajar
7. Write: Find someone who has been on Hajj. Ask them: What moved you most? What surprised you? What advice would you give someone going for the first time? Then write: if you could make one du'a at the Ka'bah, what would it be?

### 28. Worship 6: Praying in congregation

1. Hadith: عَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: صَلَاةُ الْجَمَاعَةِ أَفْضَلُ مِنْ صَلَاةِ الْفَذِّ بِسَبْعٍ وَعِشْرِينَ دَرَجَةً "Prayer in congregation is better than prayer alone by twenty-seven degrees." (Bukhari 645, Muslim 650)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Praying together with others, at the masjid or at home, brings twenty-seven times the reward. It also brings Muslims together.
3. *The blind man and the adhan.* Abdullah ibn Umm Maktum was a blind Companion. He lived far from the Prophet's masjid, and the way was not safe. He came to the Prophet ﷺ and said: "I am blind, my house is far, and I have no one to lead me. May I pray at home?" At first the Prophet ﷺ said yes. Then, as the man turned to go, he called him back: "Do you hear the call to prayer?" "Yes," said Abdullah. "Then answer it," said the Prophet ﷺ. (Muslim 653, Abu Dawud 552) If a blind man was told to come, what is our excuse when the masjid is close by?
4. Quiz: What did Ibn Umm Maktum ask? **If he could pray at home** · What did the Prophet ﷺ ask him? **Do you hear the call to prayer?** · If you pray 10 prayers in congregation, how many "prayers alone" are they worth? **270** · Your friend says: "Praying at home is just the same." **Tell him kindly about the 27 times reward**
5. Match: I sleep through Fajr = Sleep earlier and set an alarm · I am always late for the jama'ah = Make wudu as soon as I hear the adhan · The masjid is too far = Pray in congregation with my family at home · I am in the middle of a game = Pause it: the game can wait
6. Write: For one week, keep a record of how many prayers you pray with others, at the masjid or with your family. How many out of 35? What stopped you, and what will you do about it?

### 29. Worship 7: Fasting in Ramadan

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: مَنْ صَامَ رَمَضَانَ إِيمَانًا وَاحْتِسَابًا غُفِرَ لَهُ مَا تَقَدَّمَ مِنْ ذَنْبِهِ "Whoever fasts Ramadan with faith, hoping for its reward, will have his past sins forgiven." (Bukhari 38, Muslim 760)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Fasting for Allah, believing in Him and hoping for His reward, wipes away past sins. It is a gift that comes once a year.
3. *Salman and Abu ad-Darda.* The Prophet ﷺ had made Salman al-Farisi and Abu ad-Darda brothers. One day Salman visited Abu ad-Darda's home. Umm ad-Darda looked tired and untidy. "Your brother Abu ad-Darda doesn't care about anything in this world," she said. Abu ad-Darda came home and offered Salman food. "Eat," he said. "I am fasting." "I will not eat until you eat," said Salman. So he ate. At night Abu ad-Darda got up to pray. "Sleep," said Salman. Later he got up again. "Sleep." In the last part of the night Salman said: "Now get up," and they prayed together.
4. *Give everyone their right.* Salman said: "Your Lord has a right over you. Your body has a right over you. Your family has a right over you. So give each one its right." Abu ad-Darda told the Prophet ﷺ. The Prophet ﷺ said: "Salman has spoken the truth." (Bukhari 1968) Islam is balanced. In Ramadan we fast by day and eat at night. We worship more, but we look after our bodies and our families too.
5. Match: My tongue = Lies, swearing and backbiting · My eyes = Looking at what is haram · My ears = Listening to gossip · My hands = Hitting or taking what is not mine · My heart = Jealousy and grudges
6. Quiz: Why did Umm ad-Darda look tired and untidy? **Her husband spent all his time on extra worship** · What did Salman say about rights? **Your Lord, your body and your family each have a right** · What did the Prophet ﷺ say about Salman? **Salman has spoken the truth**
7. Write: Fast for a day (or part of a day, if you are young). What was the hardest moment? What did you think about when you were hungry? How did you feel about people who are hungry every day? Then set three goals for next Ramadan.

### 30. Worship 8: Long life and good deeds

1. Hadith: عَنْ عَبْدِ اللَّهِ بْنِ بُسْرٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: خَيْرُ النَّاسِ مَنْ طَالَ عُمُرُهُ وَحَسُنَ عَمَلُهُ "The best of people is the one whose life is long and whose deeds are good." (Tirmidhi 2329)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] A long life is a gift only when it is filled with good. In another hadith the Prophet ﷺ said the worst of people is the one who lives long and does bad deeds (Tirmidhi 2330).
3. *Two Companions who lived long.* Anas ibn Malik began serving the Prophet ﷺ when he was about ten. He lived to be about a hundred years old. All those years he taught the hadith of the Prophet ﷺ to people who came to learn. Much of what we know about the Prophet ﷺ comes from him. Abu Ayyub al-Ansari had hosted the Prophet ﷺ in his home when he first came to Madinah. When he was a very old man, he still went out with the Muslim army. He died on that journey and was buried near the walls of Constantinople.
4. Quiz: How old was Anas when he started serving the Prophet ﷺ? **About ten** · What did Anas do during his long life? **Taught the hadith of the Prophet ﷺ** · Where was Abu Ayyub buried? **Near the walls of Constantinople** · You teach 5 people to say SubhanAllah 33 times after each of the 5 prayers. How many times a day do they say it altogether? **825**
5. Sort into Ongoing charity / Useful knowledge / A good child: Helping to build a well (Ongoing charity) · Planting a tree that people eat from (Ongoing charity) · Teaching my brother a surah (Useful knowledge) · Showing a friend how to make wudu (Useful knowledge) · Making du'a for my parents every day (A good child) · Giving to the masjid building fund (Ongoing charity) · Being kind to my parents so they are proud of me (A good child)
6. Write: If you could start one project that keeps earning reward even after you are gone, what would it be? Who would it help, and how would it keep going?

### 31. Worship 9: Du'a between adhan and iqamah

- **check:** The printed book has the wording "الدعاء بين الأذان والإقامة مستجاب فادعوا", reported in Ahmad and others. This page gives the wording of Abu Dawud 521 and Tirmidhi 212, which has the same meaning.
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسِ بْنِ مَالِكٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: الدُّعَاءُ لَا يُرَدُّ بَيْنَ الْأَذَانِ وَالْإِقَامَةِ "Du'a between the adhan and the iqamah is not turned away." (Abu Dawud 521, Tirmidhi 212)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Five times a day there is a short gap between the adhan and the iqamah. The Prophet ﷺ told us that du'a in this gap is not turned away.
3. *The golden minutes.* The Companions loved to learn about the times when du'a is answered. This was one the Prophet ﷺ taught them. Think about it. You have just answered Allah's call, and you are about to stand in front of Him. It is the perfect time to ask. Many people chat or look at their phones while they wait for the iqamah. You can use those minutes to ask Allah for your parents, your studies, the Ummah and Jannah. First repeat the words of the adhan after the mu'adhdhin and send salawat on the Prophet ﷺ, as he taught us (Muslim 384). Then raise your hands and ask.
4. Quiz: When is du'a not turned away, in this hadith? **Between the adhan and the iqamah** · You hear the adhan at home. What could you do in the gap before you pray? **Make wudu, then make du'a** · You can only make du'a in Arabic. **False** · What should we do while the adhan is being called? **Repeat its words after the mu'adhdhin**
5. Write: Plan a du'a for after each adhan: Fajr, Dhuhr, Asr, Maghrib and Isha. Then for seven days, make du'a between every adhan and iqamah. Write what you noticed.

### 32. Worship 10: Sincerity in actions
