1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: إِنَّ اللَّهَ لَا يَنْظُرُ إِلَى صُوَرِكُمْ وَأَمْوَالِكُمْ، وَلَكِنْ يَنْظُرُ إِلَى قُلُوبِكُمْ وَأَعْمَالِكُمْ "Allah does not look at your appearance or your wealth. He looks at your hearts and your deeds." (Muslim 2564)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Allah sees why we do things. Ikhlas, sincerity, means doing a good deed only for Allah, not to be praised. The Prophet ﷺ said: "Actions are judged by intentions, and everyone will get what they intended." (Bukhari 1, Muslim 1907)
3. *The three who were judged.* The Prophet ﷺ told of three people who will be judged first on the Day of Judgement. The first died in battle. "I fought for You," he will say. Allah will say: "You lie. You fought so people would call you brave." The second learned and taught, and recited the Qur'an. Allah will say: "You did it so people would call you a scholar and a fine reciter." The third was rich and gave money away. Allah will say: "You gave so people would call you generous." All three will be thrown into the Fire. (Muslim 1905)
4. *Who am I doing it for?.* Years later, Abu Hurayrah told this hadith to a student. He was so frightened by it that he fainted more than once while telling it. (Tirmidhi 2382) Their deeds looked great, but they did them for people. A small deed done for Allah is worth more than a big one done to show off. Before you do something good, ask yourself: "Who am I doing this for?"
5. Sort into Sincere / Not sincere: Giving charity so Allah is pleased with me (Sincere) · Giving charity so my friends see how generous I am (Not sincere) · Learning Qur'an to understand Allah's words (Sincere) · Learning Qur'an only to win a prize (Not sincere) · Helping a classmate because he is stuck (Sincere) · Helping a classmate so the teacher praises me (Not sincere) · Praying well even when nobody is watching (Sincere)
6. Quiz: Why was the brave fighter punished? **He fought so people would call him brave** · What does Allah look at? **Our hearts and our deeds** · Actions are judged by... **intentions**
7. Write: Think of three good things you did this week. Be honest: why did you do each one? Was it only for Allah, partly, or not really? Before your next good deed, stop and say: "I am doing this for Allah." Write how it felt different.

### 33. Unit 3 test: Prayer and worship (test)

1. Quiz: How many times better is prayer in congregation than prayer alone? **Twenty-seven** · When is du'a not turned away? **Between the adhan and the iqamah** · Which of these keeps earning reward after death? **A good child who prays for his parents** · How many rewards does one letter of the Qur'an bring? **Ten** · Which is NOT one of the steps of tawbah? **Telling everyone about the sin** · Hajj must be done by every Muslim every year. **False** · Fasting teaches patience and thankfulness. **True** · You can only make du'a in Arabic. **False**
2. Fill the gap: Actions are judged by **intentions**. · Read the Qur'an and **act** upon it. · The one who repents from a sin is like someone who has no **sin**. · Du'a is **worship**. · Whoever fasts Ramadan with faith, hoping for its reward, will have his past **sins** forgiven.
3. Match: Abdullah ibn Mas'ud = Recited the Qur'an out loud at the Ka'bah · Ibn Umm Maktum = A blind man told to answer the adhan · Salman al-Farisi = Told his brother to give everyone their right · Hajar = Ran between Safa and Marwah · Abu Ayyub al-Ansari = Joined the army when very old
4. Match: التَّائِبُ = the one who repents · الدُّعَاءَ = du'a · الْجَمَاعَةِ = congregation · إِيمَانًا = with faith · عُمُرُهُ = his life · قُلُوبِكُمْ = your hearts
5. Write: Which worship habit did you improve during this unit? How has it changed your day?

### 34. Character 1: Good morals

1. *Good morals and virtue.* This part of the book is about character: being modest, keeping promises, being trustworthy and merciful, and guiding others to good. The Prophet ﷺ was the best example of all of these. You will see how he lived them, and how his Companions copied him.
2. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي ذَرٍّ قَالَ: قَالَ لِي رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: اتَّقِ اللَّهَ حَيْثُمَا كُنْتَ، وَأَتْبِعِ السَّيِّئَةَ الْحَسَنَةَ تَمْحُهَا، وَخَالِقِ النَّاسَ بِخُلُقٍ حَسَنٍ "Be mindful of Allah wherever you are. Follow a bad deed with a good deed, and it will wipe it out. And treat people with good character." (Tirmidhi 1987)
3. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Three pieces of advice in one hadith. Remember Allah sees you, alone or with others. When you slip, do something good straight away. And be good to people.
4. *Mu'adh ibn Jabal.* Mu'adh ibn Jabal was a young Companion who loved to learn. The Prophet ﷺ said he knew more than anyone in the Ummah about halal and haram. (Tirmidhi 3790) The Prophet ﷺ sent him to Yemen to teach the people and judge between them. The same advice as this hadith was given to Mu'adh too. Mindful of Allah wherever you are: in your room alone, or in a crowd. Allah sees you either way. Follow a bad deed with a good one: everyone makes mistakes. Do not give up. Say sorry to Allah and do some good, and it washes the bad away. Good character with people: be honest, kind and fair with everyone you meet.
5. Quiz: Where did the Prophet ﷺ send Mu'adh? **To Yemen** · What was Mu'adh known for? **Knowing about halal and haram** · You were rude to your sister this morning. What does the hadith tell you to do? **Follow it with a good deed, like saying sorry and helping her** · You are alone in your room. Does Allah see what you do? **Yes, Allah sees us wherever we are**
6. Fill the gap: Be mindful of Allah **wherever** you are. · Follow a bad deed with a **good** deed. · The good deed will **wipe out** the bad one. · Treat people with good **character**.
7. Write: Give yourself a mark out of 5 for each: honesty, patience, kindness, controlling anger, keeping promises, respecting elders, helping, speaking gently, forgiving, being grateful. Which is your weakest? Write one thing you will do every day this week to improve it.

### 35. Character 2: Modesty

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: الْإِيمَانُ بِضْعٌ وَسِتُّونَ شُعْبَةً، وَالْحَيَاءُ شُعْبَةٌ مِنَ الْإِيمَانِ "Faith has sixty-something branches, and modesty is a branch of faith." (Bukhari 9, Muslim 35)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Haya is a feeling inside that stops you from doing shameful things. It shows in how you dress, how you speak and how you act, even when no one is watching.
3. *Uthman, the most modest.* Uthman ibn Affan was one of the first Muslims. He married two daughters of the Prophet ﷺ, Ruqayyah and then Umm Kulthum, so he was called Dhun-Nurayn: the one with two lights. He was rich and spent his wealth for Islam. He was one of the ten Companions told they would go to Paradise, and later he became the third khalifah. The Prophet ﷺ said: "The most truly modest of my Ummah is Uthman." (Tirmidhi 3790)
4. *Even the angels.* One day the Prophet ﷺ was lying down at home, and part of his leg was uncovered. Aisha was there. Abu Bakr asked to come in, and the Prophet ﷺ stayed as he was while they talked. Then Umar came in, and he stayed as he was. Then Uthman asked to come in. The Prophet ﷺ sat up and put his clothes straight. Afterwards Aisha asked him why. He said: "Should I not be shy in front of a man the angels are shy in front of?" (Muslim 2401)
5. Quiz: Khalid scores the winning goal. He says "Alhamdulillah" quietly and thanks his team. **Modest** · Aisha gets full marks and posts online: "Too smart for this class!" **Not modest** · Omar makes a mistake reciting Qur'an in class and laughs: "Who cares?" **Not modest** · Maryam sees her friend's hijab slipping and quietly whispers to tell her. **Modest** · Yusuf keeps bragging about how much money his father earns. **Not modest**
6. Quiz: Why was Uthman called Dhun-Nurayn? **He married two daughters of the Prophet ﷺ** · What did the Prophet ﷺ do when Uthman came in? **Sat up and put his clothes straight** · Who else is shy in front of Uthman, said the Prophet ﷺ? **The angels**
7. Write: Write about a time you showed haya, or a time you wish you had. What happened? What would you do differently?

### 36. Character 3: Being moral

- **check:** In Ibn Majah 4259 a man asks "Which of the believers is best?" and the Prophet ﷺ answers "The best of them in character". The book joins the question and answer into one sentence; please confirm the wording.
1. Hadith: عَنِ ابْنِ عُمَرَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: أَفْضَلُ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ أَحْسَنُهُمْ خُلُقًا "The best of the believers is the one with the best character." (Ibn Majah 4259)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] The best believer is not the one who looks most religious. It is the one who treats people best.
3. *The Prophet's character.* The Companions saw good character every day in the Prophet ﷺ himself. He never criticised food. If he liked it, he ate it. If he did not, he left it and said nothing. (Bukhari 3563) At home he helped his family. He mended his own shoes, sewed his own clothes and milked his own goat. (Bukhari 676, Ahmad) When he entered Makkah in victory, the people who had hurt him and driven him out were in his hands. He forgave them. Good character is mostly small, everyday choices: how you speak, how you listen, and how you treat people who can do nothing for you.
4. Quiz: Your best friend copied in a test. The teacher asks you straight out: "Did anyone cheat?" **Tell the truth politely, and talk to my friend kindly afterwards** · You find a wallet full of money with no name in it. Nobody saw. Your family needs money. **Hand it in so the owner can be found** · Your friends make fun of a new student who speaks differently. If you defend him, they might leave you out. **Stand up for him anyway**
5. Quiz: What did the Prophet ﷺ do with food he did not like? **Left it without saying anything bad** · What did he do at home? **Helped his family and mended his own shoes** · What did he do to the people of Makkah who had hurt him? **Forgave them**
6. Write: Choose one of the hard choices. Ask a parent or a friend what they would do. Did you choose the same? Which hadith or ayah helped you decide?

### 37. Character 4: Keeping promises

- **check:** In Musnad Ahmad. Graded hasan by al-Albani (Sahih al-Jami' 7179). Please confirm the number.
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسِ بْنِ مَالِكٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَا إِيمَانَ لِمَنْ لَا أَمَانَةَ لَهُ، وَلَا دِينَ لِمَنْ لَا عَهْدَ لَهُ "There is no faith for someone who cannot be trusted, and no religion for someone who does not keep his promises." (Ahmad 12383)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Keeping promises is part of faith. The Prophet ﷺ named three signs of a hypocrite. When he speaks, he lies. When he promises, he breaks it. When he is trusted, he betrays. (Bukhari 33)
3. *The Treaty of Hudaybiyyah.* Six years after the Hijrah, the Prophet ﷺ set out for Makkah with about 1,400 Companions to do Umrah. The Quraysh stopped them at a place called Hudaybiyyah. After long talks, both sides agreed a peace treaty. Some terms seemed unfair. If a man from Makkah became Muslim and came to Madinah, he had to be sent back. Umar was upset. "Why should we accept this?" he asked. But the Prophet ﷺ agreed to the treaty. (Bukhari 2731)
4. *Abu Jandal.* Just then a Muslim called Abu Jandal arrived, still in chains. He had escaped from Makkah, where he was being hurt for his Islam. His father Suhayl was the man writing the treaty for Quraysh. Suhayl said: "This is the first one you must send back." The Prophet ﷺ asked him to let Abu Jandal go, but Suhayl refused. The Prophet ﷺ had given his word, so Abu Jandal had to return. (Bukhari 2731) Two years later, the Quraysh broke the treaty themselves, and the Muslims entered Makkah. The Prophet ﷺ never broke his word, even when it was hard.
5. Quiz: Why did the Muslims go towards Makkah? **To do Umrah** · Why did the Prophet ﷺ send Abu Jandal back? **He had given his word in the treaty** · Who broke the treaty in the end? **The Quraysh** · Which is a sign of a hypocrite? **Breaking promises**
6. Sort into Promise keeper / Promise breaker: I said I would call Grandma, and I did (Promise keeper) · I promised to return the book, then kept it (Promise breaker) · I only promise what I know I can do (Promise keeper) · I said "I swear" and then forgot about it (Promise breaker) · I told my friend a secret he trusted me with (Promise breaker) · I came on time because I said I would (Promise keeper)
7. Write: Write four promises: one to Allah, one to your parents, one to your friends and one to yourself. Make them small enough to keep. In two weeks, check: how many did you keep?

### 38. Character 5: Trustworthiness

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَا يَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ مَنْ لَا يَأْمَنُ جَارُهُ بَوَائِقَهُ "He will not enter Paradise whose neighbour is not safe from his harm." (Muslim 46)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] People must be able to trust you. Your neighbours, your family and your friends should feel safe that you will never harm them or cheat them.
3. *Al-Amin, the trustworthy one.* Long before he became a Prophet, the people of Makkah called Muhammad ﷺ al-Amin: the trustworthy one. When people had something precious to keep safe, they left it with him. Later the Quraysh turned against his message. They called him a liar and a magician, and they planned to kill him. Yet many still kept their valuables with him. On the night of the Hijrah, the Prophet ﷺ asked Ali to stay behind in Makkah for a few days. His job was to give every item back to its owner. (Sirah of Ibn Ishaq)
4. *Even with those who cheat you.* The Prophet ﷺ said: "Return the trust to the one who trusted you, and do not cheat the one who cheated you." (Abu Dawud 3535) Trust covers money, and much more. A trustworthy person keeps secrets, does his jobs, and is there when people count on him.
5. Sort into Builds trust / Breaks trust: Keeping a friend's secret (Builds trust) · Returning things I borrow, on time (Builds trust) · Reading someone's messages without asking (Breaks trust) · Doing my chores when I said I would (Builds trust) · Telling small lies to get out of trouble (Breaks trust) · Taking money from my mum's purse (Breaks trust) · Owning up when I break something (Builds trust)
6. Quiz: What did the people of Makkah call the Prophet ﷺ before he was a Prophet? **Al-Amin, the trustworthy** · What job did Ali have on the night of the Hijrah? **Giving back the things people had left with the Prophet ﷺ** · Someone cheated you in a swap. What does the Prophet ﷺ say? **Do not cheat him back**
7. Write: The Prophet ﷺ was called al-Amin when he was young. If people gave you a nickname for how you behave now, what would it be? Ask a parent, a teacher and a friend how much they trust you. What could you do to be more trustworthy?

### 39. Character 6: Being merciful

- **check:** The printed book gives this hadith from Abu Sa'id al-Khudri. In Bukhari 7376 and Muslim 2319 it is from Jarir ibn Abdullah, so the narrator has been corrected.
1. Hadith: عَنْ جَرِيرِ بْنِ عَبْدِ اللَّهِ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: مَنْ لَا يَرْحَمِ النَّاسَ لَا يَرْحَمْهُ اللَّهُ "Whoever is not merciful to people, Allah will not be merciful to him." (Bukhari 7376, Muslim 2319)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] If we want Allah's mercy, we must be merciful. The Prophet ﷺ said: "Be merciful to those on earth, and the One above the heavens will be merciful to you." (Tirmidhi 1924)
3. *Mercy at Ta'if.* After his wife Khadijah and his uncle Abu Talib died, the Prophet ﷺ went to the town of Ta'if to call its people to Islam. Its leaders mocked him and refused him. People from the town threw stones at him and chased him out. The Prophet ﷺ walked away, full of sadness. Then he looked up and saw a cloud above him. Jibril was in it. (Bukhari 3231, Muslim 1795)
4. *The angel of the mountains.* Jibril said: "Allah has heard what your people said to you. He has sent you the angel of the mountains to do whatever you ask." The angel of the mountains greeted him. "If you wish, I will bring the two mountains together on them." The Prophet ﷺ said: "No. I hope that Allah will bring from their children people who worship Allah alone." (Bukhari 3231) Years later, the people of Ta'if did become Muslim.
5. Quiz: Why did the Prophet ﷺ go to Ta'if? **To call its people to Islam** · What did the angel of the mountains offer? **To crush the people between two mountains** · What did the Prophet ﷺ choose? **Mercy, hoping their children would worship Allah** · Mercy is only for people, not animals. **False**
6. Match: An animal = Put out water for the birds · A younger child = Teach them patiently · My parents = Do a chore without being asked · A classmate = Help with something they find hard · A stranger = Smile and hold the door · Someone who wronged me = Forgive them for Allah
7. Write: Do one act of mercy each day for a week, using the ideas you matched. Which day was easiest? Which was hardest, and why?

### 40. Character 7: Manners of eating

- **check:** The printed book names the narrator as Abdullah ibn Abd al-Asad, who was Umar's father (Abu Salamah). The hadith is narrated by Umar ibn Abi Salamah himself, so the narrator has been corrected.
1. Hadith: عَنْ عُمَرَ بْنِ أَبِي سَلَمَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ لِي رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: يَا غُلَامُ، سَمِّ اللَّهَ، وَكُلْ بِيَمِينِكَ، وَكُلْ مِمَّا يَلِيكَ "Young boy, say Bismillah, eat with your right hand, and eat from what is in front of you." (Bukhari 5376, Muslim 2022)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Three simple manners at every meal. They remind us that food is from Allah, and they show respect to the people eating with us.
3. *The boy who learned to eat.* Umar ibn Abi Salamah grew up in the house of the Prophet ﷺ. His mother was Umm Salamah, who married the Prophet ﷺ after Umar's father died. One day, as a young boy, Umar was eating with the Prophet ﷺ. His hand was moving all over the dish, taking food from every side. The Prophet ﷺ said to him: "Young boy, say Bismillah, eat with your right hand, and eat from what is in front of you." Umar said: "That is how I have eaten ever since." (Bukhari 5376) There was no shouting and no long lecture. The Prophet ﷺ taught him kindly, and he remembered it all his life.
4. Sort into Sunnah / Not the Sunnah: Saying Bismillah before eating (Sunnah) · Eating with my left hand (Not the Sunnah) · Eating from the side of the plate near me (Sunnah) · Saying the food is horrible (Not the Sunnah) · Saying Alhamdulillah when I finish (Sunnah) · Blowing on hot food (Not the Sunnah) · Eating until I can hardly breathe (Not the Sunnah) · Sharing my food with others (Sunnah)
5. Quiz: Ahmad grabbed his food with his left hand. What should he do? **Eat with his right hand** · He complained that the rice was bland. What did the Prophet ﷺ do? **Never criticised food** · He stuffed himself until he could hardly breathe. What is the Sunnah? **A third for food, a third for drink, a third for air** · He left without a word. What should he say after eating? **Alhamdulillah**
6. Write: For your next three meals, check yourself. Did you say Bismillah and eat with your right hand, from in front of you? Did you sit down, avoid complaining and stop before you were too full? Did you say Alhamdulillah? How many did you remember? Which one will you work on?

### 41. Character 8: Tale-bearing

1. Hadith: عَنْ حُذَيْفَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَا يَدْخُلُ الْجَنَّةَ نَمَّامٌ "A tale-bearer will not enter Paradise." (Muslim 105, Bukhari 6056)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] A tale-bearer tells people what others said about them, to start a fight. "Guess what she said about you!" It breaks friendships, and the Prophet ﷺ warned against it strongly.
3. *The two graves.* Once the Prophet ﷺ passed by two graves. He said that the two people buried there were being punished, and for things that people think are small. "One of them did not protect himself from his urine. The other used to go about carrying tales between people." (Bukhari 216, Muslim 292) Then he took a fresh palm branch, split it in two, and planted one half on each grave. He hoped their punishment would be lightened while the branches stayed fresh.
4. *A mother's promise.* Abdullah ibn Amir was a little boy when the Prophet ﷺ visited his home. His mother called him: "Come here, I will give you something." The Prophet ﷺ asked her: "What did you mean to give him?" "Some dates," she said. The Prophet ﷺ said: "If you had not given him anything, a lie would have been written against you." (Abu Dawud 4991) Our words count, even little ones to a small child. Carrying tales and breaking promises are both wrong uses of the tongue.
5. Quiz: Your friend says, "Sara said your drawing was ugly. Go and tell her off!" **Not pass it on, and say "Let's not spread this"** · You hear a rumour about a classmate. **Keep it to myself and not spread it** · Which is a careful promise? **"I will try to help you with your project on Saturday."**
6. Fill the gap: A **tale-bearer** will not enter Paradise. · The mother promised her son some **dates**. · If she gave nothing, a **lie** would be written against her.
7. Write: List the promises you made in the last month and whether you kept them. For any you broke, what was the real reason? Then write one small promise you can make today and definitely keep.

### 42. Character 9: Guiding to goodness

- **check:** The printed book gives this hadith from Ibn Mas'ud. In Tirmidhi 2670 it is from Anas ibn Malik. The same meaning is in Muslim 1893 from Abu Mas'ud al-Ansari: "Whoever guides to good has a reward like the one who does it."
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسِ بْنِ مَالِكٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: الدَّالُّ عَلَى الْخَيْرِ كَفَاعِلِهِ "The one who points the way to good is like the one who does it." (Tirmidhi 2670)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] When you teach or encourage someone to do good, you get a reward like theirs, every time they do it. Their reward is not made any less.
3. *Abu Bakr the guide.* Abu Bakr did not keep Islam to himself. Soon after he became Muslim, he started inviting his friends. Through him, five great men came to the Prophet ﷺ and accepted Islam. (Sirah of Ibn Ishaq) They were Uthman ibn Affan, az-Zubayr ibn al-Awwam, Abdur-Rahman ibn Awf, Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas and Talhah ibn Ubaydillah. All five were among the ten promised Paradise. Think of all their prayers and charity! Abu Bakr has a reward like theirs. It works the other way too. The Prophet ﷺ said that whoever calls people to something wrong carries a sin like theirs. (Muslim 2674)
4. Quiz: How many great Companions came to Islam through Abu Bakr in this story? **Five** · You teach your little sister to say Bismillah. She says it 3 times a day for a year. How many times is that? **1,095** · Your friend shares a mean joke video and tells you to pass it on. **Not share it**
5. Order: I teach my brother a du'a for sleeping → My brother teaches it to his friend → His friend teaches it to her family → Her family says it every night
6. Write: If you had a thousand followers, what reminder would you post to guide them to something good? Write it in 50 words or fewer. Would it inspire you if you saw it?

### 43. Character 10: Make things easy

1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسِ بْنِ مَالِكٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: يَسِّرُوا وَلَا تُعَسِّرُوا، وَبَشِّرُوا وَلَا تُنَفِّرُوا "Make things easy and do not make them hard. Give good news and do not drive people away." (Bukhari 69, Muslim 1734)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] When we teach Islam or help someone, we start with what is easy and full of hope. Harshness pushes people away.
3. *Advice for two teachers.* The Prophet ﷺ sent Mu'adh ibn Jabal and Abu Musa al-Ash'ari to Yemen to teach. He told them: "Make things easy, and do not make them hard. Give good news, and do not drive people away. Work together, and do not argue." (Bukhari 3038) Aisha said: "Whenever the Prophet ﷺ had to choose between two things, he chose the easier one, unless it was a sin." (Bukhari 3560) He was gentle with the Bedouin in the masjid, and he made the prayer shorter when he heard a baby cry.
4. Ayah 2:185: "Allah wants ease for you, and He does not want hardship for you."
5. Quiz: A new Muslim asks you about prayer. Where do you start? **Show the simple steps, kindly** · Your friend makes a mistake in wudu. **Show him gently, in private** · Your cousin has stopped praying. What might bring him back? **Inviting him to pray with you, and talking about Allah's mercy**
6. Write: Explain each of these for a six-year-old, in two sentences or fewer: What is tawhid? Why do we fast? What is the Day of Judgement? Why should we not lie? What is Jannah?

### 44. Unit 4 test: Good character (test)

1. Quiz: What is haya? **Modesty in how we act, speak and dress** · Which is one of the three signs of a hypocrite? **When he is trusted, he betrays** · What does al-Amin mean? **The trustworthy one** · What did the Prophet ﷺ do at Ta'if when he could have had the people punished? **He chose mercy** · Mercy is only for humans, not animals. **False** · Eating with the left hand is the Sunnah. **False** · Guiding someone to good earns you a reward like theirs. **True** · It is better to make a big promise even if you cannot keep it. **False**
2. Fill the gap: The believer with the most complete faith has the best **character**. · Modesty is a branch of **faith**. · Whoever is not merciful to people, Allah will not be **merciful** to him. · Say Bismillah, eat with your **right** hand. · Make things **easy**, and do not make them hard.
3. Match: Uthman ibn Affan = Even the angels were shy of him · Mu'adh ibn Jabal = Sent to Yemen to teach · Abu Jandal = Sent back to keep the treaty · Umar ibn Abi Salamah = Learned the manners of eating · Ali ibn Abi Talib = Returned the trusts on the night of the Hijrah · Abu Bakr = Brought five friends to Islam
4. Match: الْحَيَاءُ = modesty · أَمَانَةَ = trustworthiness · عَهْدَ = promise · نَمَّامٌ = tale-bearer · يَسِّرُوا = make things easy · بِيَمِينِكَ = with your right hand
5. Write: Which part of your character do you most need to work on after this unit? Write your plan to improve it.

### 45. Knowledge 1: Seeking knowledge

1. *Seeking general knowledge and Islamic knowledge.* The last part of this book is about learning. The first word of the Qur'an to be revealed was "Iqra": read! Islam asks us to learn about Allah and our religion, and about the world He made. Every lesson learned for Allah brings you closer to Him.
- **check:** The printed book has "من خرج في طلب العلم فهو في سبيل الله حتى يرجع" from Anas (Tirmidhi 2647). That narration is weak, so this page gives Muslim 2699, which the book quotes in this chapter's story and activity.
2. Hadith: عَنْ أَبِي هُرَيْرَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: مَنْ سَلَكَ طَرِيقًا يَلْتَمِسُ فِيهِ عِلْمًا سَهَّلَ اللَّهُ لَهُ بِهِ طَرِيقًا إِلَى الْجَنَّةِ "Whoever follows a path looking for knowledge, Allah will make easy for him a path to Paradise." (Muslim 2699)
3. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Going to class, reading a good book and asking a teacher a question are all steps on that path. Allah makes the road to Paradise easy for the one who walks it. The Prophet ﷺ also said: "Seeking knowledge is a duty on every Muslim." (Ibn Majah 224)
4. *Anas, the boy who learned.* When Anas ibn Malik was about ten, his mother Umm Sulaym brought him to the Prophet ﷺ. "This is Anas, to serve you," she said. "Pray to Allah for him." The Prophet ﷺ prayed: "O Allah, give him lots of wealth and children, and bless him in what You give him." (Bukhari 6334) For ten years Anas stayed close to the Prophet ﷺ. He watched, listened and remembered. He became one of the Companions who told the most hadith: more than two thousand. He lived past a hundred, and students came from far away to learn from him. The Companions loved knowledge so much that Jabir ibn Abdullah travelled for a month to hear one hadith from another Companion. (Bukhari, Book of Knowledge)
5. Quiz: Who brought Anas to the Prophet ﷺ? **His mother, Umm Sulaym** · About how many hadith did Anas pass on? **More than two thousand** · How long did Jabir travel for one hadith? **A month** · What does Allah make easy for someone who follows a path to knowledge? **A path to Paradise**
6. Order: Make the intention to learn for Allah → Go to class and listen carefully → Ask the teacher about what I did not understand → Practise what I learned → Teach it to someone else
7. Write: Write five things you want to learn this month, about Islam or about the world, and how you will learn each one. Then ask an older person: "What is the most important thing you ever learned?" Write their answer.

### 46. Knowledge 2: Wisdom

- **check:** The printed book has "الحكمة ضالة المؤمن حيث وجدها أخذها" (Tirmidhi 2687 has a similar wording). That narration is weak, so this page gives a sound hadith about wisdom (Bukhari 73).
1. Hadith: عَنْ عَبْدِ اللَّهِ بْنِ مَسْعُودٍ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: لَا حَسَدَ إِلَّا فِي اثْنَتَيْنِ: رَجُلٌ آتَاهُ اللَّهُ مَالًا فَسُلِّطَ عَلَى هَلَكَتِهِ فِي الْحَقِّ، وَرَجُلٌ آتَاهُ اللَّهُ الْحِكْمَةَ فَهُوَ يَقْضِي بِهَا وَيُعَلِّمُهَا "You may only wish to be like two people: a man whom Allah gave wealth and who spends it on what is right, and a man whom Allah gave wisdom and who judges by it and teaches it." (Bukhari 73, Muslim 816)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Wisdom is knowing what is right and doing it at the right time, in the right way. A wise person uses it and passes it on.
3. *Luqman the wise.* Luqman was not a prophet, but Allah gave him wisdom. A whole surah of the Qur'an is named after him. Allah tells us the advice Luqman gave his son. It starts with the most important thing of all: worship Allah alone.
4. Ayah 31:13: "And when Luqman said to his son, advising him: "O my son, do not join anything with Allah in worship. Joining others with Him is a terrible wrong.""
5. Ayah 31:17: ""O my son, keep up the prayer, tell people to do what is right and stop them from what is wrong, and be patient with whatever happens to you. That takes real determination.""
6. Ayah 31:19: ""Walk at a moderate pace and lower your voice. The ugliest of sounds is the braying of donkeys.""
7. Match: Do not join anything with Allah = Tawhid · Keep up the prayer = Worship · Be patient with what happens to you = Patience · Do not walk proudly on the earth = Humility · Lower your voice = Gentle manners
8. Quiz: "My friends make fun of me for praying at school. Should I stop?" **No. Keep praying and be patient, as Luqman told his son** · "I got the top mark in class. Now I feel better than everyone." **Thank Allah and stay humble** · "My older brother keeps bossing me around. I want to shout at him." **Lower my voice and talk to him calmly** · Luqman was a prophet. **False**
9. Write: Imagine a younger student writes to you with a problem. Write the problem, then write your reply using some of Luqman's advice.

### 47. Knowledge 3: A light for the believer

- **check:** The printed book has "اغد عالما أو متعلما أو مستمعا أو محبا ولا تكن الخامسة فتهلك" from Abu Bakrah. That narration is very weak, so this page gives a sound hadith on the value of knowledge (Bukhari 71). The book's idea is kept on the next page: be a scholar, a student, a listener or a lover of knowledge.
1. Hadith: عَنْ مُعَاوِيَةَ قَالَ: قَالَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ ﷺ: مَنْ يُرِدِ اللَّهُ بِهِ خَيْرًا يُفَقِّهْهُ فِي الدِّينِ "When Allah wants good for someone, He gives him understanding of the religion." (Bukhari 71, Muslim 1037)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Understanding Islam is a sign that Allah wants good for you. Knowledge is like a light: it shows you the way, so you do not trip in the dark. Try to always be one of these: someone who teaches, someone who learns, someone who listens, or someone who loves knowledge.
3. *Ubayy ibn Ka'b.* Ubayy ibn Ka'b was one of the best reciters among the Companions. The Prophet ﷺ said: "The best reciter among them is Ubayy." (Tirmidhi 3790) One day the Prophet ﷺ said to him: "Allah has told me to recite Surah al-Bayyinah to you." "Did Allah mention me by name?" asked Ubayy. "Yes," said the Prophet ﷺ. And Ubayy wept. (Bukhari 4959, Muslim 799) He spent his life with the Qur'an: learning it, understanding it and teaching it.
4. *Knowledge is light.* The Prophet ﷺ said: "The Qur'an is a proof for you or against you." (Muslim 223) He said: "Read the Qur'an, for it will come on the Day of Resurrection to speak up for its companions." (Muslim 804) Someone without knowledge is like a person walking in the dark. Knowledge lights up the road, so you can see the traps of Shaytan and the way to Jannah.
5. Ayah 6:122: "Is someone who was dead, and We gave him life and a light to walk by among people, like someone in darkness who cannot get out of it?"
6. Quiz: Ahmad never makes du'a after prayer because nobody taught him any. With knowledge he would... **Learn some du'as and say them after prayer** · Fatima backbites a classmate. She does not know how serious it is. With knowledge she would... **Stop, because backbiting is a serious sin** · Yusuf skips Fajr because he thinks only some prayers are fard. With knowledge he would... **Pray all five, because all five are fard** · Why did Ubayy weep? **Allah had mentioned him by name**
7. Write: Think of something about Islam you did not know before, and then learned. How did it change what you do? Now pick one topic you are still "in the dark" about. Who or what will you learn it from, and by when?

### 48. Knowledge 4: Clear communication

- **check:** The printed book gives "ما أنت بمحدث قوما حديثا لا تبلغه عقولهم إلا كان لبعضهم فتنة" as words of the Prophet ﷺ. It is a saying of Ibn Mas'ud in the introduction to Sahih Muslim, so it is shown under the card as his words. The card gives a sound hadith on clear speech (Bukhari 95).
1. Hadith: عَنْ أَنَسٍ عَنِ النَّبِيِّ ﷺ أَنَّهُ كَانَ إِذَا تَكَلَّمَ بِكَلِمَةٍ أَعَادَهَا ثَلَاثًا حَتَّى تُفْهَمَ عَنْهُ "When he said something, he would repeat it three times so that it was understood." (Bukhari 95)
2. *The words of the hadith.* [table] Good speech is clear speech. Speak so people can follow you, use words they understand, and repeat what matters.
3. *The Prophet's clear speech.* Aisha described how the Prophet ﷺ spoke: "He did not rush his words the way you do. He spoke clearly, so anyone sitting with him could remember it." (Tirmidhi 3639) If someone had wanted to count his words, they could have counted them. (Bukhari 3568) He did not use difficult words to impress people. He spoke slowly and clearly, and he repeated important things. He also said: "Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day, let him say something good or keep quiet." (Bukhari 6018)
4. *Why it matters.* Unclear words cause arguments. Someone says one thing, and someone else hears something quite different. Speaking clearly shows respect. It tells the other person: "I care that you understand me." So think before you speak, choose your words carefully, and speak at a pace people can follow.
5. Quiz: Which is clearest? **"Can we go to the park tomorrow after Asr?"** · Which is clearest? **"I am upset because you didn't wait for me after school."** · Which is clearest? **"Please tell Bilal that Aunt Sara wants his family to come on Friday."** · How many times did the Prophet ﷺ often repeat important words? **Three**
6. Write: Write one message you really want your family to understand. Then write it three ways: as it is, shorter, and as a question.

### 49. Unit 5 test: Seeking knowledge (test)

1. Quiz: Which Companion served the Prophet ﷺ for ten years and passed on over two thousand hadith? **Anas ibn Malik** · Luqman was a prophet of Allah. **False** · Which of these is advice Luqman gave his son? **Lower your voice** · Why did Ubayy ibn Ka'b weep? **Allah had mentioned him by name to the Prophet ﷺ** · Why did the Prophet ﷺ repeat important words three times? **So that they were understood** · When Allah wants good for someone, what does He give him? **Understanding of the religion**
2. Fill the gap: Seeking knowledge is a **duty** on every Muslim. · Whoever follows a path looking for knowledge, Allah will make easy for him a path to **Paradise**. · Let him say something good or keep **quiet**. · The Qur'an is a **proof** for you or against you.
3. Match: عِلْمًا = knowledge · طَرِيقًا = a path · الْحِكْمَةَ = wisdom · الدِّينِ = the religion · ثَلَاثًا = three times
4. Write: How will you use what you learned in this unit in your daily life? Write about knowledge, wisdom and the way you speak.

## Tawheed: Islamic Monotheism (Level 4, ages 10–14)

Volume One of the school's Tawheed course: six units on the Oneness of Allah, from the three categories of Tawheed to living it every day, with ayat, hadith, activities and a test after each unit.

### 1. A letter to the student

1. *Dear student.* Welcome to this book about Tawheed. It is the most important knowledge in Islam, because everything else in our religion is built on it. Tawheed means believing that Allah is One. He has no partner and no equal. No one shares in His power, His knowledge or His creation. He is perfect, and He is unlike anything we can imagine. When you understand Tawheed, your heart grows stronger. You know who your Lord is. You know you can turn to Him at any moment. That knowledge changes the way you see the world and the way you live each day. You start to worship Him sincerely. You obey Him gladly. You trust Him with whatever happens. This book was written for you, so that you can learn, think, and grow closer to Allah.
2. *What is in this book.* The book has six units. Each unit has four lessons and ends with a test. [table] In every lesson you will find explanations, examples from real life, stories from Islamic history, and ayat of the Qur'an and hadith with their meanings. There are activities to do and questions to think about. O Allah, make this knowledge of benefit to us. Help us to understand it, remember it and act on it. Make us among those who worship You alone and follow Your guidance. Ameen.
3. Write: What do you already know about Tawheed? Write two or three sentences. Then write one question you hope this book will answer.

### 2. 1.1 What is Tawheed?

1. *Unit One: introduction to Tawheed.* This unit is about the most important knowledge in Islam. You will learn what Tawheed means and why it is the foundation of the religion. You will see how the scholars explain Tawheed in three categories. Then you will learn about the blessings that come from living by it. Lesson 1: What is Tawheed?; Lesson 2: Why were we created?; Lesson 3: The three categories of Tawheed; Lesson 4: The blessings and fruits of Tawheed; Unit One test
2. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define Tawheed correctly; explain the Arabic root of the word Tawheed; explain La ilaha illallah in your own words
3. *Words to know.* [table]
4. *The meaning of Tawheed.* The word Tawheed comes from the Arabic verb wahhada, which means "to make one". To do tawheed of something is to single it out, so that nothing else shares with it. In Islam, Tawheed means believing that Allah is the One and only God. All worship belongs to Him. We love Him, obey Him and rely on Him above everything else. Tawheed is to single out Allah alone in His Lordship, in worship, and in His Names and Attributes. The opposite of Tawheed is shirk. Shirk is giving someone or something a share in what belongs only to Allah, such as praying to an idol.
5. Ayah 112:1: "Say: He is Allah, the One."
6. Ayah 21:25: "And We did not send any messenger before you except that We revealed to him: there is no god but Me, so worship Me."
7. *The statement of Tawheed.* لَا إِلَٰهَ إِلَّا ٱللَّهُ La ilaha illallah means: there is no god worthy of worship except Allah. It has two parts. [table] Both parts are needed. Someone who says that Allah is God but also prays to idols has not said "la ilaha". Someone who rejects idols but does not worship Allah has not said "illa Allah".
8. *Ibrahim searches for the truth.* The Qur'an tells us that the people of Prophet Ibrahim worshipped idols, and so did his father. Ibrahim did not follow them because everyone else did. Surah al-An'am (6:74 to 79) describes how he looked at a star, then the moon, then the sun. Each one rose and then set. A thing that disappears cannot be the Lord of the worlds. So Ibrahim turned away from everything his people worshipped. He turned to the One who created the heavens and the earth.
9. Ayah 6:79: "Indeed, I have turned my face to the One who created the heavens and the earth, inclining to the truth, and I am not one of those who associate partners with Allah."
10. Fill the gap: The Arabic root of the word Tawheed is **wahhada**. · Tawheed is explained in **three** categories. · "La ilaha", which denies every false god, is the **negation** part of the statement. · "Illa Allah", which says that Allah alone deserves worship, is the **affirmation** part. · Giving Allah a partner in worship is called **shirk**.
11. Quiz: Tawheed means believing in many gods. **False** · All the prophets came to teach Tawheed. **True** · Ibadah (worship) is part of Tawheed. **True** · Shirk means giving Allah a partner in what belongs only to Him. **True** · Ibrahim followed the idols of his people without thinking. **False**
12. Match: Tawheed = Singling out Allah alone · Ilah = Anything that is worshipped · Ibadah = Worship · Shirk = Giving Allah a partner · Wahhada = To make one
13. Write: In three or four sentences, write what you learn from the story of Ibrahim about finding the truth.

### 3. 1.2 Why were we created?

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: answer the question of why Allah created us; understand Surah adh-Dhariyat 51:56; connect Tawheed to the purpose of life
2. Ayah 51:56: "And I did not create the jinn and mankind except to worship Me."
3. *The greatest question.* Things that are made have a purpose. A pencil is made for writing. A key is made to open a lock. So what about us? Why did Allah create us? Allah gives the answer in the Qur'an. He created jinn and human beings to worship Him. That is the greatest purpose of our lives. Worship is much wider than prayer. It covers everything we do to please Allah, done with love and obedience, for Him alone. [table]
4. *What does this have to do with Tawheed?.* We were created to worship Allah.; Worship must be sincere, for Allah alone.; This sincerity is Tawheed. So the purpose of our life and Tawheed are joined together. We live to worship Allah, and Tawheed means worshipping Him alone. Allah does not need our worship. We are the ones who need Him. When we worship Him, the good of it comes back to us.
5. Hadith: حَقُّ اللَّهِ عَلَى عِبَادِهِ أَنْ يَعْبُدُوهُ وَلَا يُشْرِكُوا بِهِ شَيْئًا، وَحَقُّ الْعِبَادِ عَلَى اللَّهِ أَنْ لَا يُعَذِّبَ مَنْ لَا يُشْرِكُ بِهِ شَيْئًا "The right of Allah over His servants is that they worship Him and do not associate anything with Him. The right of the servants over Allah is that He does not punish anyone who does not associate anything with Him." (Sahih al-Bukhari 2856 and Sahih Muslim 30)
- **check:** Salman's story is summarised from his own account in Musnad Ahmad 23737 (graded hasan). The textbook's line that "his heart found peace when he heard the Qur'an" is not in that account, so it is left out.
6. *Salman al-Farisi: the man who searched for the truth.* Salman grew up in Persia, in a family that worshipped fire. His father loved him so much that he kept him at home to tend the fire, so that it never went out. One day Salman passed a church and saw Christians at prayer. He liked their worship more than the fire worship of his people. He left home and went to Syria to learn their religion. He served one monk after another. As each one was dying, Salman asked whom he should follow next. The last monk told him that a prophet would soon appear in Arabia with the religion of Ibrahim. He would move to a land of palm trees. On the way to Arabia, Salman was betrayed and sold as a slave. He was taken to Madinah, a land of palm trees. When the Prophet ﷺ arrived there, Salman went to him and saw the signs the monk had described. He accepted Islam. Salman left his home and his comfort because he wanted the truth more than anything. He did not follow his people blindly.
7. Order: He grows up in a fire-worshipping family in Persia → He sees Christians praying and leaves home → He serves one monk after another → The last monk tells him about a prophet in Arabia → He is sold as a slave and taken to Madinah → He meets the Prophet ﷺ and accepts Islam
8. Quiz: Why did Allah create jinn and human beings? **To worship Him** · Which sentence about Allah is true? **He does not need anything from us** · How can schoolwork become worship? **By doing it well with the intention of pleasing Allah** · What is "the right of Allah over His servants"? **That they worship Him and do not associate anything with Him**
9. Write: What is the main reason we were created? How can your schoolwork and daily activities become acts of worship? How does believing in Tawheed change the way you live each day?

### 4. 1.3 The three categories of Tawheed

1. *Lesson objectives.* Allah is One. He alone is our Lord. He alone deserves worship. He alone has perfect Names and Attributes. To help us understand our belief clearly, the scholars of Ahl as-Sunnah explain Tawheed in three categories. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: name the three categories of Tawheed; define each category; give an example of each from daily life; understand how the three categories work together
2. *Words to know.* [table]
3. *1. Tawheed ar-Rububiyyah: Lordship.* This is believing that Allah alone is the Creator and Owner of everything. He provides for all of creation. He controls and runs every affair. When it rains, we know that Allah sent the rain. When plants grow, we know that Allah gave them life and made them grow.
4. Ayah 1:2: "All praise is for Allah, Lord of the worlds."
5. *2. Tawheed al-Uluhiyyah: worship.* This is believing that Allah alone deserves every act of worship, and then worshipping Him alone. We pray only to Allah. We make du'a only to Him. We put our hope and our trust in Him alone.
6. Ayah 1:5: "You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help."
7. *3. Tawheed al-Asma was-Sifat: Names and Attributes.* This is believing in Allah's perfect Names and Attributes as they come in the Qur'an and the Sunnah. We do not deny them or change their meaning. We do not say they are like the qualities of anything He created. We believe Allah is Al-'Alim, the All-Knowing, and As-Sami', the All-Hearing. Each of these is perfect, in a way that belongs to Him alone. [table]
8. *A lesson from the time of the Prophet ﷺ.* Many Arabs in Makkah in the time of the Prophet ﷺ believed that Allah created everything. The Qur'an tells us what they would answer if they were asked.
9. Ayah 31:25: "And if you ask them who created the heavens and the earth, they will surely say: Allah."
10. *Why that was not enough.* They believed that Allah was the Creator and the Provider. Yet they still worshipped idols. They called on them, asked them for help and offered sacrifices to them. So believing in Allah's Lordship on its own did not make them Muslims. Tawheed is complete when a person believes in Allah as Lord, worships Him alone, and affirms His perfect Names and Attributes. Allah created me and provides for me: that is Lordship. So I pray and make du'a only to Him: that is worship. I call on Him as Ar-Rahman, the Most Merciful: that is His Names and Attributes.
11. Sort into Lordship (Rububiyyah) / Worship (Uluhiyyah) / Names and Attributes: Allah created the heavens and the earth. (Lordship (Rububiyyah)) · I pray only to Allah. (Worship (Uluhiyyah)) · Allah is the All-Knowing. (Names and Attributes) · I ask Allah for help. (Worship (Uluhiyyah)) · Allah provides food and water. (Lordship (Rububiyyah)) · Allah is the All-Hearing. (Names and Attributes) · Rain falls by Allah's will. (Lordship (Rububiyyah)) · I put my trust in Allah alone. (Worship (Uluhiyyah)) · Allah is Ar-Rahman, the Most Merciful. (Names and Attributes)
12. Quiz: The people of Makkah believed Allah was the Creator. Why were they still not upon Tawheed? **They worshipped idols as well as Allah** · Which category is about doing every act of worship for Allah alone? **Tawheed al-Uluhiyyah** · Which is the right way to believe in Allah's Attributes? **As they come in the Qur'an and Sunnah, without likening them to creation**
13. Write: Write one example from your own day for each category: Lordship, worship, and Names and Attributes.
14. Write: Explain to a friend why all three categories of Tawheed are needed. Use the example of the people of Makkah.

### 5. 1.4 The blessings and fruits of Tawheed

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: list some of the blessings of Tawheed; understand how Tawheed affects daily life; see why Tawheed comes first in Islam
2. *Tawheed is something you live.* Tawheed is the foundation of our faith. It is also the way to a happy life. It brings great blessings in this world and in the Hereafter. Four of them are below. When you worship only Allah, your heart rests in Him. You stop worrying about pleasing people or winning their approval. Allah promises forgiveness and Paradise to those who hold on to Tawheed and avoid shirk. Tawheed gives us real honour. We bow to no one except the One who created everything. Tawheed brings Muslims together as one Ummah. We worship the same Lord and follow the same truth.
3. Ayah 6:82: "Those who believe and do not mix their belief with injustice: they will have security, and they are rightly guided."
4. *Which injustice?.* When this ayah came down, the Companions were worried, because everyone does some wrong. The Prophet ﷺ explained that the "injustice" here means shirk (Sahih al-Bukhari 32). So the security and guidance in this ayah are promised to the people of Tawheed.
5. Hadith: مَنْ مَاتَ وَهُوَ يَعْلَمُ أَنَّهُ لَا إِلَهَ إِلَّا اللَّهُ دَخَلَ الْجَنَّةَ "Whoever dies knowing that there is no god but Allah will enter Paradise." (Sahih Muslim 26)
- **check:** "Ahad, Ahad" is in Sunan Ibn Majah 150 (graded hasan). The rock on his chest and his master Umayyah ibn Khalaf come from Ibn Ishaq's Sirah. Please check the summary.
6. *Bilal ibn Rabah: "One, One".* Bilal ibn Rabah was a slave in Makkah when he accepted Islam. His master, Umayyah ibn Khalaf, was furious that Bilal now worshipped Allah alone. The idol-worshippers tortured Bilal to make him leave Islam. They laid him on the burning sand in the midday sun. Ibn Ishaq reports that Umayyah had a heavy rock placed on his chest. He told Bilal he would stay that way until he left the religion of Muhammad ﷺ. Bilal kept repeating one word: "Ahad, Ahad." It means "One, One." He was declaring that Allah is One. The pain did not change his words. Later, Abu Bakr bought Bilal and set him free, and Bilal became the first mu'adhdhin of Islam.
7. Match: Peace in the heart = You stop worrying about pleasing people · Forgiveness and Paradise = Allah's promise to those who avoid shirk · Dignity and honour = You bow to no one but your Creator · Unity = Muslims are one Ummah with one Lord
8. Quiz: In Surah al-An'am 6:82, what does "injustice" mean? **Shirk** · What did Bilal say while he was being tortured? **"Ahad, Ahad": One, One** · How does Tawheed bring Muslims together? **They all worship the same Lord and follow the same truth**
9. Write: Write two benefits of Tawheed that you feel in your life, or that you would like to feel.
10. Write: How does believing in Tawheed bring peace to our hearts? What does Bilal's story teach you about courage and faith? How does Tawheed help Muslims stay united?

### 6. Unit One test (test)

1. *Before you start.* This test covers Unit One: what Tawheed is, why we were created, the three categories of Tawheed and its blessings. Read each question carefully. Do your best and trust Allah.
2. Quiz: What is the meaning of La ilaha illallah? **There is no god worthy of worship except Allah** · What is the main purpose of our creation? **To worship Allah alone** · In how many categories is Tawheed explained? **Three** · Which of these is a fruit of Tawheed? **Peace in the heart** · The Arabic root of the word Tawheed means: **To make one** · The people of Makkah believed Allah was the Creator. What was missing? **They did not worship Allah alone**
3. Quiz: Tawheed means believing that Allah alone deserves worship. **True** · Tawheed ar-Rububiyyah means that Allah alone is our Creator and Provider. **True** · Tawheed al-Uluhiyyah means we worship Allah alone. **True** · Shirk means associating partners with Allah in what belongs to Him. **True** · Believing in Tawheed has no effect on how we live. **False**
4. Match: Tawheed ar-Rububiyyah = Oneness of Allah in His Lordship · Tawheed al-Uluhiyyah = Oneness of Allah in worship · Tawheed al-Asma was-Sifat = Oneness of Allah in His Names and Attributes · Ibadah = Everything Allah loves and is pleased with · Shirk = Giving Allah a partner
5. Write: Write a clear definition of each term: Tawheed, Tawheed ar-Rububiyyah, Tawheed al-Uluhiyyah.
6. Write: Allah says: "And I did not create the jinn and mankind except to worship Me" (51:56). Explain in your own words what this ayah teaches about why we were created. Then describe the three categories of Tawheed with an example of each.
7. Write: Bilal's story shows great trust in Allah. What can you learn from his faith and courage that you can use in your own life?

### 7. 2.1 Allah, the Creator

1. *Unit Two: Tawheed ar-Rububiyyah.* This unit is about believing in Allah as the only Lord. He creates, He provides, and He plans and controls everything. He gives life and He takes it. You will look at the world He made and the way He looks after it. You will also see how this belief changes the way we work, pray and trust. Lesson 1: Allah, the Creator; Lesson 2: Allah, the Provider; Lesson 3: Allah, the Planner and Controller; Lesson 4: Allah, the Giver of life and death; Unit Two test
2. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define creation; give evidence from the Qur'an that Allah is the Creator; compare Allah's creation with things that people make
3. Ayah 39:62: "Allah is the Creator of all things, and He is the Guardian over all things."
4. *What does it mean that Allah is the Creator?.* To create means to bring something into being from nothing. Allah needed no material and no helper. He created everything by His will alone. Look at the sky and the mountains. Look at the trees, the oceans and the stars. Think about your own body: your heartbeat, your senses, and your ability to learn and to love. None of this came about by chance. All of it points to one perfect Creator.
5. Ayah 36:82: "His command, when He wills a thing, is only that He says to it, "Be," and it is."
6. *Allah's creation and human making.* People make things too. But what people make is very different from what Allah creates. [table] A carpenter shapes wood, but he did not create the tree. An engineer builds a phone, but he did not create the metal, the light or the mind he used to design it.
7. Ayah 16:20: "And those they call on besides Allah create nothing, and they themselves are created."
8. Ayah 22:73: "Indeed, those you call on besides Allah will never create a fly, even if they all gathered together to do it."
9. *The scientist who found Allah in a lab.* Dr Yasir loved science. He spent years studying cells, the tiny building blocks of our bodies. Under his microscope he saw a whole world. He saw mitochondria, tiny power stations that give the cell energy. He saw the nucleus, which stores information like a perfect library. Other parts carried messages, built new materials or recycled old ones. Every cell was like a busy city where everything worked together. Dr Yasir tried to build even a simple living cell in the lab. However hard he tried, he could not create life. He saw that one human cell is far more complex than any machine or computer. He looked up from his microscope and smiled. "This could not happen by itself," he said. "It must have been created by the All-Knowing, All-Powerful Creator." The more he learned about creation, the more he saw the greatness of the One who created it all.
10. Sort into Allah's creation / Human making: Created from nothing (Allah's creation) · Made from materials that already exist (Human making) · Perfect, without any flaw (Allah's creation) · Can break or wear out (Human making) · A living cell (Allah's creation) · A computer (Human making) · The sun and the stars (Allah's creation) · A wooden table (Human making)
11. Quiz: What does "to create" mean? **To bring something into being from nothing** · What does Surah al-Hajj 22:73 say the false gods could never create? **A fly** · What did Dr Yasir find when he tried to build a living cell? **He could not create life** · How does Allah bring a thing into being? **He says to it "Be," and it is**
12. Write: Look around you today. Name five things Allah created that you can see. Next to each one, write one thought about it.
13. Write: How does learning about the universe increase your awe of Allah? Why do you think the things people worship besides Allah cannot create even a tiny fly?

### 8. 2.2 Allah, the Provider

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: understand the Name Ar-Razzaq; tell the difference between provision for the body and provision for the heart; explain how our effort and Allah's provision fit together; show gratitude for what Allah gives
2. Ayah 51:58: "Indeed, it is Allah who is the Provider, the Possessor of strength, the Firm."
3. *Ar-Razzaq, the One who provides.* Ar-Razzaq is one of Allah's beautiful Names. It means the One who provides for all of creation. He gives without limit, and He gives even when no one asks. No one can provide anything except by His permission. Every bird, fish and insect eats from what He provides. [table]
4. Ayah 11:6: "And there is no creature on the earth except that its provision is from Allah. He knows where it lives and where it is kept. All is in a clear record."
5. *If Allah is the Provider, why do we work?.* Working hard is part of our duty. We plan, study and work, using the means Allah has given us. But the provision itself does not come from our effort. It comes from Allah. We do our part, and we trust Allah for the result. [table]
6. Hadith: لَوْ أَنَّكُمْ كُنْتُمْ تَوَكَّلُونَ عَلَى اللَّهِ حَقَّ تَوَكُّلِهِ لَرُزِقْتُمْ كَمَا تُرْزَقُ الطَّيْرُ، تَغْدُو خِمَاصًا وَتَرُوحُ بِطَانًا "If you were to rely on Allah as He should be relied on, He would provide for you as He provides for the birds. They go out in the morning hungry and come back in the evening full." (Jami at-Tirmidhi 2344 and Sunan Ibn Majah 4164)
7. *Thanking Allah for His provision.* We did not create our food, our water or our homes. We did not give them to ourselves. Allah gives them out of His mercy. When we see that everything comes from Him, our hearts fill with gratitude, and our worship grows stronger. We show shukr (gratitude) for what Allah provides when we: thank Allah with our hearts and our tongues; spend in ways that please Allah; avoid waste; share with people in need We also say the du'a the Prophet ﷺ taught for these moments: Bismillah before eating, Alhamdulillah after it.
- **check:** Graded hasan. The textbook gives a shorter "Alhamdulillah"; this is the full du'a taught by the Prophet ﷺ.
8. Du'a (After eating): الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ الَّذِي أَطْعَمَنِي هَذَا وَرَزَقَنِيهِ مِنْ غَيْرِ حَوْلٍ مِنِّي وَلَا قُوَّةٍ / Alhamdu lillahil-ladhi at'amani hadha wa razaqanihi min ghayri hawlin minni wa la quwwah / "All praise is for Allah, who fed me this and provided it for me without any power or strength from me." (Sunan Abi Dawud 4023 and Jami at-Tirmidhi 3458)
- **check:** The textbook has "Alhamdulillah 'ala al-matar"; it is replaced with the du'a the Prophet ﷺ said when it rained.
9. Du'a (When it rains): اللَّهُمَّ صَيِّبًا نَافِعًا / Allahumma sayyiban nafi'a / "O Allah, make it a beneficial rain." (Sahih al-Bukhari 1032)
10. Sort into For the body / For the heart: Food (For the body) · Guidance (For the heart) · A home (For the body) · Faith (For the heart) · Health (For the body) · Useful knowledge (For the heart) · Clean water (For the body) · Patience (For the heart)
11. Quiz: What does Ar-Razzaq mean? **The One who provides for all creation** · A farmer plants seeds and waters them. Who makes them grow? **Allah** · What does the hadith of the birds teach? **Make an effort and trust Allah for the result**
12. Write: Write five blessings Allah has given you. Next to each one, write how you will thank Him for it.

### 9. 2.3 Allah, the Planner and Controller

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: understand that Allah controls everything; find examples of Allah's planning in nature; understand tawakkul, trust in Allah
2. *What is tadbir?.* Tadbir means Allah's perfect planning and managing of every affair. Nothing happens by chance. Allah knows everything, plans everything and controls everything, with perfect wisdom.
3. Ayah 32:5: "He arranges every affair from the heaven to the earth."
4. *Signs of Allah's control in the world.* Allah turns the earth, bringing day and night. He knows when each will come and how long it will last. Allah sends rain from the sky, stores it in rivers and oceans, then raises it again into the clouds. He provides water for all life. Your heart beats without you telling it to. Allah keeps it going every second to keep you alive. Allah holds everything in perfect order. The earth stays in its path, and we stay on the earth.
5. *Four signs of Allah's control.* He creates and provides. The sun rises, plants grow and food reaches our tables by His will.; He brings benefit and harm. He sends rain that helps crops grow. He can also send a storm or an illness as a test.; He knows what is hidden. He knows what we think, what we say and what we hide.; He controls time and events. He decides when things happen. Not a leaf falls without His knowledge.
6. Ayah 6:59: "And not a leaf falls but that He knows it."
- **check:** Graded hasan by al-Albani; at-Tirmidhi called it gharib. The textbook tells this as a story about Umar ibn al-Khattab; the hadith is a question put to the Prophet ﷺ, as given here.
7. Hadith: اعْقِلْهَا وَتَوَكَّلْ "Tie it, and trust in Allah." (Jami at-Tirmidhi 2517)
8. *Effort and tawakkul.* Islam teaches a balance. We take the means (asbab) that Allah has created. We tie the camel, study for the exam and wear a seatbelt. Then we place our hearts in Allah's hands, because He is the One who protects and provides. That is real tawakkul. Lazy "tawakkul" means doing nothing and saying "Allah will sort it out". The Prophet ﷺ did not teach that.
9. Ayah 64:11: "No disaster strikes except by the permission of Allah."
10. Hadith: وَاعْلَمْ أَنَّ الْأُمَّةَ لَوِ اجْتَمَعَتْ عَلَى أَنْ يَنْفَعُوكَ بِشَيْءٍ لَمْ يَنْفَعُوكَ إِلَّا بِشَيْءٍ قَدْ كَتَبَهُ اللَّهُ لَكَ، وَلَوِ اجْتَمَعُوا عَلَى أَنْ يَضُرُّوكَ بِشَيْءٍ لَمْ يَضُرُّوكَ إِلَّا بِشَيْءٍ قَدْ كَتَبَهُ اللَّهُ عَلَيْكَ "Know that if the whole nation gathered together to benefit you with something, they could only benefit you with what Allah has already written for you. And if they gathered together to harm you with something, they could only harm you with what Allah has already written against you." (Jami at-Tirmidhi 2516)
11. Quiz: What does tadbir mean? **Allah's perfect planning and running of every affair** · What did the Prophet ﷺ tell the man with the camel? **Tie it, and trust in Allah** · Which is real tawakkul? **Revising for an exam, then trusting Allah with the result** · Who keeps your heart beating while you sleep? **Allah**
12. Fill the gap: Taking means like medicine or study is called taking the **asbab**. · Relying on Allah after doing your part is called **tawakkul**. · Not a **leaf** falls without Allah knowing it.
13. Write: When you study hard for an exam, how can you also put your trust in Allah? Think of a time you were ill and then got better: what does it teach you about Allah's control over our health? What is the difference between lazy tawakkul and real tawakkul?
14. Write: Choose one thing in nature that you can see around you: the sky, the seasons, the sea or something else. Describe it. Explain how it shows Allah's control and planning, and write how it makes you feel.

### 10. 2.4 Allah, the Giver of life and death

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: understand that life and death belong to Allah; believe in the resurrection; use your life well
- **check:** Al-Muhyi and Al-Mumit are in the list of names in Jami at-Tirmidhi 3507, whose list is weak. The meaning, that Allah gives life and causes death, is in many ayat (for example 10:56 and 30:40), and Allah is described as "muhyi al-mawta" in 30:50.
2. *Life and death belong to Allah.* [table] Allah gives life to everything that lives. Without Him, nothing could live for a moment. He also takes the souls of all living beings when their time comes. No one can give life or cause death except by His will.
3. Ayah 10:56: "It is He who gives life and causes death, and to Him you will be returned."
4. *The three stages of life.* [table]
5. Ayah 3:185: "Every soul will taste death."
6. Ayah 67:2: "He who created death and life to test you, which of you is best in deeds."
7. *Life is a trust.* Allah did not give us life for nothing. He created death and life to test which of us does the best deeds. Knowing that we will die, and be raised again, helps us live with purpose. It gives us hope, because Allah rewards every good deed. It also makes us careful, because we will answer for what we did. Surah ar-Rum 30:40 says that Allah created us, then provided for us, then will cause us to die, then will give us life again. It asks: can any of the false gods do any of that?
8. Ayah 30:40: "Allah is the One who created you, then provided for you, then will cause you to die, then will give you life."
9. Order: First life in this world → Death → Resurrection → Judgement for our deeds
10. Quiz: Who decides when a person dies? **Allah alone** · Why did Allah create death and life? **To test which of us does the best deeds** · What happens after death for every person? **They will be raised again and judged**
11. Write: Allah gave you life so you can do good. Write one good deed you will do, one useful skill you will learn, and one habit you will improve.
12. Write: How should believing that Allah gives life and causes death change your daily life? How does believing in the resurrection change the choices you make today?

### 11. Unit Two test (test)

1. *Before you start.* This test covers Unit Two: Allah as the Creator, the Provider, the Planner and Controller, and the Giver of life and death. Do your best and trust Allah.
2. Match: Ar-Razzaq = The Provider · Tadbir = Allah's planning and control of all affairs · Tawakkul = Trusting Allah after taking the means · Al-Muhyi = The Giver of life · Al-Mumit = The One who causes death
3. Quiz: Who provides for every living being? **Allah** · The belief that everything happens under Allah's control is called: **Tadbir** · We should take the means and then: **Trust Allah for the result** · What happens after death? **Resurrection and return to Allah**
4. Quiz: Allah is the only One who can create. **True** · Only Allah gives provision to His creation. **True** · Everything happens randomly, without any plan. **False** · After death, people will be brought back to life. **True** · We should rely only on the means and not on Allah. **False**
5. Write: 1. What does it mean that Allah is the Creator? Give an example from creation. 2. How does Allah provide for His creation? Give two examples. 3. What does it mean that Allah plans and controls everything? 4. How is Allah the Giver of life and the One who causes death?

### 12. 3.1 What is worship?

1. *Unit Three: Tawheed al-Uluhiyyah.* In this unit you will learn what worship (ibadah) means and why it belongs to Allah alone. You will see how two conditions protect our worship from mistakes: sincerity, and following the Sunnah. You will also learn how everyday actions can become worship when they are done for Allah. Lesson 1: What is worship?; Lesson 2: Sincerity: worshipping Allah alone; Lesson 3: Following the Messenger ﷺ; Lesson 4: Types of worship; Unit Three review and test
2. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define ibadah; explain why Allah alone deserves worship; find worship in daily life
3. *Ibadah (worship).* The scholar Ibn Taymiyyah gave a definition of worship that is often taught: ibadah is a name for everything Allah loves and is pleased with, from words and actions, inside and outside. Allah alone deserves worship because He alone is our Lord. He created us and He provides for us. It makes no sense to worship something that cannot create anything, or even hear a du'a. Prayer and fasting are worship. So is every act done for the sake of Allah, in the way He loves. Worship includes what is in our hearts, what we say, and what we do.
4. Ayah 6:162: "Say: Indeed, my prayer, my sacrifice, my living and my dying are for Allah, Lord of the worlds."
5. *Your whole life can be worship.* When you do something good with the right intention for Allah, it becomes worship. Study, work, helping others and being kind can all be acts of worship. [table]
- **check:** Sahih al-Bukhari 2272 and Sahih Muslim 2743. The textbook's version (three young men who slept in a cave for many years) mixes this hadith with the story of the People of the Cave; the hadith is told here instead.
6. *The three men in the cave.* The Prophet ﷺ told of three men from the people before us. They were travelling when they took shelter in a cave. A huge rock rolled down the mountain and closed the mouth of the cave. They could not move it. They said to each other that nothing would save them except calling on Allah by the good deeds they had done sincerely for Him. The first man had elderly parents. Every night he gave them their milk before anyone else. One night they fell asleep before he came. He stood holding the cup until dawn rather than wake them, while his own children cried from hunger. The second man had the chance to commit a serious sin. He left it out of fear of Allah. The third man had hired workers. One of them left without taking his wages. The man invested those wages until they grew into large herds of animals. Years later the worker returned, and he gave him all of it. After each du'a the rock moved a little. After the third, the mouth of the cave opened and they walked out.
7. Quiz: Which is the best definition of ibadah? **Everything Allah loves and is pleased with, from words and actions, inside and outside** · Why does Allah alone deserve worship? **He alone is our Lord, who created us and provides for us** · What saved the three men in the cave? **Calling on Allah by good deeds they had done sincerely for Him** · Can sleeping become worship? **Yes, if you rest so you can worship Allah and do good the next day**
8. Write: Write three everyday actions. Next to each one, write how you can do it for the sake of Allah.
9. Write: How can the things you do every day become worship when you do them for Allah?

### 13. 3.2 Sincerity: worshipping Allah alone

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define ikhlas; explain why worship must be for Allah alone; recognise the signs of sincerity
2. Ayah 98:5: "And they were not commanded except to worship Allah, being sincere to Him in religion."
3. *What is sincerity?.* Sincerity is called ikhlas in Arabic. It means doing all of our worship for Allah alone. When we are sincere, we want Allah's pleasure. We are not after praise or attention from people. Allah knows what is in our hearts. He accepts only sincere worship. [table]
4. Hadith: أَنَا أَغْنَى الشُّرَكَاءِ عَنِ الشِّرْكِ، مَنْ عَمِلَ عَمَلًا أَشْرَكَ فِيهِ مَعِي غَيْرِي تَرَكْتُهُ وَشِرْكَهُ "I am the One who least needs a partner. Whoever does a deed in which he associates someone else with Me, I leave him and his shirk." (Sahih Muslim 2985)
5. *For Allah or for people?.* Two people can do exactly the same action. One does it for Allah. The other does it to be seen. [table]
- **check:** The textbook has a made-up story of a rich man who gave in secret at night; it is replaced with this hadith, which teaches the same thing.
6. Hadith: وَرَجُلٌ تَصَدَّقَ بِصَدَقَةٍ فَأَخْفَاهَا حَتَّى لَا تَعْلَمَ شِمَالُهُ مَا تُنْفِقُ يَمِينُهُ "A man who gives in charity and hides it, so that his left hand does not know what his right hand gives." (Sahih al-Bukhari 1423 and Sahih Muslim 1031)
7. *Protecting our sincerity.* Sincerity is a precious gift from Allah. Shaytan tries to take it away by whispering in our hearts. So we have to protect it. Here are four ways. Remember that Allah sees the heart. He knows what is inside you, even when no one else does.; Ask Allah to keep your heart sincere. Make du'a for it often.; Hide some good deeds. Do some of them in secret, so that only Allah knows.; Accept correction. When someone corrects you, accept it gladly and improve for the sake of Allah. [table]
- **check:** Graded sahih by al-Albani. The textbook's du'a ("make all my actions righteous and sincere for Your sake") is a saying of Umar, not of the Prophet ﷺ, so this du'a is used instead.
8. Du'a (A du'a to protect us from hidden shirk): اللَّهُمَّ إِنِّي أَعُوذُ بِكَ أَنْ أُشْرِكَ بِكَ وَأَنَا أَعْلَمُ، وَأَسْتَغْفِرُكَ لِمَا لَا أَعْلَمُ / Allahumma inni a'udhu bika an ushrika bika wa ana a'lam, wa astaghfiruka lima la a'lam / "O Allah, I seek refuge in You from associating anything with You knowingly, and I ask Your forgiveness for what I do not know." (al-Adab al-Mufrad 716)
9. Sort into For Allah / For people: I help a friend and hope Allah is pleased. (For Allah) · I share my food so people will praise me. (For people) · I give charity quietly because Allah sees. (For Allah) · I pray longer only when the imam is watching. (For people) · I pray on time because I want Allah's reward. (For Allah) · I post my good deeds online to get likes. (For people) · I smile at others because the Prophet ﷺ called it charity. (For Allah)
10. Quiz: What is riya? **Doing good deeds to be seen or praised by people** · Is it sincere to pray because you want Allah's reward? **Yes, hoping for Allah's reward is part of sincerity** · Which of these helps protect sincerity? **Doing some good deeds in secret**
11. Write: Think of one good deed you can do in secret this week, for Allah alone. Write what it is and how you will do it. (You do not need to tell anyone!)
12. Write: Why do we sometimes want people to see our good deeds? How can you check your intention before you do something? Why is ikhlas so important in our worship?

### 14. 3.3 Following the Messenger ﷺ

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: explain why Muslims follow Prophet Muhammad ﷺ; tell the difference between worship taught by revelation and worship people invent; say what the Sunnah is
2. Ayah 33:21: "There has certainly been for you in the Messenger of Allah an excellent example."
3. *What is the Sunnah?.* The Sunnah is everything Prophet Muhammad ﷺ said, did or approved of. It explains the Qur'an and shows us how to live. It guides our beliefs, our worship and our character. Sincerity is the first condition for worship to be accepted. Following the Sunnah is the second. A deed must be for Allah alone, and it must be done the way the Prophet ﷺ taught.
4. *Why do we follow the Messenger ﷺ?.* Allah commanded us to obey the Messenger (Surah an-Nisa 4:59).; He taught us the true way to worship Allah.; He is the best example for us in every part of life.; Loving him and following him is part of our faith.
5. Ayah 4:59: "O you who believe, obey Allah and obey the Messenger."
6. Ayah 3:31: "Say: If you love Allah, then follow me; Allah will love you and forgive you your sins. And Allah is Forgiving and Merciful."
7. Hadith: صَلُّوا كَمَا رَأَيْتُمُونِي أُصَلِّي "Pray as you have seen me pray." (Sahih al-Bukhari 631)
8. Hadith: مَنْ أَحْدَثَ فِي أَمْرِنَا هَذَا مَا لَيْسَ فِيهِ فَهُوَ رَدٌّ "Whoever brings into this matter of ours something that is not part of it, it is rejected." (Sahih al-Bukhari 2697 and Sahih Muslim 1718)
9. *The Prophet's mercy to children.* Anas ibn Malik once passed by some children and greeted them with salam. He said that the Prophet ﷺ used to do the same (Sahih al-Bukhari 6247). The Prophet ﷺ did not ignore children. He greeted them, was gentle with them and showed them respect. Following his example means being kind to everyone, young and old.
10. *Following the Sunnah in daily life.* [table]
11. Sort into Taught by revelation / Invented by people: Praying the five daily prayers (Taught by revelation) · Adding a sixth daily prayer (Invented by people) · Fasting in Ramadan (Taught by revelation) · Making up a new compulsory fast (Invented by people) · Saying Bismillah before eating (Taught by revelation) · Praying Fajr with four rak'ahs instead of two (Invented by people) · Greeting with as-salamu alaykum (Taught by revelation)
12. Quiz: What is the Sunnah? **What the Prophet ﷺ said, did or approved of** · What are the two conditions for worship to be accepted? **Sincerity to Allah, and following the Sunnah** · What does Surah Al Imran 3:31 say we must do if we love Allah? **Follow the Messenger ﷺ**
13. Write: Choose one Sunnah from the table: greeting with salam, eating with the right hand, kindness to children, keeping promises, or cleanliness. When will you practise it this week, and how will it help you?
14. Write: How does copying the Prophet's good character help us in daily life? How can we remind ourselves to follow his guidance every day?

### 15. 3.4 Types of worship

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: identify worship of the heart, the tongue, the body and wealth; give examples of each type; understand that worship is both inward and outward; remember that all worship must be sincere and follow the Sunnah
2. *Four kinds of worship.* We can sort worship by where it takes place. Whatever the type, it must be sincere for Allah alone and done the way the Prophet ﷺ taught. [table]
3. *Worship of the heart.* Loving Allah more than anything else. We enjoy obeying Him, and we choose what He loves even when it is hard. Being afraid of disobeying Allah and of His punishment. It keeps us away from sin, even when no one is watching. Hoping for Allah's mercy and reward. It keeps us positive and makes us keep making du'a. Relying on Allah in every affair. We study for the test, then trust Allah with the result.
4. *Worship of the tongue.* Remembering Allah with words like SubhanAllah, Alhamdulillah and Allahu Akbar. Say SubhanAllah when you see something amazing. Speaking to Allah with humility. We ask Him for help, guidance, forgiveness and every good thing. Reading the Qur'an with understanding and reflection, even a few ayat a day. Telling the truth and using kind words. We avoid lying, backbiting and harsh words.
