5. *Worship with our bodies and our wealth.* Islam teaches us to worship Allah with everything He has given us. [table]
- **check:** Sunan Abi Dawud 1678 and Jami at-Tirmidhi 3675 (graded hasan). The textbook adds that "Allah blessed his wealth even more"; that is not in the narration and is left out.
6. *Abu Bakr's generosity.* Umar ibn al-Khattab said that the Prophet ﷺ once asked the Companions to give in charity. Umar had some wealth at the time, and he brought half of it. Then Abu Bakr came with everything he had. The Prophet ﷺ asked him what he had left for his family. Abu Bakr answered, "I have left them Allah and His Messenger." Umar said that he knew then he could never get ahead of Abu Bakr in doing good.
7. *Everyday actions can become worship.* When we do something lawful with a sincere intention for Allah, it can become an act of worship. [table] One evening a boy saw that his mother was tired after a long day. Without saying anything, he filled her water bottle and left her a kind note. When she thanked him, he said, "I did it for Allah, so you can rest." The action was small. The intention made it worship.
8. Hadith: إِنَّ اللَّهَ لَيَرْضَى عَنِ الْعَبْدِ أَنْ يَأْكُلَ الْأَكْلَةَ فَيَحْمَدَهُ عَلَيْهَا، أَوْ يَشْرَبَ الشَّرْبَةَ فَيَحْمَدَهُ عَلَيْهَا "Allah is pleased with a servant who eats a meal and praises Him for it, or drinks a drink and praises Him for it." (Sahih Muslim 2734)
9. Sort into Heart / Tongue / Body / Wealth: Loving Allah (Heart) · Saying SubhanAllah (Tongue) · Praying salah (Body) · Giving sadaqah (Wealth) · Trusting Allah (Heart) · Making du'a (Tongue) · Fasting in Ramadan (Body) · Paying for a water well (Wealth) · Hoping for Allah's mercy (Heart) · Telling the truth (Tongue)
10. Quiz: Which of these is worship of the heart? **Trusting Allah** · What did Abu Bakr say he had left for his family? **Allah and His Messenger** · How can eating a meal become worship? **By eating halal food and praising Allah for it**
11. Write: Write a sincere intention that turns each of these into worship: studying for a test, doing homework, taking a nap, playing a sport, eating a meal, helping at home.
12. Write: Which kind of heart or tongue worship is easiest for you? Which do you find hardest? How can you improve it this week?

### 18. 4.1 Allah's beautiful Names

1. *Unit Four: Tawheed al-Asma was-Sifat.* In this unit you will come to know Allah through His beautiful Names and perfect Attributes. You will learn why His Names matter and how to understand His Attributes correctly, without comparing Allah to anything He created. You will also see how knowing His Names improves our worship and our character. Lesson 1: Allah's beautiful Names; Lesson 2: Understanding Allah's Attributes; Lesson 3: Living by the Names of Allah; Lesson 4: Names that strengthen faith; Unit Four test
2. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define al-Asma al-Husna, the most beautiful Names; explain why Allah's Names are perfect; connect learning the Names with worship
3. Ayah 7:180: "And to Allah belong the most beautiful Names, so call on Him by them."
4. Hadith: إِنَّ لِلَّهِ تِسْعَةً وَتِسْعِينَ اسْمًا، مِائَةً إِلَّا وَاحِدًا، مَنْ أَحْصَاهَا دَخَلَ الْجَنَّةَ "Allah has ninety-nine Names, one hundred less one. Whoever preserves them will enter Paradise." (Sahih al-Bukhari 2736 and Sahih Muslim 2677)
5. *What is a Name of Allah?.* A Name of Allah (ism) is a word that describes Him with one of His perfect qualities. His Names are perfect, and they never change. Each one teaches us something about Him. They show us who He is and what He does. Ar-Rahman, the Most Merciful, shows His endless mercy.; They guide our actions. Al-'Adl, the Just, teaches us to be fair.; They help us worship Him with love, hope and trust. These Names belong to Allah alone. Nothing and no one can share them with Him in their perfect meaning.
6. *Four Names to begin with.* [table]
7. *Aisha and the All-Knowing.* Aisha loved learning about Allah. One day her teacher wrote Al-'Alim, the All-Knowing, on the board. Aisha put up her hand. "Does Allah know everything?" she asked. "Yes," said her teacher. "Allah knows what is in the skies and on the earth. He knows what we say and what we feel, even our hidden thoughts." Aisha thought for a moment. "So He knows when I am happy to help my friend, and when I am feeling sad?" "Yes. That is why we can always talk to Him. He understands us completely." Aisha smiled. "Alhamdulillah. Knowing that makes me feel safe. It also reminds me to do what is good."
8. *How the Names change our hearts.* [table]
9. Match: Ar-Rahman = The Most Merciful · Al-Ghafur = The Most Forgiving · Al-'Alim = The All-Knowing · As-Sami' = The All-Hearing · Al-Qadir = The All-Powerful · As-Salam = The Source of Peace
10. Quiz: What does al-Asma al-Husna mean? **The most beautiful Names** · What does "preserving" Allah's Names include? **Learning them, understanding them and calling on Allah by them** · Knowing that Allah is As-Sami' should make us: **Careful about what we say**
11. Write: Choose one beautiful Name of Allah. Write the Name, its meaning in English, what it teaches us about Allah, and one action you will do this week because of it.
12. Write: Which of these four Names do you find most comforting, and why? How does learning the Names of Allah help you grow closer to Him?

### 19. 4.2 Understanding Allah's Attributes

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: explain what an Attribute of Allah is; understand that Allah's Attributes are perfect; avoid comparing Allah to His creation
2. Ayah 42:11: "There is nothing like Him, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing."
3. *Allah is not like His creation.* An Attribute (sifah) is a quality that Allah has told us about Himself, such as His hearing, His sight, His knowledge, His mercy and His power. Allah's Attributes are perfect. They are not like the qualities of human beings. He hears, but not with ears like ours. He sees, but not with eyes like ours. He knows without ever having to learn. He is powerful without any weakness. [table]
4. Ayah 112:4: "And there is none equal to Him."
5. *Four mistakes to avoid.* Ahl as-Sunnah believe in every Name and Attribute that Allah and His Messenger ﷺ told us about. We affirm them as they came, and we stay away from four mistakes. [table] We believe in Allah's Attributes because He told us about them. We do not imagine how they are, because they are perfect and beyond our understanding. Picturing them like the qualities of creation would make Allah like His creation, and that is wrong.
6. *How do we learn about Allah's Attributes?.* [table]
7. Ayah 3:190: "Indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth and the alternation of the night and the day are signs for people of understanding."
8. *The child who asked a big question.* A young boy looked up at the night sky. "What does Allah look like?" he asked his teacher. "That is a big question," she said gently. "Allah is not like anything we can see. He made everything, and nothing is like Him." "Then how do we know about Him?" asked the boy. "Allah tells us about Himself in the Qur'an. The Prophet ﷺ teaches us in the Sunnah. And we reflect on what He created." The boy nodded. "So we believe what Allah and His Messenger told us, and we love Him with our hearts." "Exactly," said his teacher.
9. Sort into Allah (Attribute) / Humans (creation): Knows everything perfectly (Allah (Attribute)) · May forget or not know (Humans (creation)) · His mercy is endless and perfect (Allah (Attribute)) · Can be kind in their own small way (Humans (creation)) · His power has no limit (Allah (Attribute)) · Have strength, but it runs out (Humans (creation)) · Hears every du'a at the same moment (Allah (Attribute)) · Need sleep and rest (Humans (creation))
10. Quiz: What does "There is nothing like Him" (42:11) teach us? **Allah's Attributes are not like anything in creation** · Which mistake is "trying to describe exactly how an Attribute is"? **Takyif** · Which source does NOT tell us how Allah is? **Looking at creation**
11. Write: Complete these sentences. "Allah is unlike His creation because..." "Learning Allah's Attributes helps me to..."

### 20. 4.3 Living by the Names of Allah

1. *Lesson objectives.* Knowing Allah's Names is the first step. Then we live by them. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: connect Allah's Names with worship; build mercy, honesty, gratitude and patience; choose actions guided by faith
- **check:** As-Sabur is in the list of names in Jami at-Tirmidhi 3507, whose list is weak. That Allah is patient is affirmed in Sahih al-Bukhari 6099 ("No one is more patient with harmful words than Allah"). A teacher may prefer to teach Al-Halim (the Forbearing, Qur'an 2:235) here.
2. *Knowledge should change us.* When we learn the Names of Allah, we let them guide our hearts and our actions. [table]
3. *When I know Allah is Al-Ghafur and Ash-Shakur.* He forgives our mistakes when we are sorry. So I will say sorry when I get something wrong. I will forgive others, and ask Allah for forgiveness every day. He rewards even the smallest good deed. So I will thank Allah for His many gifts with my words and my actions, and try to do more good. A teacher once saw a boy upset because his friend had knocked over his tower of blocks. She reminded him that Allah is Ar-Rahman, the Most Merciful, who is kind to us all the time. The boy took a deep breath and forgave his friend. Then he helped him build the tower again.
4. *When I know Allah is patient.* Allah is never in a hurry. He gives each thing its right time, and He is with those who are patient. So when things are hard, I will be patient. I will work calmly and trust Allah's timing. Yusuf planted seeds in his field. Some days were hot and some days it rained. He wanted the plants to grow quickly, but they did not. He kept watering and caring for them. Weeks later his field was green. Pause and take a breath. Ask Allah for patience. Choose a good response. Then keep trying.
5. Ayah 2:153: "O you who believe, seek help through patience and prayer. Indeed, Allah is with the patient."
6. *Do not claim Allah's Names for yourself.* Allah's Names are perfect, and their full meaning belongs to Him alone. Some Names, like Allah and Ar-Rahman, may not be given to any person at all. That is why Muslims name their children Abdullah and Abd ar-Rahman: servant of Allah, servant of the Most Merciful. People can show a little mercy and learn a few things. They are never perfect the way Allah is. [table]
7. Quiz: Your little brother broke your toy by accident. **Al-Ghafur: I forgive him, because Allah forgives me** · Your friend is sad after a bad day. **Ar-Rahman: I show her mercy and comfort her** · You received a gift you really wanted. **Ash-Shakur: I thank Allah and the person who gave it** · You have to wait a long time in a queue. **As-Sabur: I stay calm and wait patiently** · You promised your parents you would tidy your room. **Al-'Alim: Allah knows whether I keep my promise**
8. Write: For each situation, write the Name of Allah that can help you and the action you will take. 1. A friend did not invite me to play. 2. I broke something by accident. 3. My test result was better than I expected. 4. Someone said something unkind to me and I feel angry.
9. Write: Choose one action to practise this week to please Allah, using one of the Names from this lesson.

### 21. 4.4 Names that strengthen faith

1. *Lesson objectives.* Allah's beautiful Names tell us who He is and how He cares for us. When we know and remember them, our faith grows and we feel close to Him. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: explain how Allah's Names strengthen faith; connect Allah's Names with hope and fear; use Allah's Names in hard times and in times of gratitude
2. *Four more Names.* [table]
3. Ayah 2:186: "And when My servants ask you about Me, I am near. I answer the call of the caller when he calls on Me."
4. *Trusting Allah on a difficult day.* Yusuf had a big test, but he had forgotten to study. He felt worried. He decided to do his best. He studied carefully and asked his teacher for help. Then he turned to Allah and said, "Ya Allah, help me do my best." He remembered that Allah is Al-Wakil, the Trustee. Whatever the result, Allah is in control. Yusuf felt calm, because he had done his part and trusted Allah with the rest.
5. Order: He saw the problem and felt worried → He took responsible steps and studied → He made du'a to Allah → He remembered Al-Wakil and trusted the result to Allah
6. Ayah 65:3: "And whoever relies on Allah, He is enough for him."
7. *Hope and fear.* We always hope in Allah's mercy. Allah wants good for us. Hope keeps us positive and patient. It stops us giving up, and it helps us believe Allah can make things better. We fear displeasing Allah and we fear His punishment. This fear keeps us away from sin and bad choices. It makes us respect Allah and His rules, and it protects our hearts. A believer keeps both together. Hope without fear makes a person careless about sin. Fear without hope makes a person give up. With both, we keep doing good and keep asking for forgiveness.
8. *Du'a, effort and trust.* Tawakkul means relying on Allah while taking the right steps. It has three parts: Make du'a: turn to Allah and ask Him with hope.; Take the right steps: work hard, be responsible and do what is right.; Trust the result: leave it to Allah, who knows what is best.
9. Match: Al-Hakim = The All-Wise · Al-Qadir = The All-Powerful · Al-Ghafur = The Forgiving · Al-Wakil = The Trustee, who takes care of us · Al-Hafiz = The Preserver · Al-Mujib = The One who answers du'a
10. Quiz: You have a big test next week and you feel worried. **Revise, make du'a and trust Allah with the result** · You did not win the game and you want to give up. **Be patient, keep practising and hope in Allah** · A friend is upset and you want to help. **Listen kindly, help where you can and make du'a for them**
11. Write: Write a short du'a you will make to Allah about something you care about. Then write what you can control (your effort) and what you leave to Allah. Finish with one step you will take.

### 23. 5.1 Tawheed gives us courage

1. *Unit Five: the effects of Tawheed.* In this unit you will learn how Tawheed, believing in One Allah, changes our hearts and our actions. You will see how it builds courage, gratitude and good character. It also teaches us to look after everything Allah has trusted us with. Lesson 1: Tawheed gives us courage; Lesson 2: Tawheed gives us gratitude; Lesson 3: Tawheed builds good character; Lesson 4: Tawheed and responsibility; Unit Five test
2. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: explain how belief in Allah gives courage; tell the difference between courage and recklessness; make brave choices while trusting Allah
3. Ayah 3:175: "So do not fear them, but fear Me, if you are believers."
4. *What is true courage?.* True courage (shaja'ah) comes from believing in Allah. When we know that Allah is with us, our hearts become strong. We are not frightened of people, of hard tasks or of difficult times. Courage does not mean doing dangerous things or showing off. That is recklessness. Real courage is doing what is right, even when it is hard and even when others disagree. When we rely on Allah, we find strength we did not know we had. With Tawheed and tawakkul, we can face anything with courage and calm.
5. Hadith: مَا ظَنُّكَ يَا أَبَا بَكْرٍ بِاثْنَيْنِ اللَّهُ ثَالِثُهُمَا "O Abu Bakr, what do you think of two when Allah is the third of them?" (Sahih al-Bukhari 3653 and Sahih Muslim 2381)
6. Ayah 9:40: "Do not grieve; indeed Allah is with us."
7. Hadith: الْمُؤْمِنُ الْقَوِيُّ خَيْرٌ وَأَحَبُّ إِلَى اللَّهِ مِنَ الْمُؤْمِنِ الضَّعِيفِ، وَفِي كُلٍّ خَيْرٌ، احْرِصْ عَلَى مَا يَنْفَعُكَ، وَاسْتَعِنْ بِاللَّهِ وَلَا تَعْجِزْ "The strong believer is better and more beloved to Allah than the weak believer, and there is good in both. Be eager for what benefits you, seek Allah's help, and do not give up." (Sahih Muslim 2664)
8. Sort into Courage / Recklessness: Telling the truth when you could get in trouble (Courage) · Jumping off a high wall to impress friends (Recklessness) · Standing up for a classmate who is being bullied (Courage) · Riding your bike on a busy road without a helmet (Recklessness) · Praying at school even if others stare (Courage) · Saying no when friends dare you to cheat (Courage) · Accepting a dangerous dare online (Recklessness)
9. Quiz: Where does true courage come from? **Believing in Allah and trusting Him** · What did the Prophet ﷺ say to Abu Bakr in the cave? **What do you think of two when Allah is the third of them?** · Which is recklessness rather than courage? **Doing something dangerous to show off**
10. Write: Think of a time when doing the right thing needed courage. What happened? How could trusting Allah help you be brave next time?

### 24. 5.2 Tawheed gives us gratitude

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: explain shukr (gratitude) and qana'ah (contentment); show gratitude even in hard times; choose contentment instead of complaining
2. *Words to know.* [table]
3. Ayah 14:7: "And when your Lord proclaimed: If you are grateful, I will surely give you more."
4. *Gratitude during difficulty.* Shukr does not mean pretending that a hard time is easy. When things are hard, we still trust Allah and stay thankful. We can show gratitude by: noticing the blessings we still have; making du'a and asking Allah for ease; asking for help from Allah and from others; responding with sabr (patience) and a good heart Yusuf had a terrible week. He failed a test, his best friend moved away, and he felt tired and sad. That night he made a list of what was still good. He had loving parents. He was healthy. He could learn and try again. Allah was always with him. Yusuf made du'a, asked for help with his studies and stayed patient. By the end of the week he felt stronger and more thankful.
5. *Gratitude and contentment.* Gratitude (shukr) is thanking Allah for what He has given us. Contentment (qana'ah) is being happy with what Allah has chosen for us. Not every wish will come true. When we are grateful and content, our hearts are at peace and we stay close to Allah.
6. Hadith: انْظُرُوا إِلَى مَنْ أَسْفَلَ مِنْكُمْ، وَلَا تَنْظُرُوا إِلَى مَنْ هُوَ فَوْقَكُمْ، فَهُوَ أَجْدَرُ أَنْ لَا تَزْدَرُوا نِعْمَةَ اللَّهِ "Look at those who have less than you, and do not look at those who have more than you. That is better, so that you do not look down on Allah's blessings to you." (Sahih Muslim 2963)
7. Hadith: لَيْسَ الْغِنَى عَنْ كَثْرَةِ الْعَرَضِ، وَلَكِنَّ الْغِنَى غِنَى النَّفْسِ "Being rich is not having many possessions. Being rich is being content in the soul." (Sahih al-Bukhari 6446 and Sahih Muslim 1051)
8. Sort into Contentment (qana'ah) / Complaining: "I thank Allah for the toys I already have." (Contentment (qana'ah)) · "It's not fair! I want that one." (Complaining) · "Alhamdulillah for the rain." (Contentment (qana'ah)) · "This weather is the worst!" (Complaining) · "I'll try harder next time." (Contentment (qana'ah)) · "It's useless. I can't do anything." (Complaining) · "I'm happy for my friend." (Contentment (qana'ah)) · "Why are they always better than me?" (Complaining) · "Helping at home is worship." (Contentment (qana'ah)) · "I always have to do everything!" (Complaining)
9. Hadith: لَا يَشْكُرُ اللَّهَ مَنْ لَا يَشْكُرُ النَّاسَ "Whoever does not thank people has not thanked Allah." (Sunan Abi Dawud 4811 and Jami at-Tirmidhi 1954)
10. Quiz: What does Allah promise to those who are grateful? **He will give them more** · Who is really rich, according to the hadith? **The person who is content in the soul** · You got a lower grade than you hoped. Which response shows qana'ah? **"Alhamdulillah, I will learn from it and try again."** · What is the difference between shukr and hamd? **Shukr is thanks for blessings; hamd is praising Allah for who He is**
11. Write: For seven days, write one blessing from Allah, one sentence of praise to Allah, one person you thanked, and one action of gratitude. At the end of the week, write how gratitude changed your words, your choices and your mood.

### 25. 5.3 Tawheed builds good character

1. *Lesson objectives.* By the end of this lesson you will be able to: explain how Tawheed shapes character; connect worship with honesty, kindness, humility and trustworthiness; see good character as part of living Islam
2. Ayah 2:195: "And do good; indeed, Allah loves those who do good."
3. *What does Tawheed teach us?.* Tawheed means we believe that Allah alone is our Lord. When our hearts belong to Allah, our actions show it. Tawheed shapes our akhlaq, our character. Honesty: we tell the truth, even when no one is watching.; Kindness: we treat others with care.; Humility: we stay modest and do not look down on anyone.; Trustworthiness (amanah): we keep our promises and can be relied on. Good character is a sign of a strong connection with Allah.
4. Hadith: أَكْمَلُ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ إِيمَانًا أَحْسَنُهُمْ خُلُقًا "The most complete of the believers in faith are those with the best character." (Sunan Abi Dawud 4682 and Jami at-Tirmidhi 1162)
5. *Belief and character.* [table]
6. *The trustworthy classmate.* At break time Ahmed found a wallet in the playground. Inside were some money and an ID card. He remembered that Allah sees everything, so he took the wallet straight to his teacher. The teacher found the owner, who was very grateful. Ahmed felt happy. He knew he had done the right thing, and that he had done it for Allah.
7. *Small actions, great character.* Greet others with as-salamu alaykum and a smile.; Give back what you borrow.; Use gentle words, not harsh ones.; Do what you said you would do.; Help your family with love.; Say sorry when you are wrong, and try to do better.
8. Quiz: You meet a classmate in the morning. **Say as-salamu alaykum with a smile** · A friend lends you their pencil. **Return it on time** · Someone says something hurtful to you. **Speak gently** · You promised to finish your homework. **Do it and hand it in** · Your parents are busy and need help. **Offer to help** · You break a glass by accident. **Admit it and clean it up**
9. Write: How does believing in Tawheed help you make good choices? Which part of good character do you most want to grow in, and why?

### 26. 5.4 Tawheed and responsibility

1. *Lesson objectives.* Because Allah is One and we worship Him alone, He has given us many trusts. He also holds us responsible for how we use them. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: understand amanah as a trust from Allah; explain that every choice will be asked about; practise responsible choices at home and at school
2. *Words to know.* [table] A Muslim asks, "What can I do?" A Muslim also asks, "What has Allah trusted me with?"
3. Ayah 4:58: "Indeed, Allah commands you to give back trusts to those they belong to."
4. Hadith: كُلُّكُمْ رَاعٍ، وَكُلُّكُمْ مَسْئُولٌ عَنْ رَعِيَّتِهِ "Each of you is a shepherd, and each of you is responsible for his flock." (Sahih al-Bukhari 893 and Sahih Muslim 1829)
5. *Our first responsibility: worshipping Allah.* Tawheed means worshipping Allah alone. It shapes every act of worship and makes us trustworthy servants. [table]
6. Ayah 40:60: "And your Lord says: Call on Me; I will answer you."
7. *Time is an amanah.* Time is one of the greatest trusts from Allah. Every hour is a chance to please Him. When we use our time well, we worship Allah, grow in knowledge, care for our families, rest properly and serve others. Rest is part of the plan too. A tired body cannot pray well, study well or help anyone.
8. Hadith: لَا تَزُولُ قَدَمَا عَبْدٍ يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ حَتَّى يُسْأَلَ عَنْ عُمُرِهِ فِيمَا أَفْنَاهُ، وَعَنْ عِلْمِهِ فِيمَ فَعَلَ، وَعَنْ مَالِهِ مِنْ أَيْنَ اكْتَسَبَهُ وَفِيمَ أَنْفَقَهُ، وَعَنْ جِسْمِهِ فِيمَ أَبْلَاهُ "The feet of a servant will not move on the Day of Resurrection until he is asked about his life and how he spent it, his knowledge and what he did with it, his wealth and where he earned it and how he spent it, and his body and how he used it." (Jami at-Tirmidhi 2417)
9. *Responsible speech and choices.* A believer knows that Allah sees every word and every choice. That includes how we speak face to face and how we behave online. Words: choosing what is true, kind and helpful; Promises: keeping what we commit to; Online: using technology with good manners and respect; Everyday choices: doing what pleases Allah in small ways and big ones [table]
10. Ayah 50:18: "He does not utter a word except that there is a watcher with him, ready to record."
11. Hadith: مَنْ كَانَ يُؤْمِنُ بِاللَّهِ وَالْيَوْمِ الْآخِرِ فَلْيَقُلْ خَيْرًا أَوْ لِيَصْمُتْ "Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day, let him say something good or stay silent." (Sahih al-Bukhari 6018 and Sahih Muslim 47)
12. *Accountability is not fear alone.* Believing that we will be asked about our deeds is a gift. It reminds us that every choice counts, because Allah sees and knows all we do. It helps us: think before we act; repent quickly when we make a mistake; put things right with Allah and with people; keep hoping in Allah's mercy, because He loves those who turn back to Him
13. Ayah 99:7: "So whoever does an atom's weight of good will see it."
14. Sort into Excuse / Responsibility: "It wasn't my fault." (Excuse) · "I made a mistake and I will fix it." (Responsibility) · "Everyone else was doing it." (Excuse) · "I choose to do what is right." (Responsibility) · "You made me do it." (Excuse) · "I'm sorry. I will be more careful." (Responsibility) · "I can't change." (Excuse) · "I will try, and ask Allah for help." (Responsibility)
15. Order: Notice: what is happening? → Remember: Allah sees and knows → Choose: which action is honest and kind? → Act: do it carefully, then look back
16. Order: Receive the trust with gratitude → Understand what it is for → Care for it and keep it safe → Use it for good and allowed things → Return it in good condition, or keep caring for it
17. Quiz: You borrowed a book from a friend and it is due back tomorrow. **Return it on time and in good condition** · You find some money in the classroom. **Give it to the teacher** · A friend asks you to help hide a mistake. **Tell the truth kindly and help fix it** · Your little sister asks for help with her homework. **Help her kindly** · You see litter in the park on your way home. **Pick it up and put it in the bin**
18. Write: Think of a time you made a mistake. Own it: what happened? Fix it: what can you do to put it right? Learn from it: what will you remember for next time?
19. Write: What is one amanah Allah has given you? How will you use it to please Him this week? Write one du'a asking Allah to make you a person of trust.

### 27. Unit Five test (test)

1. *Unit Five review.* Tawheed gives us courage.; Tawheed gives us gratitude.; Tawheed builds good character.; Tawheed teaches responsibility. Look back over the words, the main ideas and your own actions. Then do your best and trust Allah.
2. Match: Tawheed = Belief that Allah is One, and worshipping Him alone · Shukr = Thanking Allah for His blessings · Akhlaq = Good character in words and actions · Amanah = A trust we must look after · Qana'ah = Being content with what Allah gives
3. Quiz: Belief in Allah gives us courage to: **Do what is right** · When we feel thankful for Allah's blessings, we should: **Say Alhamdulillah and use them well** · Good character means we should: **Be kind and honest** · Responsibility means we should: **Do our part and keep promises** · Which is real courage? **Telling the truth when it may get you in trouble**
4. Quiz: Belief in Allah helps us face difficult situations. **True** · We should only thank Allah when we get something we want. **False** · Good character is part of living Islam. **True** · Amanah means we are responsible for what we are given. **True** · If no one is watching, our choices do not count. **False**
5. Write: Write one example of each: courage that comes from belief in Allah; gratitude for a blessing; good character; looking after an amanah.
6. Write: One change I will make this week is...

### 28. 6.1 Niyyah: the heart behind every action

1. *Unit Six: Tawheed in daily life.* Tawheed is something we learn, and it is also something we live. It touches every moment, every decision and every relationship, as we remember Allah and try to please Him. [table] Lesson 1: Niyyah, the heart behind every action; Lesson 2: Ihsan, doing our best for Allah; Lesson 3: Tawakkul, trusting Allah while we act; Lesson 4: Shukr and sabr; Unit Six test
2. *Lesson objectives.* Intention gives an action its direction. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define niyyah (intention); understand that Allah knows the intention behind every action; practise making sincere intentions
3. Hadith: إِنَّمَا الْأَعْمَالُ بِالنِّيَّاتِ، وَإِنَّمَا لِكُلِّ امْرِئٍ مَا نَوَى "Actions are only judged by intentions, and every person will have only what he intended." (Sahih al-Bukhari 1 and Sahih Muslim 1907)
4. *When the heart chooses.* Niyyah is the intention in the heart before an action. It is the silent choice we make before we start. A sincere intention is made to please Allah. The same action can be worth a great deal or almost nothing, depending on the intention. When the niyyah is for Allah, even simple actions become worship. [table] Who am I trying to please?
5. Hadith: إِنَّ اللَّهَ لَا يَنْظُرُ إِلَى صُوَرِكُمْ وَأَمْوَالِكُمْ، وَلَكِنْ يَنْظُرُ إِلَى قُلُوبِكُمْ وَأَعْمَالِكُمْ "Allah does not look at your appearance or your wealth. He looks at your hearts and your deeds." (Sahih Muslim 2564)
6. Match: Studying for a test = To gain knowledge and benefit others · Helping at home = To support my family and bring blessings · Exercising = To keep my body healthy to serve Allah · Sharing a snack = To be kind and strengthen brotherhood · Tidying my desk = To make a clean space to focus
7. Quiz: What is niyyah? **The intention in the heart before an action** · Two boys tidy their rooms. One does it to please Allah, the other so he is allowed to play. What is different? **Their intentions, and so their reward** · What does Allah look at, according to Sahih Muslim 2564? **Our hearts and our deeds**
8. Write: Write how you can do each of these to please Allah: doing homework, eating a meal, going to bed.
9. Write: Yusuf sees his classmate Amin stuck on a maths problem, and explains it to him during break. What might Yusuf's intention be? How could he make his intention better? Then set one sincere intention for your own day today.

### 29. 6.2 Ihsan: doing our best for Allah

1. *Lesson objectives.* Excellence means doing what is right with care, even when no one is watching. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define ihsan; recognise excellence in worship and daily work; choose one way to improve your effort
2. Hadith: أَنْ تَعْبُدَ اللَّهَ كَأَنَّكَ تَرَاهُ، فَإِنْ لَمْ تَكُنْ تَرَاهُ فَإِنَّهُ يَرَاكَ "That you worship Allah as though you see Him, and if you do not see Him, He surely sees you." (Sahih Muslim 8 and Sahih al-Bukhari 50)
3. Hadith: إِنَّ اللَّهَ كَتَبَ الْإِحْسَانَ عَلَى كُلِّ شَيْءٍ "Allah has prescribed excellence in everything." (Sahih Muslim 1955)
4. *Excellence in ordinary moments.* Ihsan means doing the best we can for Allah in every moment. It is for everyday life as well as for big occasions. [table] Allah sees the care we put into what we do. That care is ihsan.
5. Quiz: You are given a small task and no one will check it. **I do it carefully, because Allah sees my effort** · You find some money on the playground. **I hand it in, because it is not mine** · A friend makes a mistake and blames you. **I stay calm and speak kindly and honestly**
6. *Self-check: am I practising ihsan?.* Read each sentence and ask yourself honestly whether it is true for you most of the time. I try to do my work carefully and with full effort.; I am honest in my words and actions.; I am kind and polite to others.; I try to worship Allah with focus and sincerity.; I take care of my belongings and the things around me.
7. Write: Write one thing you will do more carefully today to practise ihsan.
8. Write: How can ihsan change the way you work when nobody is watching?

### 30. 6.3 Tawakkul: trusting Allah while we act

1. *Lesson objectives.* Tawakkul means relying on Allah while taking responsible steps. We prepare, we ask for help, and then we place our trust in Allah. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define tawakkul; tell the difference between trusting Allah and simply doing nothing; practise trusting Allah after a sincere effort
2. Ayah 3:159: "And when you have decided, then rely on Allah. Indeed, Allah loves those who rely on Him."
3. *Effort first, then trust.* Tawakkul does not mean ignoring our duties. It means taking the right steps, making du'a, and trusting Allah with the outcome. Remember the man with the camel in Unit Two: "Tie it, and trust in Allah." [table]
4. *A real-life scenario.* A student prepares for a class presentation. She practises many times. On the day, she still feels nervous. She takes a deep breath, makes du'a, and trusts Allah as she begins. She did everything that was in her control. The rest, she left to Allah.
5. Sort into Steps I can take / The outcome I leave to Allah: Study for my test (Steps I can take) · Get a good grade (The outcome I leave to Allah) · Pray sincerely (Steps I can take) · Win the competition (The outcome I leave to Allah) · Plan my project (Steps I can take) · People like my idea (The outcome I leave to Allah)
6. Quiz: Which is real tawakkul? **Practising for the match, making du'a and accepting the result** · Omar has studied well and asked Allah for help. Now he is waiting for his result. What can tawakkul give him? **Calm, because he did his part and Allah is in charge**
7. Write: Before I begin something hard, I can... When I feel worried, I can... After I have tried my best, I can...
8. Write: Write three actions you will take this week where you will do your part with care, then trust Allah with the result.

### 31. 6.4 Shukr and sabr: gratitude and patience

1. *Lesson objectives.* A believer thanks Allah for blessings and holds firm through difficulty. By the end of this lesson you will be able to: define shukr and sabr; see how they work together; practise a grateful response and a patient response
2. Hadith: عَجَبًا لِأَمْرِ الْمُؤْمِنِ، إِنَّ أَمْرَهُ كُلَّهُ خَيْرٌ، وَلَيْسَ ذَاكَ لِأَحَدٍ إِلَّا لِلْمُؤْمِنِ، إِنْ أَصَابَتْهُ سَرَّاءُ شَكَرَ فَكَانَ خَيْرًا لَهُ، وَإِنْ أَصَابَتْهُ ضَرَّاءُ صَبَرَ فَكَانَ خَيْرًا لَهُ "How wonderful is the affair of the believer! All of his affairs are good, and that is for no one except the believer. If something good happens to him, he is grateful, and that is good for him. If something bad happens to him, he is patient, and that is good for him." (Sahih Muslim 2999)
3. *Shukr: seeing blessings and giving thanks.* Shukr is more than saying "thank you". It is a way of life. It starts when we notice the blessings Allah has given us. It continues when we thank Him with our hearts and our words. It shows in how we use those blessings. [table]
4. Order: Notice the blessing around you → Thank Allah with your heart and your words → Use the blessing in ways that please Allah
5. Ayah 2:152: "So remember Me; I will remember you. And be grateful to Me, and do not be ungrateful."
6. *Sabr: holding firm with Allah.* Sabr is patience that holds firm. It means continuing to do what is right, staying away from what is wrong, and keeping control of ourselves when life is hard. Sabr keeps our hearts calm and our actions good. [table] Stop. Breathe. Remember Allah. Choose the best next action.
7. Ayah 39:10: "Indeed, the patient will be given their reward without measure."
8. Ayah 94:6: "Indeed, with hardship comes ease."
9. Sort into A grateful response (shukr) / A patient response (sabr): A friend helps you with a difficult subject. (A grateful response (shukr)) · A plan you were excited about does not work out. (A patient response (sabr)) · You receive a gift from someone you love. (A grateful response (shukr)) · You have to wait your turn for something you want. (A patient response (sabr)) · Someone forgives you when you make a mistake. (A grateful response (shukr)) · You find an exam question very hard. (A patient response (sabr))
10. Quiz: According to Sahih Muslim 2999, why is every affair of the believer good? **He is grateful in good times and patient in bad times** · A teammate did not do their part of a group project and the result was poor. What is a patient response? **Stay calm, speak to them kindly and work out the next step** · Which shows shukr for the blessing of time? **Planning my day so I do not waste it**
11. Write: Write three blessings you noticed today and how you thanked Allah for each one.
12. Write: A teacher spent extra time helping a student, and the student improved. Later the student did badly in a test even after studying hard. How can the student show shukr for the help, and sabr after the setback? Then choose three actions you will do this week to practise shukr and sabr.

### 32. Unit Six test (test)

1. *Unit Six review.* [table] Before the test, make sure you can explain all five words and give an example of each from your own life. Ask Allah for help and do your best.
2. Match: Niyyah = Making a sincere intention for the sake of Allah · Ihsan = Doing something with excellence, knowing Allah sees · Tawakkul = Trusting Allah while doing our best · Shukr = Being grateful for the blessings of Allah · Sabr = Being patient in difficulty and hardship
3. Quiz: Which word means making our intention only for Allah? **Niyyah** · Doing your best in work or study even when nobody is watching is an example of: **Ihsan** · Before we trust Allah for the result, we should: **Do our best and be responsible** · Saying Alhamdulillah after a blessing is an example of: **Shukr** · When something is difficult, a patient Muslim will: **Keep trying and trust Allah**
4. Quiz: Niyyah is the heart behind every action. **True** · Ihsan means doing our best only when people are watching. **False** · Tawakkul means trusting Allah while we act and do our best. **True** · Shukr means being grateful for Allah's blessings. **True** · Sabr means being patient in difficulty and not giving up. **True** · Tawakkul means we do not need to study, because Allah decides. **False**
5. Write: 1. How does intention (niyyah) change an action? 2. How can we show ihsan in daily life? 3. How does effort join with trust in Allah? 4. How do shukr and sabr guide us in everyday life?

### 33. Closing message

1. *Dear student.* You have reached the end of Volume One. You started by asking what Tawheed is. Now you know that it means singling out Allah alone: as your Lord, in your worship, and in His beautiful Names and Attributes. You have seen that Tawheed is something to live as well as to learn. It is in the intention you make before you study. It is in the money you hand back, the promise you keep and the du'a you make before a hard test. It is in the "Alhamdulillah" you say over a meal and the calm you keep when a plan goes wrong. Bilal held on to one word, "Ahad", in the burning sun. Salman left his home to find the truth. You will not be tested in the same way. But every day you will have small chances to choose Allah over everything else. Keep coming back to these lessons. Keep asking Allah to keep your heart firm.
2. Du'a (A du'a the Prophet ﷺ often made): يَا مُقَلِّبَ الْقُلُوبِ ثَبِّتْ قَلْبِي عَلَى دِينِكَ / Ya muqallibal-qulub, thabbit qalbi 'ala dinik / "O Turner of hearts, keep my heart firm on Your religion." (Jami at-Tirmidhi 2140)
3. Write: Which lesson in this book meant the most to you, and why? Write one thing you will do differently because of what you have learned about Tawheed.
